## 2026-09-09 — Report Viz library (`-> Report frames (WIP)`)

Ten real visualization components added, extracted from an actual built artifact
rather than designed speculatively: the Brewlune GEO report (`~/geo-full-report`),
a 10-page A4 PDF built entirely on Foundations tokens. Every one earned its place by
being needed twice or more in that document.

**Built:** `Viz / Bar` (4 tone variants), `Bar Row`, `Stacked Bar`, `Progress Ring`,
`Stat Tile`, `Cell Grid Row`, `Tile Grid`, `Rank Chip` (2 variants), `Own Tag`,
`Brand Cell`. Laid out as two frames — a labelled library of instances, and the
component sources beneath it.

**These fill the `Chart Slot` / `Donut Chart Slot` placeholders** left on this page in
the earlier pass. `Progress Ring` is the real version of `Donut Chart Slot`.

**Token discipline — verified, not assumed.** A traversal audit of every node in all
ten components reports **0 unbound values**: 68 fills, 24 strokes, 212 radii and 13
spacing values all bound to `semantic` variables. That audit also caught the two
`COMPONENT_SET` wrapper frames carrying Figma's default raw 5px radius / 16px padding
— bound to `border/radius/sm` and `spacing/padding/400` so nothing in the group holds
a stray value. Re-run the traversal after any edit here; it is cheap and it is the
only way to know.

**Notes worth keeping:**
- `Stacked Bar` segments use `layoutGrow` rather than fixed widths, so an instance
  stays proportional when resized. Set the three grow values to the percentages.
- `Progress Ring` is built from two `ELLIPSE` nodes with `arcData` (`innerRadius`
  0.80), the arc starting at `-PI/2` so twelve o'clock is the top. It takes
  `chart/single/line`, not `chart/single/bar` — a ring is a stroked arc, and those
  tokens are named for the mark they draw.
- `Rank Chip` deliberately marks only "first". An earlier code version stepped ink
  through four greys and flipped the numeral white→ink halfway down, which split four
  chips into two visually different kinds of object. Contrast was never the problem
  (worst step measured 6:1); coherence was.
- `Cell Grid Row` keeps one meaning for the filled colour. Recolouring cells to carry
  an overall verdict inverts the cell's own meaning — carry the verdict in a badge.

**Companion skill:** `~/design-system/.claude/skills/gist-report/` (symlinked into
`~/.claude/skills/` so it loads in any project) captures the token/type/spacing/viz
rules these were built under, plus two audit scripts. Written the same day, from the
same work.

## Token architecture (3 variable collections)

- **`global`** — raw palette (`color/*`, `font/*`). Almost never bind to these directly.
- **`semantic`** — roles with meaning. **Bind here for ~95% of the UI.** Families: `action/*` (background/action/{primary,secondary}-{default,hover,pressed,content}), `background/interactive/*` (hover/pressed/selected tints), `content/*` (default, secondary, disabled, inverse, loading, link, status-*), `border/*` (default, interactive, interactive-strong, interactive-disabled, interactive-pressed, focus, strong, radius/*, status-*), `spacing/*` (padding/gap × xs–xxl).
- **`component`** — legacy `button/*` aliases (e.g. `button/content/primary`, `button/background/secondary`). **Being retired.** Don't create new component-collection tokens; if you find a node still bound to one, rebind it directly to the semantic token it resolves to (same visual result, but complies with the guide's "no component color tokens" rule) — see the audit script in the Definition of Done below.

Five rules from the guide, worth re-reading before any token decision: (1) the name tells you the use — don't pick by inspecting a hex; (2) surfaces vs. text vs. borders map to `surface/*` / `content/*` / `border/*`; (3) a control that owns a solid fill uses `action/*`, a surface that only tints on interaction uses `interactive/*`; (4) a committed selection (checkbox/chip) is `action`, a "you are here" nav state is `interactive/selected`; (5) a token means the same thing in light and dark — it's one role, two values.

## Spacing scale — restructured from t-shirt sizes to numeric steps (2026-08-21)

Task: add layout-level spacing guidelines (page margins, section gaps, gutters) to bring consistency across products that had been shipping against this half-baked system with real drift already baked in. Started by reading an actual product screen (sidebar + content dashboard, `Spacings/layouts` page) and a second, structurally different one (a Settings panel, found hidden on the `-> Breakpoints logic` page) rather than guessing.

**Real evidence from both screens:** existing `spacing/padding|gap/{xs,sm,md,lg,xl,xxl}` = 4/8/12/16/32/40. But both real screens repeatedly use **20px** (page margin, sidebar gutter, settings panel padding) and **24px** (three separate uses inside one settings panel) — neither exists in the scale, both fall in the dead zone between `lg` (16) and `xl` (32). Checked actual usage file-wide before touching anything: `xl` (32) had **zero real bindings anywhere**; `xxl` (40) had 65; `md` (12) had 426 (all at the component-padding tier, e.g. Button/Input — a different concern from layout spacing, not "underused" as first assumed).

**Restructured to numeric steps** (researched first — confirmed via [Atlassian's spacing tokens](https://atlassian.design/foundations/spacing) and Adobe Spectrum that numeric scales, not t-shirt sizes, are standard practice specifically because they let you insert a new step later without renaming anything — the exact problem this file just hit). Matches the numbering convention Gist's own color and type tokens already use (100→900), so it's not a foreign pattern. Renamed in place (same variable IDs — zero bindings broken, verified against a real Button instance post-rename): `xs→100`, `sm→200`, `md→300`, `lg→400`, `xl→700` (renamed off, cost zero since nothing used it, value 32 **kept, not deleted**), `xxl→800` (value 40 unchanged). Added two new steps, correctly aliased through the existing `global/size/*` primitive layer (not raw literals — `size/8`=20, `size/9`=24 already existed in `global`, just unused): `500`=20, `600`=24. Final scale: 100=4, 200=8, 300=12, 400=16, 500=20, 600=24, 700=32, 800=40 — for both `spacing/padding/*` and `spacing/gap/*`.

**Next:** build the lean semantic-role mapping (which token for which layout purpose) on the `Spacings/layouts` page, same format as the Typography semantic mapping.

## Component status

### Alert — ✅ core fixes done (2026-08-21), one flagged token issue open

Found via a new `-> Alerts` page, component set `Alert` (`4874:1972`). Started in the same broken state Input did: variant property literally named "Property 1" with placeholder values `Outlined/Variant2/Variant3/Variant4`. Fixed:
- Renamed to `Status = Info/Warning/Error/Success`.
- **Info variant was bound to `content/link` (blue) instead of the real `content/status-info` token** — same "coincidentally similar-looking wrong token" bug pattern seen elsewhere this session. Fixed across all 4 places it was used identically wrong (border stroke, icon vector, Title fill, Body fill).
- **Added the missing background tint.** A full `background/status/{info,warning,error,success}` token family existed and was completely unused — every variant had zero fill, just a colored border/icon/text floating on the page background, unlike every mainstream alert pattern (Material/Ant/Bootstrap all use a tinted background). Bound all 4 variants' `UnstyledAlert` fill to the matching token.
- **Exposed two real, pre-existing hidden capabilities** rather than building from scratch: a `Title` text node (16px Medium, sits above the body message) and an `Actions` frame (containing an `UnstyledButton` secondary text action + an `UnstyledIconButton` close "✕") both existed fully built but permanently hidden with no way to toggle them — exact same shape as the Input icon-slot regression. Added `Show Title` and `Dismissible` booleans (both default `false`), bound to the Title text and the Actions frame respectively. Deliberately left `UnstyledButton` (the secondary text action) hidden and unexposed — only the close button was asked for; the slot still physically exists if that capability gets scoped in later.
- Confirmed via `createInstance()` that this file still exhibits the "fresh instance doesn't mirror the master's full current structure" gotcha from the Input work — a newly created Alert instance was missing both Title and Actions entirely, even though they exist plainly on the master. Diagnosed by temporarily toggling `visible` directly on the **master** component and screenshotting that, not a fresh instance.

**Background-tint idea tried, then reverted — decided to stay borderless (2026-08-21).** Initially added a background fill to all 4 variants (using the previously-unused `background/status/*` token family) after noticing `background/status/info` was miscalibrated (pointed at `color/blue/100`, a saturated blue, while warning/error/success point at genuinely pale steps in their own families — confirmed by tracing the alias chains). While computing the fix, ran a full WCAG contrast check of `content/status-*` text against its own `background/status/*` tint and found **3 of 4 statuses failed AA (4.5:1) in Light mode**: Warning 3.65:1, Error 4.38:1, Success 4.17:1 (Dark mode was fine throughout, 5.9–9.07:1, since it composites a translucent tint over a dark surface rather than using an opaque pale literal). All three content tokens were already pinned to their family's *darkest available step* (`600`, with no `700`+ existing), so a real fix would have meant creating brand-new primitive color steps — a foundational palette change, correctly escalated rather than done silently.

**Token-guide QA pass (2026-08-21).** Cross-checked every Alert binding against the user's own Token Usage Guide PDF. Found a real, guide-confirmed mistake across **all 4 variants**: the border stroke was bound to `content/status-{X}` — the same token driving the icon and text — when the guide defines a dedicated `border/status-{X}` family specifically for "outlines for status containers," which existed and was completely unused. Fixed all 4 (seeding the correct resolved color before rebinding, per the established pattern). Also found the corner radius was a hardcoded `10`, not bound to any variable at all, despite `border/radius/*` (xs=2, sm=4, md=8, lg=20, xl=32) being the documented token family for this — bound all 4 to `border/radius/md` (8px, the closest existing step; 10 didn't cleanly match anything). Icon and text bindings were already correct (`content/status-{X}`, matching the guide's "text & icons from `content/*`" rule) — not touched. Re-verified: 0 stale colors, 0 non-local variables, all 4 corners consistently bound.

**User's call: drop the background-tint idea entirely, go back to the original bordered-only look.** Reverted `background/status/info` back to `color/blue/100` and cleared the fills added to all 4 variants' `UnstyledAlert` frames. The earlier, unrelated legitimate fix (Info's border/icon/text were wrongly bound to `content/link` instead of `content/status-info`) was **not** reverted — that was a real wrong-token bug independent of the background question. Re-checked contrast against a plain page background instead: Error (5.97:1) and Success (4.55:1) pass cleanly without a tint, but **Warning still fails even on plain white (4.01:1)** — its content color is just too light for AA body text regardless of background. This is now a smaller, isolated open item (only Warning needs a darker primitive, not all three) — not yet resolved, pending the user's call on whether to create `color/warning/700` or accept the shortfall as a documented tradeoff (same precedent as the `content/placeholder` decision earlier).

**Open questions, not blockers:** Warning and Error currently share the exact same icon (`light/alert-triangle`), differing only by color — a WCAG 1.4.1 "don't rely on color alone" consideration, but no clear alternative icon was located and the broader icon library page remains unresolved (same blocker as the Input icon-slot work). User confirmed the close/dismiss affordance should exist (now built); the secondary text-action slot's fate is still open.

### Tag — ✅ done (2026-08-21)

Component set `Tag` (`4874:2165`), 12 variants (Size = Small/Medium/Large × Type = Default/Success/Warning/Error), found via a new `-> Tags` page. Fixed:
- **Normalized variant naming** — `Size= Small`/`Size= Medium` had a stray space after `=` that `Size=Large` didn't; stripped for consistency.
- **"Default" type was two different unbound, inconsistent looks depending on size** — Small/Medium used a raw, unbound blue tint (~`blue/50`, no idea why blue — likely a copy/paste artifact), Large used a completely different raw, unbound dark-gray wash. Neither was tokenized and they didn't even match each other. Gave Default a real, consistent treatment across all 3 sizes: fill → `background/status/neutral` (existed, unused — and its resolved value happens to closely match what Large's hand-rolled wash was already going for, confirming Large was closer to the right idea, just not bound), border → `border/default`, text → `content/default`.
- **Error's fill used the raw global `color/error/200`** instead of the semantic `background/status/error` — same resolved color (so invisible today), but breaks the "never bind global directly" rule and would silently drift if the semantic token is ever retuned independently. Rebound to the semantic token.
- **Error was missing its border entirely** — Default/Success/Warning all had one, Error didn't. Added, bound to `border/status-error`.
- **Success/Warning strokes were bound to `content/status-{X}` instead of `border/status-{X}`** — the exact same mistake just fixed on Alert, recurring here. Fixed both.
- Re-verified: 0 stale colors/opacity, 0 non-local variables, 0 unbound raw paints across all 12 variants.

**Self-correction: initially added a border to Error too, then removed all borders per user decision.** Error was the only one of the 4 types with no stroke at all — flagged it as "missing" and added one (`border/status-error`) alongside the other token fixes, without separating that judgment call from the objective binding bugs above. User caught this: it wasn't necessarily a bug, could've been intentional. Reconsidered, and the user's actual call went further — **no type should have a border at all**. Removed strokes entirely from all 12 variants; Tag is now fill + text only, no border on any type. Lesson: "the other three have X and this one doesn't" is a consistency observation, not proof of a bug — should have flagged it as a question the same way the background-tint/contrast tradeoffs on Alert were, not bundled it in with the objective wrong-token fixes.

**Left as-is, not a bug:** a `Checkbox` element exists on the Large-size variants only, fully built but permanently hidden (`visible: false`) with no exposed property — same "built but not exposed" shape as other components this session, likely a selectable/filterable-tag capability. User explicitly chose to leave it hidden for now rather than expose it. Leading/Trailing icon booleans were already correctly wired (default off) before this session touched the component — no action needed there.

### Template Card (renamed from "Toast") — ✅ done (2026-08-21)

Found via a new `-> Toast` page, single `COMPONENT` (not a variant set), id `4912:7049`. **Named "Toast" but isn't one** — no dismiss button, no status-driven color, and has a large decorative illustration, none of which fit a standard transient-notification toast (Material/Ant/Chakra pattern: small, icon + message, dismissible, auto-color-coded). User confirmed: it's actually a **template/widget-library card** (thumbnail preview + title + status tag + optional CTA + overflow menu with Rename/Duplicate/Delete/Get code). Recommend renaming away from "Toast" to avoid confusing the next person expecting a notification component.

**Real bugs found and fixed:**
- Card's own border used `border/interactive` (meant for form controls) instead of `border/default` (cards/dividers) — the inner illustration sub-frame already correctly used `border/default`, just the outer card didn't match.
- **This card was referencing an entirely different, completely un-tokenized duplicate Tag component** (`State=Succes` *(sic — typo)*/`Error`/`Warning`/`Branded`, every variant with raw unbound fills) and duplicate un-tokenized Button/Action and Button/Icon components — not the ones already fixed earlier this session. Confirmed by checking the actual master components' bindings, not just this card's instances. **User's call: swap the instances to point at the correct, already-tokenized Tag/Button/Action/Button/Icon components** rather than tokenizing the duplicates — treats the un-tokenized duplicates as dead weight, not a second real variant family. Swapped via reassigning `.mainComponent` on all 4 instances, restored the one known original text content (`Tag1` = "Using 'Local Getaways: Homepage' default") since the swap reset text overrides to placeholder defaults.
- **The actual "cards appear black, content invisible" bug**: the "Homepage" title was bound to `content/default` (near-black) sitting on `background/surface/inverse` (near-black) — **1.05:1 contrast, functionally invisible**. This is exactly the scenario `content/inverse` exists for ("text/icons on a dark or filled surface") but wasn't used. Fixed → `content/inverse`, now 18.89:1. Caught by the user visually, not by my own binding-validity audit — a reminder that "is this bound to a valid local token" and "is this the semantically correct token for its actual background" are different checks; the first passed here while the second badly failed. Ran a full ancestor-aware contrast sweep afterward (matching each text node to its *nearest actual background*, not just the top-level card) to confirm nothing else on the card has the same failure mode — clean.

**Second correction, same day: the surface choice itself was wrong, not just the text color on top of it.** User asked the right follow-up question after the first fix: "were we meant to use inverse tokens... these are cards on a page?" The guide is explicit — `surface/inverse` is scoped to "Tooltips, toasts, high-contrast callouts," while `surface/raised` is scoped to exactly "Cards, panels, modals, popovers, menus, dropdowns." Now that this is understood to be a persistent template/widget card (not a toast), `surface/inverse` was the wrong surface family entirely, not just missing the right content token on top. User confirmed: should be a normal light card. Changed card fill `surface/inverse`→`surface/raised`, title text `content/inverse`→`content/default`.

**That change surfaced a second, more serious bug in my own earlier fix: all 4 swapped instances (2 Tags, Button/Action, Button/Icon) had `.fills = []` set during the component-swap step, intending to "clear the override and inherit the master's real fill" — but in the Plugin API, assigning an empty array creates an actual *empty/transparent* override, it does not reset anything.** All 4 instances were silently rendering with **no fill at all** — invisible on the dark card (where text alone stayed legible so it wasn't obviously broken), then fully exposed once the card turned white and the CTA button's white text landed on a transparent button with the white card showing through (0 contrast, caught by a follow-up contrast sweep). Fixed properly by copying each instance's actual bound-fill object from its `mainComponent` rather than clearing to `[]`. Lesson for next time: **`instance.fills = []` is a real override, not a reset** — to make an instance inherit its master's value, copy the master's actual paint/value onto the instance instead of clearing it.

**Renamed** page, section, and master component from "Toast" → "Template Card" — confirmed via research this is genuinely standard terminology (Airtable and others use "template card" for exactly this pattern: thumbnail + title + rename/duplicate/delete actions). Considered "Widget Card" too, given the overflow menu's "Get code" action reads more like an embeddable-widget action than a generic template action — user's call: Template Card.

**Spacing/radius audit (a second pass, after colors were already fixed) found the same "reached for whatever token had the matching number" mistake, just in a different token family:**
- Card's own corner radius bound to `size/2` (raw **global** primitive) instead of `border/radius/md` — fixed.
- Content area padding bound to `size/6` (raw global) instead of `spacing/padding/400` — fixed.
- Header row gap (10px) was unbound and didn't cleanly match any spacing step — snapped to `spacing/gap/200` (8px) and bound.
- The "···" button's corner radius (40, for full-round) was bound to **`font/size/xxxl`** — a font-size token driving a corner radius, purely because the numbers happened to coincide. Cleared it to a plain literal; full-round radii on icon buttons are typically not tokenized in most systems anyway (just "large enough to guarantee a circle").
- Both Tags' vertical padding: one was bound to `size/1` (raw global), the other to **`border/radius/sm`** — a radius token bound to a padding field, wrong family entirely, same "number matched" mistake as the font/radius one above. Both fixed to `spacing/padding/100`.
- CTA button's internal gap (4px) was unbound — bound to `spacing/gap/100`.

Re-ran the full recursive audit (colors + corner radius + padding/gap) after all fixes: 0 issues except the one already-flagged, out-of-scope Button/Icon "plus" fill from before.

**Flagged, not fixed (separate, wider-reaching issues surfaced along the way):**
- A raw unbound white fill on the "plus" icon nested inside the *correct* Button/Icon component (already documented as fully audited) — found only because this card started using that specific icon. Blast radius across Button/Icon's 150 variants couldn't be confirmed (scan came back empty, likely the known nested-instance-resolution limitation, not proof it's clean).
- Tag's own text-vs-background contrast (Success 3.99:1, Error 3.04:1) is below AA — pre-existing to the Tag component itself, affects every use of Tag, not unique to this card.
- Preview illustration has no swap mechanism at all — it's meant to show a per-template screenshot but is hardcoded to always render the same placeholder ripple graphic. User confirmed real preview components don't exist yet but should eventually; deferred adding an `INSTANCE_SWAP` property until they do (premature without real candidates to swap to).

### Widget Type Card — ✅ done, converted from a plain frame to a real component (2026-08-21)

Found via a new `-> Other test` page as a plain `FRAME` (not a component at all) named "Widger type cards" *(typo)*, id `4965:2053`. Same structural shape as Template Card — title, status Tag, hidden "not configured" warning row, an overflow action button — but here the preview area is a real **`Widget types` component set (6 variants: Question bar/Inline/Floating/Hover/Preview loading/Preview failed)**, not a placeholder illustration. **Worth circling back to Template Card**: this may be exactly the kind of real preview component that slot was waiting for — evaluate whether Template Card's illustration should instance-swap to this once there's a reason to connect them.

**Directly relevant to the designer's typography-scope question from earlier:** the title text ("Section page", 16px Haffer Medium) visually matched our real `Accent/Tight/Desktop/400` but its `textStyleId` resolved to **a non-local, foreign style** — not our token at all, just something that happened to look identical today. Confirms the answer given to the designer in practice: checking "does this look like the right size" isn't enough, a component's text has to actually be *bound* to a real local token, since foreign/stale styles can coincidentally match and hide themselves indefinitely. Fixed by reassigning to the real local style (same "fetch fresh from `getLocalTextStylesAsync`, reassign `textStyleId`" recipe from the original gotcha).

**Other bugs, same familiar shapes as everything else this session:**
- Outer card fill + stroke both raw/unbound → `background/surface/raised` + `border/default`.
- The Tag instance had the identical `fills = []` empty-override bug from Template Card, *plus* a stray raw stroke override even though Tags haven't had borders since the earlier "no borders on any tags" decision — cleared the stroke, copied the real fill from its master.
- The `Widget types` preview instance and the "···" `Button/Icon` instance both had raw fill/stroke overrides sitting on top of otherwise-correct masters — copied the real values from each master rather than clearing to `[]` (applying last time's lesson correctly this time).
- Three separate `itemSpacing` values (8, 10, 10) were unbound — bound to `spacing/gap/200`, snapping the two 10s down to 8.
- The alert-triangle icon's color was a raw red literal → bound to `content/status-error`.
- Fixed the "Widger" → "Widget" typo in the frame's own name.

**User caught something I couldn't have from the binding alone: the Tag instance's `mainComponent` itself was pointing at a remote/foreign component, not this file's own Tag.** My earlier fix corrected the *paint* bindings but never checked whether the instance's `mainComponent.remote` was `true` — a case the color/spacing audits in this file hadn't needed to check for before now, since every other "wrong token" bug this session was a local-but-wrong binding, not a genuinely remote instance source. User manually reassigned the instance to a real local Tag variant (`Size=Medium, Type=Default`, part of the already-fixed `4874:2165` set); verified afterward: `mainComponent.remote === false`, fill correctly on the local `background/status/neutral` variable, no stroke. **New standing check to add to future component audits: confirm `instance.mainComponent.remote` is `false`, not just that its paints resolve to local variables** — a remote instance can still have locally-bound-*looking* overrides layered on top that mask the real problem.

Re-ran the full recursive audit after all fixes: 0 issues. **Converted the fixed frame into a real Figma `COMPONENT`** (`figma.createComponentFromNode`, per the user's explicit ask — it was never a component before, just a loose frame), renamed to "Widget Type Card," and exposed the previously-hidden "not configured" warning row as a real `Not Configured` boolean property (default `false`) — same "expose the hidden capability" pattern as Input's icon slots, Alert's Title, and Tag's Checkbox. Verified both states render correctly via test instances.

### Topic-selection modal ("choose one topic") — ✅ done, plus 2 new design proposals (2026-08-21)

User's v1 modal (page `-> Multiple choice`, `5036:2118`) is a flat list of 5 topic rows (colored tag chip + a radio-style circle indicator) plus a "Type your own" input row — simpler than the rich expandable reference they'd shown, and confirmed intentionally so: v1 is topics-only, single-select, no expand.

**Audit + fix:** 104 unbound spacing/radius issues across all 6 near-identical rows (fixed via a uniform loop), one real inconsistency (the "Sponsorships" row had different vertical padding than its siblings and a stray white fill neither other row had — normalized and removed), 0 issues after. **Converted the row into a real `Topic Row` component** (was a plain duplicated frame before) with a `State` variant: Default / Hover (`background/interactive/hover` tint) / Selected (radio circle fills solid with `background/action/primary-default` + a `content/inverse` checkmark — per the guide's rule 4, a committed single-choice pick uses `action`, not `interactive/selected`, which is reserved for nav "you are here" state). Swapped the modal's 5 static rows to real instances of this component.

**Noted but not changed:** the radio-style circle indicator is semantically correct for single-select (it was already circular, not square — despite reading initially as "checkbox," it's actually radio-shaped), but there's no real `Radio` component in this file yet (confirmed absent from the component list). Left as-is per "subtle changes only" — flagged as a candidate for a proper Radio component if single-select pickers become a repeated pattern.

**Option 2 — modern chip-cloud alternative (still v1 scope, no expand):** a wrapping row of pill chips instead of a vertical list. Deliberately moved away from the 5-different-rainbow-colors-per-topic treatment toward a neutral default (outlined, `border/default`) + bold solid `action/primary` + checkmark for the selected chip — closer to current (Linear/Arc/iOS-era) minimal selector patterns than the more Material-2018-era multicolor-pill look, while unselected chips stay neutral so the one selection reads instantly. Includes a dashed-border "+ Type your own" chip matching the existing free-text affordance.

**Option 3 — expandable accordion, for when the roadmap gets there:** category rows (chevron + colored tag chip, kept for continuity with the original reference + prompt count + the same radio indicator from the fixed Topic Row) expand to reveal the tracked prompts underneath — but the prompt rows are deliberately built as visually inert (muted `content/secondary` text on `Default/Tight/Desktop/300`, small non-interactive number badges, subtle zebra shading via `background/surface/sunken`, no radio/hover states at all) so it's clear only the category itself is selectable, exactly matching "expand to preview tracked prompts, still select the topic bucket only."

**Recommendation given to the user:** Option 2 for the current v1 scope (most modern, most intuitive at a glance for "no expand needed yet"); Option 3 is the right foundation to reach for once the expand-to-preview-prompts capability actually ships, rather than a competing alternative to Option 2 — they represent v1 vs. the next iteration, not two choices for the same moment.

**Option 3b — solving "these are illustrative examples, not the exhaustive tracked list" without extra copy (2026-08-21).** User liked Option 3 but needed to communicate that expanding a topic shows *example* prompts, not literally every prompt tracked — while explicitly not wanting to reveal that only one prompt is actually tracked per topic. Two minimal, low-text moves instead of an explanatory sentence: (1) **replaced the sequential number badges (1, 2, 3...) with plain bullet dots** — sequential numbering subtly signals "this is the complete, exact, counted list," which is precisely the wrong implication here; a bullet doesn't carry that baggage. (2) **Added a tiny "EXAMPLE PROMPTS" caption label** (small, muted, letter-spaced like a section eyebrow) directly above the list — two words, states what it is without justifying why or leaking the one-tracked-prompt implementation detail. (3) A trailing "···" row reinforces "more like this exist" purely visually. Net: zero new sentences, two words of new copy total.

**Also found and fixed, unrelated to the framing task:** the Option 3 version being duplicated from already had a real content bug — two prompt rows both read "Top rated sweatshirts for everyday wear" (a duplicated row whose text was never changed). Not introduced by this task; fixed with a distinct fourth prompt.

### Pagination — two real bugs from my own earlier fix, caught by the user (2026-08-21)

**Bug 1 — hiding the checkmark via `visible = false` collapsed its layout space, breaking row alignment.** In Figma auto-layout, `visible = false` removes a child from the layout flow entirely (like CSS `display:none`), it does not just make it invisible in place (like `visibility:hidden`). Setting the checkmark to invisible for Default/Hover states caused their text to shift left into the checkmark's old position, so unselected rows' text no longer lined up with the selected row's. Fixed by using `opacity = 0` instead (keeps `visible = true`, so the layout slot stays reserved) for Default/Hover, and `opacity = 1` for Selected. General lesson: `visible` and `opacity` are not interchangeable for "hide but reserve space" — `opacity: 0` is the one that preserves alignment in auto-layout.

**Bug 2 — cloned the wrong vector for the checkmark in the first place.** When originally replacing the remote "Check" icon, I grabbed the *first* `VECTOR` found inside a checked Checkbox instance — which turned out to be the checkbox's own solid background square (`Rectangle 1`), not the actual tick-mark glyph (nested one level deeper inside a `light/check` instance). Result: every "selected" row showed a solid black square instead of a checkmark. Fixed by finding and cloning the real nested tick-mark vector instead. Lesson: `findOne(n => n.type === 'VECTOR')` finds the *first* match in document order, not necessarily the semantically-relevant one — worth confirming what a cloned vector actually contains before trusting it, especially in a component (like Checkbox) that has more than one vector in its tree.

Both caught visually by the user, not by any binding-validity audit — a reminder that automated token/binding checks don't catch layout-behavior or wrong-asset bugs; visual review still matters even after a clean audit.

**Also caught: `State=Hover, Size=Small` was the only one of all 9 variants set to `HUG`/`AUTO` sizing instead of `FIXED`** — giving it an odd fractional width (110.47px) while its Default/Selected siblings at the same size were a clean fixed 92px. A menu item resizing itself on hover would make the whole dropdown list visibly jump. Fixed to match (92×28, `FIXED`/`FIXED`). All 9 variants now confirmed consistent per size tier.

**Also corrected: a second, separate local component set — "pagination dropdown" (`5071:4046`, Default/Hover/Active × Small/Medium/Large) — existed and was never found during the original pagination audit.** This is the real local source for the "10 ▾" page-size trigger; earlier this was incorrectly flagged as "no local equivalent exists yet." Audited and fixed it properly: the Active state's border was bound to **`brand/primary`** — a direct, guide-confirmed violation ("brand/* — not for functional UI"), caught by the user, not by my own audit. Also found its focus-ring effect was bound to the wrong token (`border/interactive`, not `border/focus`) and set invisible even though Active should show it. Fixed both (Active border → `border/interactive-strong`, ring → visible `border/focus`, matching Input's own Active-state pattern), plus the same unbound-color/stale-style/lowercase-naming cleanup as everywhere else. Then swapped Table Pagination's remote "pagination result per page" instances to this real local component, closing the gap properly. The Back/Next `pagination control` instances remain genuinely remote with no local equivalent — that part of the earlier flag still stands.

**Added a missing `Pressed` state to Pagination Menu Item, per the user's catch.** Only had `Default`/`Hover`/`Selected` — no momentary click-down state, even though Button/Icon's own established state set already includes `Pressed` as standard, and Pressed/Selected are genuinely different things (same distinction drawn out for Radio earlier: momentary vs. persistent). Added 3 new variants (`Pressed` × Small/Medium/Large), fill bound to `background/interactive/pressed` — the guide's own natural sibling to `interactive/hover` ("the same surfaces while being pressed/actively clicked"). Now 12 variants total, 0 issues, laid out as a clean 4-column grid (Default/Hover/Selected/Pressed) per size row.

### Pagination (3 component sets) — ✅ done (2026-08-21)

Found via `-> Page pagination`: **Pagination Menu Item** (9 variants, State×Size — a page-size dropdown option), **Pagination Dropdown Results** (3 variants — the open page-size dropdown), **Table Pagination** (3 variants — the assembled `Show [10▾] ... 2 of 4 [< >]` bar). Researched first per the user's ask: this is a real, standard pattern — [Carbon Design System](https://carbondesignsystem.com/components/pagination/usage/) documents the exact same anatomy (items-per-page selector + range label + prev/next, "placed at the bottom of the data table") for table pagination specifically, as distinct from article/blog numbered-page pagination. The pattern choice was right; the mess was structural.

**Real structural bugs found and fixed (not just tokens):**
- **Every item in the page-size dropdown showed as "Selected" simultaneously** — all 5 options in every size tier were bound to the `Selected` variant. Also **every option showed the literal same text ("10") repeated 5 times** — not real distinct choices at all. Fixed both: real distinct values (10/25/50/100/200), only the first marked Selected, rest Default. (First attempt at the value fix silently failed because I set the text *before* reassigning `mainComponent` instead of after — same override-reset behavior as the Template Card fills bug earlier; redid it in the right order.)
- **A stray auto-generated duplicate variant, `Size=Size4`** on the dropdown set — exact duplicate of `Size=Small`'s content. Same smell as Radio's `State4`. Removed.
- **`itemSpacing: 1110`** on the assembled bar's root frame, unbound — turned out to be completely inert dead data: `primaryAxisAlignItems` is already `SPACE_BETWEEN`, which makes `itemSpacing` unused. Not a real bug once understood, just confusing leftover; reset to 0 rather than binding a meaningless number to a token.

**Token/binding fixes, following the same patterns as everywhere else this session:**
- Remote "Check" icon (all 9 Menu Item variants) → replaced with a clone of Checkbox's real local checkmark vector (not a raw override — a genuine local source).
- Non-local Hover background → `background/interactive/hover` (guide's own listed use case: "menu items, list rows").
- Non-local text fills + stale/foreign text styles ("10" labels, "Show", "2 of 4") → `content/default` + the correct `Default/Comfy/Desktop/*` step per size tier (200/300/400 for Small/Medium/Large).
- Dropdown wrapper's non-local white fill → `background/surface/raised` (guide's own listed use case: "menus, dropdowns").
- Removed a stray empty "Text" node (single space character, white-on-white, functionally invisible) from 2 of 3 dropdown size tiers.
- Nested remote chevron/text colors inside the Back/Next controls and the page-size trigger → rebound directly to `content/default` even though the parent instances remain remote (see below).

**Not fully resolved — flagged, not silently left unclear:** the Back/Next arrow controls and the page-size dropdown trigger (`pagination control`, `pagination result per page`) are still **remote instances** — no local equivalent exists yet to swap them to. Button/Icon (already correct and local) has no generic icon-swap property, so it can't be dropped in directly as a replacement without a bigger rebuild. Fixed their visible colors directly since that's accessible even on a remote instance, but the components themselves still need a proper local rebuild — a real open item, not something resolved by this pass.

**Naming cleanup, per explicit request to check property-naming consistency against the rest of the system:** all 3 component sets were lowercase (`pagination menu item`, `pagination dropdown results`, `table pagination web, mobile`) and all `Size` variant values were lowercase (`small/medium/large`) — inconsistent with the established Title Case convention already used by Button/Icon and Tag (`Small`/`Medium`/`Large`). Renamed all 3 sets to Title Case (`Pagination Menu Item`, `Pagination Dropdown Results`, `Table Pagination` — dropped the awkward "web, mobile" comma-suffix, matching how no other component set appends platform names), and capitalized every `Size` value across all 3 sets to match.

### Dark mode bug: 3 of 4 `border/status-*` tokens were nearly invisible, and Success was the wrong hue entirely (2026-08-21)

User flagged that Alert's borders "aren't visible enough" in Dark mode. Checked properly with alpha-compositing (not just raw RGB comparison — two of these are translucent, so that step actually mattered): against the Dark-mode page background, **Info was 1.70:1, Warning 1.21:1, Success 1.02:1** — all functionally invisible (need ~3:1 minimum for a UI border). Only Error was fine (6.47:1).

Traced each to its root cause:
- **Info** aliased to `color/blue/500` — a mid-tone blue, simply too dark to read against a near-black surface. Dark mode needs the *opposite* move from light mode (lighter colors read better on dark backgrounds, not darker ones).
- **Warning** aliased to `color/warning/450`, which has **10% opacity baked into the primitive itself** — diluting an otherwise-good vibrant yellow into near-nothing. Found `color/warning/400` is the exact same RGB at full opacity (same hue, no alpha) — simple retarget.
- **Success** aliased to `color/blue/550` — **a blue primitive**, not green. Almost certainly a copy-paste error from Info's setup that never got swapped to the success/green family. This is why Success rendered as a dark, barely-visible blue instead of green.

Fixed all three (Dark mode values only — Light mode was already correct and untouched): Info → `color/blue/200` (6.06:1), Warning → `color/warning/400` full-opacity (11:1), Success → `color/success/500` (7.45:1, correcting both the hue and the contrast). Verified with a temporary Dark-mode-forced preview (`setExplicitVariableModeForCollection`) showing all 4 Alert variants — all four borders now read clearly, and Success is genuinely green for the first time. Since this fix lives at the token level, it automatically applies everywhere `border/status-*` is used (Alert, Tag), not just the one component that surfaced it.

### Radio (Option/Radio) — ✅ done (2026-08-21)

Found via a new `-> Radio` page — `Option/Radio` component set (`5034:1531`), 3 states (Pressed/Default/Hover). Existed in the file already but had never been through an audit; `COMPONENT_GUIDE.md` still listed Radio as "not yet covered."

**Found:** the label ("Cell" instance) in all 3 states was a **remote instance** — a generic foreign "Type=Title" component (likely meant for a table cell in its source library), holding placeholder text "Label," carrying a stray unbound fill and unbound spacing. Nothing about a radio label needs a foreign table-cell component. Replaced it in all 3 states with a plain local text node bound to `Accent/Tight/Desktop/400` (16px Medium — the same step Checkbox already uses for its own labels) + `content/default`. Also fixed unbound `itemSpacing` (→ `spacing/gap/200`) at both the wrapper and variant level.

**Walked back an earlier "leave it, it's a judgment call" note — it was wrong.** Initially waved off the outer ring's `border/interactive-strong` fill and the selected dot's `content/default` as a legitimate stylistic choice. User pushed back and asked directly whether it should be `action`/`content/inverse` instead — checking the actual resolved values settled it: `border/interactive-strong` is a **translucent 10%-opacity wash**, while `background/action/primary-default` is **solid, 100% opacity**. The guide's Rule 4 ("a committed choice — checkbox, chip — is high-emphasis → `action`") and Checkbox/Chip's own established idiom (solid `action/primary` fill + `content/inverse` mark on top when selected) make this unambiguous: Default/Hover states are fine keeping the translucent wash (nothing's committed yet), but **Selected specifically was missing the "owns a fill" treatment entirely** — the ring stayed a faint wash even when checked, with only a small near-black dot appearing on top of it, instead of the ring itself going solid dark with a white dot on top. First attempt overcorrected: made the outer ring a *solid* `background/action/primary-default` fill with a white (`content/inverse`) dot on top — directly copying Checkbox's "solid fill + inverse mark" idiom. User immediately flagged it didn't feel like a normal radio button, and they were right — checked against real convention (radio buttons near-universally use a **hollow ring + a solid dot in the same accent color**, not a filled disc with a contrasting cutout; that inverse-cutout pattern belongs to Checkbox, where a checkmark glyph genuinely needs contrast to read, not to a plain dot). **Final fix**: reverted the outer ring back to the original translucent `border/interactive-strong` wash (unchanged from Default/Hover — it was never actually wrong), and changed only the inner dot from `content/default` to `background/action/primary-default` — same token family the guide calls for, just paired correctly this time with the *ring-and-dot* idiom instead of the *solid-fill* one. Lesson: "guide says committed choice → `action`" doesn't mean every selected-state fix should ape Checkbox's exact visual treatment — the correct application still has to match the actual convention for that specific control shape.

**Two more things caught by the user during final review, both fixed:**
- A **stray 4th variant, `State=State4`**, had appeared on `Option/Radio` as a side effect of the Radio Indicator extraction work — an exact duplicate of the `Selected` state's content (same indicator + label), never processed by any of the fixes above since it didn't exist yet when the audit ran. Removed; `Option/Radio` is back to exactly 3 states.
- **Naming mismatch: `Option/Radio`'s state was called "Pressed," but functionally it's "Selected."** Pressed and Selected are genuinely different concepts — Pressed is a momentary `:active`-style state while the mouse is down, Selected is persistent (this option *is* the checked one, click or no click). What this component calls "Pressed" was always doing Selected's job (showing the filled dot), which is exactly what got mapped to Radio Indicator's own `Selected` variant. Renamed `Option/Radio`'s state to `Selected` to match, removing the ambiguity and making the two component sets consistently named. If a true momentary Pressed state (e.g. a brief click-flash) is ever needed, that would be a genuinely new fourth state to add later — not something to conflate with Selected.

Re-ran the full audit after fixes: 0 issues across all 3 states (colors, spacing, radius, text styles, remote-instance check).

**Extracted the dot/circle itself into its own atomic component, at the user's request** — so it's reusable anywhere a selection indicator is needed, not permanently fused to a label. New component set **`Radio Indicator`** (`5075:109`), 3 states matching `Option/Radio`'s own (`Selected`/`Default`/`Hover`), built by cloning the exact existing shapes out of each `Option/Radio` variant (preserving the double-layered fill technique on Hover). `Option/Radio` now composes an instance of `Radio Indicator` next to its label text, rather than inlining the shape group directly. Verified: 0 token issues on the new component, and all 3 `Option/Radio` states correctly reference the matching *local* `Radio Indicator` variant (confirmed non-remote).

### Pagination control (Back/Next arrows) — ✅ done, correcting an earlier false "remote, no local equivalent" claim (2026-08-30)

**This corrects two of my own prior notes above** ("The Back/Next `pagination control` instances remain genuinely remote with no local equivalent" and "the Back/Next arrow controls... are still remote instances — no local equivalent exists yet"). Both were wrong. The user pushed back directly ("what do you mean you can't add") and a fresh, targeted read of `5071:3926` ("pagination control") showed `isRemote: false` — a real, fully local `COMPONENT_SET` with 36 variants (`Type` = Back/Next/First/Last × `State` = Default/Hover/Disabled × `Size` = small/medium/large). I'd missed it entirely — same blind spot as the earlier "pagination dropdown" trigger discovery, now repeated on a second component in the same system. Worth remembering: "no local equivalent" should be a conclusion from having actually inspected the node, not an assumption carried over from an earlier, unverified pass.

**Audited and fixed once the real component was found:**
- **Naming** — `Size` values were lowercase (`small/medium/large`), inconsistent with the Title Case convention already applied everywhere else on this page; Title-cased all 36. Also found `State` had one stray typo'd value, `Disable` (on exactly one variant, `Type=First, State=Disable, Size=large` — not a duplicate, just the misspelled 12th "Disabled" slot), renamed to match.
- **Colors** — the root fill for every Default/Disabled variant was bound to a **non-local (foreign/library) variable**, not a real local token, despite looking visually correct (plain white) — same "bound to *something*, not the *right* thing" class of bug as Tag's earlier cross-file pointer. Rebound to `background/surface/raised`, matching the sibling "pagination dropdown" trigger's own Default/Active token (checked for consistency first). The Hover state's background was a **translucent wash bound to a different foreign variable** — rebound to a solid `background/interactive/hover`, again matching what both "pagination dropdown" and Pagination Menu Item already use; there's no local wash/overlay token in this file, so the translucent-wash pattern here was never the established convention to begin with. The `border/default` stroke was already correctly local — untouched.
- **Icons** — the nested Chevron left/right/double-chevron instances are genuinely remote (confirmed: the sibling "pagination dropdown" trigger's own chevron icon is *also* remote) — this reads as a shared external icon library, not a stray unfixed component reference, so left as-is rather than treated as a violation.
- **Added the missing `Pressed` state**, per the same Pressed-vs-Selected/Hover gap already caught on Pagination Menu Item — Default/Hover/Disabled existed but no momentary click-down state. Cloned each of the 12 Hover variants, renamed to `Pressed`, rebound fill to `background/interactive/pressed` (the same token Menu Item's own Pressed state uses). Now 48 variants total (Type × State[Default/Hover/Pressed/Disabled] × Size), reorganized into a clean 4-row × 12-column grid.
- **Swapped Table Pagination's 6 Back/Next instances** (2 per size tier: Small/Medium/Large) off the remote foreign component onto this real local one — the same fix already applied earlier to the page-size trigger, now closing the parity gap for Back/Next too. Verified visually across all 3 sizes post-swap.

**New gotcha: `.clone()` on a variant `COMPONENT` that's a child of a `COMPONENT_SET` does not preserve that parent.** The clone gets appended to `figma.currentPage` instead — which, per the standing page-reset rule, is whatever page loads first in a fresh script (here, "Cover," not the pagination page) — silently landing 12 new components on the wrong page entirely. Caught immediately by re-fetching the component set right after cloning and finding it still reported the old variant count. Fixed by explicitly `componentSet.appendChild(clone)` for each. Also hit a related sizing issue: repositioning children via raw `x`/`y` does not auto-grow the `COMPONENT_SET` frame's own bounding box — had to explicitly `resizeWithoutConstraints()` the set afterward (never plain `.resize()`, per the earlier Menu Item scaling bug) once the true max child extent was known.

### Button/Action — ✅ done (audited 2026-08-16)
45 variants: 3 types (Primary/Secondary/Tertiary) × 3 sizes (Small/Medium/Large) × 5 states (Default/Hover/Inactive/Pressed/Focus).

Token map (what "correct" means for this component):
- **Primary**: fill `background/action/primary-default|hover|pressed`, label `content/inverse`
- **Secondary**: fill `background/action/secondary-default|hover|pressed`, stroke `border/strong` (1px) at every state, label `content/default`. *(Note: the token guide describes Secondary as transparent-fill + `border/interactive-strong` — the user explicitly chose to keep the already-built solid-fill + `border/strong` look instead and standardize on it. If revisiting this component, don't "fix" it back toward the doc without asking first.)*
- **Tertiary**: no fill at rest, hover/pressed tint via `background/interactive/hover|pressed`, label `content/default`
- **Inactive** (any type): muted fill `background/action/secondary-default` (Primary) or dropped border `border/interactive-disabled` + no fill (Secondary/Tertiary), label `content/disabled`, no hover/pressed
- **Focus** (any type): same base look as Default, plus a `DROP_SHADOW` effect bound to `border/focus` (offset 0/0, radius 0, spread 2) as an offset ring; Primary additionally gets a 2px `background/surface/page` stroke to create the visible "gap" against its solid fill

### Button/Icon — ✅ done, full 5-type parity (audited 2026-08-16)
75 variants: all 5 types (Primary/Secondary/Tertiary/Danger/Quaternary) × 3 sizes × 5 states — matches Button/Action exactly. Same token logic throughout: the circle background uses the identical fill tokens as the text-button container per type; the icon glyph itself is a **stroke** (not fill) on a nested `light/plus` instance's Vector child. Danger reuses the exact same `danger-subtle-*` tokens and near-black→white text-color crossover (Default/Hover/Focus dark, Pressed white) as Button/Action's Danger, with the same red Focus ring (`border/status-error`, per the user's explicit preference — see Button/Action's Danger section above, do not "fix" this to `border/focus`).

**Gotcha hit while building this — RESOLVED, not actually a bug:** the Tertiary and Quaternary creation scripts initially appeared to land with wrong names (`Ghost` and `Tertiary` swapped from what was intended). Root cause: **the user was independently renaming things directly in Figma at the same time** — they renamed the original transparent/ghost-style type from `Tertiary` → `Ghost`, and the grey-filled type from `Quaternary` → `Tertiary`, on **Button/Action itself**. I mistook this for a bug and "fixed" it back once, which the user then had to correct me on. **Current, correct, final naming (as of 2026-08-16) — matches on both Button/Action and Button/Icon:**
- `Ghost` = the transparent/no-fill-at-rest style (was originally called Tertiary)
- `Tertiary` = the grey-filled style (was originally called Quaternary — `background/action/quaternary-*` tokens are still named that internally, only the display Type value changed)
- `Quaternary` no longer exists as a name anywhere in the file.

**Do not rename these again** — if something looks like a naming mismatch between Action/Icon or against this doc's older sections above (which still say "Quaternary" from when it was first built), trust the CURRENT live file and this correction, not the earlier prose.

### Button/Action — Danger variant — ✅ built, then replaced with a subtle style (2026-08-16)
A 4th `Type` value, not in the original token guide (guide only documents Primary/Secondary/Tertiary) — added per explicit user request. 15 variants: 3 sizes × 5 states, at a 4th row per size block. Cloned structurally from the equivalent Primary variant (same padding/radius/ring — only fill/text differ), so Inactive is intentionally identical to Primary's Inactive (disabled state doesn't carry type-specific color, matching how Secondary/Tertiary/Quaternary Inactive already converge).

**History, because it matters for the next colored-button type:**

1. **First attempt — solid fill** (mirrored Primary's structure: dark saturated fill, white text, white-gap-stroke Focus ring). Created `background/action/danger-{default,hover,pressed}` aliased to the `color/error` ramp, and initially bound the label to `content/inverse` — **wrong**: `content/inverse` is calibrated to contrast with Primary's own fill, which *fully flips polarity* between modes (near-black↔near-white). Danger's fill doesn't flip that way — it stays a red hue in both modes, getting *darker* at Pressed in dark mode. `content/inverse` there put near-black text on dark red: harsh, hard to read. Fixed at the time by adding a dedicated fixed-white `background/action/danger-content` token instead of reusing `content/inverse`.
   **Lesson for any future colored-fill button type:** don't reach for `content/inverse` by reflex just because Primary uses it — check whether the new type's fill actually flips polarity like Primary's does. If it just shifts shade within one hue, it needs its own fixed "-content" token, not `content/inverse`.
2. **User reconsidered** after test-driving the solid style and finding an old internal design system reference showing a two-tier destructive pattern: a low-emphasis tinted "Delete light" alongside a high-emphasis solid "Delete". Confirmed this is standard practice (Carbon, Ant Design both do exactly this — separate emphasis levels for destructive actions, mirroring how Primary/Secondary scale emphasis for regular actions) and recommended keeping *both* tiers, since the solid tier's real purpose is reserving visual weight for genuinely irreversible actions. **User decided this product doesn't need that distinction and replaced the solid style entirely** with the subtle one — a deliberate, informed simplification, not an oversight. If a future need arises for a "point of no return" destructive action, the removed solid-style pattern (see git/version history of this doc, or just redo step 1's approach) is the reference to reintroduce as an addition, not a replacement.
3. **Second consideration:** the subtle style's colors happen to already exist in the file as `background/status/error` / `content/status-error` (alert-banner tokens). Directly binding the *button* to those would violate the guide's own Rule 1 ("the name tells you the use") and couple two independent surfaces (alerts, buttons) through one token neither name mentions. Instead, created dedicated button-scoped alias tokens pointing to the *same* underlying primitives:
   - `background/action/danger-subtle-default` — Light: `color/error/200` (matches `status/error`), Dark: `color/error/450` (matches `status/error`)
   - `background/action/danger-subtle-hover` — Light: `color/error/300`, Dark: `color/error/500`
   - `background/action/danger-subtle-pressed` — Light: `color/error/400`, Dark: `color/error/600`
   - `background/action/danger-subtle-content` — Light: `color/error/600` (matches `content/status-error`), Dark: `color/error/400` (matches `content/status-error`) — constant across Default/Hover/Pressed/Focus, same as the solid style's content token was
   Direction: light mode darkens/saturates with interaction (200→300→400, palest at rest), dark mode increases opacity/saturation with interaction (450 translucent → 500 → 600 opaque) — both converge toward the ramp's middle as interaction increases, same principle as Primary and the original solid Danger, just starting from the opposite (pale) end.
4. **Focus** uses the ring effect only, no white-gap stroke (the 2px white inset only makes sense against Primary's dark solid fill; against a pale tint it wouldn't read — same reasoning as Secondary/Tertiary/Quaternary Focus).
5. **Cleanup:** verified zero remaining bindings to the old solid tokens (`danger-{default,hover,pressed,content}`) across Button/Action and Button/Icon, then deleted them rather than leaving them orphaned.

**Not built:** any Danger icon-button, or a solid/subtle pairing (see point 2 above — solid was deliberately removed). Add only if a real use case shows up.

**Accessibility audit (2026-08-16), and a second fix:** ran WCAG contrast checks (computed relative luminance + contrast ratio directly in a `use_figma` script, alpha-compositing translucent fills over the correct page background per mode — don't eyeball this from a screenshot). Found real failures: `danger-subtle-hover`/`-pressed` were aliased to `color/error/300`/`400` (light) and `500`/`600` (dark), which — because Hover/Pressed got progressively more saturated toward the *same hue as the text* — dropped text-vs-fill contrast as low as **1.33:1** in dark-mode Hover (effectively unreadable). Light-mode Default itself was marginal at 4.38:1 (below the 4.5:1 minimum for normal-weight 14-16px text).

**Root cause, worth remembering for any tinted/subtle button state:** don't push a hover/pressed tint's saturation toward the label's own text color to signal "more interaction" — as the fill's hue converges on the text's hue, contrast collapses regardless of which one is "lighter," because WCAG contrast is about relative luminance, not just visual difference. This bit both modes, but dark mode is far less forgiving: swept every opaque step of the `error` ramp against the dark-mode text color and **none** reached 4.5:1 — only a translucent low-alpha overlay did.

**Fix:** replaced the ramp-step aliases with a single unified technique — a fixed alpha of the *same* dark-red hue (`color/error/600`'s RGB) at 8% (Default) / 11% (Hover) / 14% (Pressed), identical in both Light and Dark mode. This is a deliberate exception to "always alias to a primitive ramp step" — verified computationally that translucent alpha-compositing naturally adapts contrast correctly against *either* page background without needing separate per-mode values, the same way the original `background/status/error` dark value (`error/450`, also a 10%-alpha overlay) already worked. Confirmed via the same script: Light 5.16/4.88/4.61, Dark 6.32/6.23/6.14 — all clear 4.5:1 with margin. Focus ring vs page (19.25/18.04 in Light/Dark) and Inactive's `content/disabled` text were also checked — both fine (Inactive is WCAG-exempt anyway, per 1.4.3, since it's a disabled control).

**Trade-off to flag to the user if it comes up:** because all three states now live in a narrow, accessible alpha band (8–14%), Hover and Pressed are visually quite close to Default and to each other — a deliberate accessibility-over-visual-punch trade, not an oversight. If a future design pass wants stronger state differentiation, it needs a different mechanism (e.g. a border appearing on hover) rather than pushing saturation further, which is what broke contrast the first time.

**Handed back to the user, then reworked into its final form (2026-08-16, same day):** user first asked to clear the bindings to tweak themselves, then to restore them, then manually re-pointed the fills to opaque `error/400`/`500`/`600` (Default/Hover/Pressed) with white text throughout — visually a wider, more dramatic pale→vivid→dark range than the `danger-subtle` attempt, closer to how Primary's own states already span a range. That's a legitimate, common pattern (many systems vary fill lightness a lot across states) — but white text only has enough contrast against the darkest step (`600`); against `400`/`500` it fails (2.84:1 / 3.76:1).

**Final fix — per-state (not per-mode) text color switching:** kept the user's fill choice (`error/400` → `500` → `600`) and instead fixed the text: near-black for Default/Hover/Focus (which share the `400` fill), switched to white specifically for Pressed (`600`). This crossover point was found by computing contrast of both candidate text colors against all three fills — near-black scores 6.98/5.26 on Default/Hover but drops to 3.19 (fail) on Pressed; white scores 6.21 on Pressed but fails (2.84/3.76) on Default/Hover. There's no single text color that works across this fill range — it has to switch. This is the same principle Material Design's dynamic color system uses (text/foreground color chosen based on the underlying surface's computed lightness), so it's the standard answer, not a one-off hack.

Because the fills are now opaque (not translucent) and identical in both modes, contrast is mode-independent: **6.98 / 5.26 / 6.21 across Default/Hover/Pressed, identical in Light and Dark, identical across all 3 sizes** (18/18 checks pass). Token changes from this pass:
- `background/action/danger-subtle-content` **repurposed** from fixed-white to **fixed near-black** (`color/darken/1000`, both modes) — now used for Default/Hover/Focus text.
- `background/action/danger-content` **recreated** (it existed earlier for the original solid-Danger attempt, then got deleted as orphaned) as **fixed white** (`color/content/1000`, both modes) — now used only for Pressed text.
- The fill tokens (`danger-subtle-default/hover/pressed`) were NOT touched — still aliased to `error/400`/`500`/`600` as the user set them.

**This is the version currently in the file.** If picking this up again: don't reflexively "fix" it toward the earlier pale `danger-subtle` look — that was superseded intentionally. If the user changes the fill tokens again, re-derive the correct text-color crossover the same way (compute contrast of both candidate text colors against every fill state) rather than assuming these exact tokens still apply.

**Focus ring color — deliberately red, not a bug.** Danger's Focus ring is bound to `border/status-error` (red), not the shared `border/focus` (near-black) that every other type uses. I initially "fixed" this myself, assuming focus rings should look identical across all button types for consistent recognizability — the user explicitly corrected me: they *intend* Danger's focus ring to be red, and I reverted it back. **Do not change this to `border/focus` again without asking** — it reads as a bug from a generic consistency standpoint, but it isn't one here. The white 2px "gap" stroke was still removed from Danger's Focus (fill is too pale for it to read), and that part was not objected to.

### Menu / Menu Item / Menu Divider — ✅ new component, themed from an imported component (2026-08-30)

First component in the system with no prior local precedent — brought in from another design system (`-> Menu` page) with foreign tokens throughout, to fill a real gap (no Menu existed yet). Before building, asked the user to resolve 4 genuine forks rather than guessing, since "think of scalability" was an explicit part of the ask: trailing-slot semantics (→ generic icon, matching the source), icon flexibility (→ INSTANCE_SWAP, not a fixed placeholder like Input/Alert), whether to add a Size variant (→ yes, matching the rest of the system), whether to build a Divider now (→ yes).

**Source structure found:** a `_menuItem` `COMPONENT_SET` (4 states: `state=default/Hover/pressed/disabled` — inconsistent casing, same messy-import pattern seen on every other brought-in component this session) — each variant: leading icon instance + label text + trailing icon instance, HORIZONTAL auto-layout — plus an assembled `Menu` `COMPONENT` (a VERTICAL auto-layout dropdown shell wrapping 4 item instances, all pointing at `Default` regardless of visual position, plus a stray single-space empty text node — same two smells as the earlier Pagination Dropdown Results bug). Every fill/stroke/text-style on both was bound to **foreign (hash-prefixed) variables**, not local tokens, and the label used **Inter Medium** — this file's typography guide uses **Haffer**, never Inter, for real UI text.

**Retokened, following the sibling "list-row" component (Pagination Menu Item) for every scale decision rather than inventing a new one** — the two are the same idiom (icon + label, State × Size) and should read as one family:
- Padding → `spacing/padding/200` (8px, matches Pagination Menu Item exactly). Gap → `spacing/gap/200` (8px) at Small/Medium, `spacing/gap/300` (12px) at Large — the same size-scaling Pagination Menu Item already uses.
- Corner radius → `border/radius/sm` (4px), matching Pagination Menu Item's own radius rather than the source's arbitrary `6`.
- Row height / font size per `Size`, copied directly from Pagination Menu Item's already-established scale: Small = 28px / `Default/Comfy/Desktop/200` (12px), Medium = 36px / `Default/Comfy/Desktop/400` (16px), Large = 40px / `Default/Comfy/Desktop/500` (18px) — all Haffer Regular. The source's one existing size (144×40, Inter Medium 14px) became the `Large` tier; `Small`/`Medium` were built by cloning and rescaling.
- Background per state: Default/Disabled → none; Hover → `background/interactive/hover`; Pressed → `background/interactive/pressed` (same tokens as every other interactive row in the file).
- Text/icon color: `content/default` (Default/Hover/Pressed), `content/disabled` (Disabled) — icons recolored via their actual **stroke** (see bug below), not fill.
- Icon slots left genuinely remote per the icon-flexibility decision above — swapped to `INSTANCE_SWAP` properties instead of a fixed local glyph, so real usage can point at whatever icon fits; the current default is still the source's remote "Acorn" icon since no local icon library exists yet (same standing blocker as Input's icon slots).

**New component properties:** `Label` (TEXT), `Show Leading Icon` / `Show Trailing Icon` (BOOLEAN), `Leading Icon` / `Trailing Icon` (INSTANCE_SWAP) — **discovered mid-build that these already existed** on the source component set, unbound, under typo'd names (`preffix`/`suffix`/`preffixState`/`suffixState`/`label`) and a stale default value pointing at a node that wasn't even the one actually placed in the variants. First pass didn't notice this and created a second, duplicate set of properties with clean names — caught before shipping, by re-reading `componentPropertyDefinitions` after adding mine and seeing both sets listed. Fixed properly: deleted my duplicates, renamed the originals via `editComponentProperty` (which returns a **new** key — the numeric ID suffix persists across a rename, but code must use the returned key, not assume the old one), corrected the stale `INSTANCE_SWAP` default to match the real placed icon (`5071:4846`), then bound all 12 variants' nodes to the corrected keys.

**Two new bugs, self-caught before they shipped:**
- **Recolored the icons on the wrong paint property.** The icon's visible color lives on each nested `VECTOR`'s **`strokes`** (it's a stroke-style icon, `fills: []`) — first pass called the fill-rebind helper on those vectors anyway, which happily bound a *new, correct, local* color to `fills` while leaving the actual visible `strokes` still pointed at the old foreign variable. Silent because an empty/absent fill renders as invisible either way — nothing looked broken until a direct read of the vectors' `strokes` array was checked against `fills`. Fixed by removing the stray fills and rebinding `strokes` instead, across all 24 vectors (2 icons × 3 vector shapes × 4 states, before the Size clone).
- **`.clone()` on a variant does not preserve `componentPropertyReferences`.** After cloning the 4 Large-tier variants into Small/Medium, the clones' text/icon nodes came back with `componentPropertyReferences: {}` — completely disconnected from `Label`/`Leading Icon`/etc. even though every other property (fills, fonts, geometry) cloned correctly. This is a new gotcha distinct from the already-known "`.clone()` on a `COMPONENT_SET` child doesn't preserve its parent" one (found on Pagination control) — the parenting bug is fixed by `appendChild`; this one has no structural fix, it just has to be redone by hand on every clone: re-set `componentPropertyReferences` on the cloned nodes after appending, same as any other clone-doesn't-carry-forward property.

**Also found and removed:** a debug-looking purple stroke + tinted fill on the `_menuItem` `COMPONENT_SET`'s own container (the set itself, not a variant) — cosmetic/organizational only, invisible at runtime, but cleaned up to match every other component set in this file (no fill/stroke on the set itself). Also found the **Pressed** state alone carried a stray foreign-bound dark-olive stroke that Default/Hover/Disabled didn't have — same "one variant has X and the others don't, with no stated reason" smell flagged before on Tag; removed rather than tokenized, for consistency with the other 3 states.

**Menu (assembled container):** fill → `background/surface/raised`, border → `border/default`, radius → `border/radius/md` (8px) — all matching this file's established "menus, dropdowns" use-case tokens (same ones "pagination dropdown" and Pagination Dropdown Results already use). Removed the stray empty text node, swapped its 4 item instances onto the corrected `State=Default, Size=Large` variant.

**Menu Divider (new):** a plain horizontal rule, `border/default`, wrapped in an auto-layout frame with `spacing/padding/100` (4px) top/bottom breathing room, matching the item's own width (144px, Large tier). Built as a real `COMPONENT` (not a loose frame) so it's a first-class, insertable piece of the system.

Final audit: 0 issues across all 12 Menu Item variants + Menu + Divider (colors, spacing, radius, text styles, unexpected-remote check — icons excluded from the remote check per the explicit decision to keep them swappable/remote for now).

**Icon size fixed post-ship: 24px carried over from the source was oversized (2026-08-30).** User caught it visually and asked directly. Checked against Input — the one other component in the file with icon slots — and found a real, measurable gap: Input keeps its icon fixed at 16px across all its Sizes, filling 50%/40%/33% of its Small/Medium/Large row height (32/40/48px). Menu Item's icon at 24px was filling 86%/67%/60% of its own (smaller) row heights (28/36/40px) — oversized on an absolute basis and relative to Input's established "fixed icon size regardless of component Size" convention. Fixed by shrinking all 24 icon instances (2 per variant × 12 variants) from 24×24 to 16×16, matching Input's fixed value exactly rather than scaling per-size. No other geometry changed — auto-layout recentered everything automatically.

**Nudged back up to 20×20 after seeing it in real use (2026-08-30).** User's follow-up call after living with 16px for a bit: it read too small, particularly once a heavier real-world icon (see the icon-swap issue below) was in the slot instead of the source's thin "Acorn" glyph. Split the difference between the rejected 24px and the also-rejected 16px — resized all 24 icon instances to 20×20. Still clears every row height cleanly (Small 28px/Medium 36px/Large 40px rows, 71%/56%/50% fill respectively) with no clipping. Fixed-size-regardless-of-Size-variant convention unchanged, only the fixed value moved.

**Icon-swap gotcha found via a real user test: instance-swap can silently blank an icon's color.** User swapped Profile's leading icon to `light/user-group` (a different remote icon family than the default "Acorn") and it rendered as an empty box. Traced it: the `light/user-group` master component genuinely has a colored stroke — the problem was that Figma's instance-swap tried to carry the old icon's per-instance overrides onto the new icon's differently-shaped vector tree, and the color didn't survive the remap, leaving the swapped-in vector's stroke overridden to empty even though its master isn't blank. Fixed the one instance by rebinding its vector's stroke directly to `content/default`. **This isn't a one-off — it's a real, recurring limitation of the instance-swap decision**: because there's no single, consistently-pre-tokenized local icon library backing these slots, swapping to any icon from a different family than the current default may need its color manually re-set afterward. Flagged to the user as the same "no confirmed local icon library yet" blocker already on record for Input/Alert, now visible in practice rather than theoretical.

**New example: "Menu — Example (With Divider)" (2026-08-30).** User asked to see the Divider actually in use. Built a real `COMPONENT` (not just a loose frame) by cloning the base `Menu` container, keeping 2 of its 4 default items relabeled "Profile"/"Settings" (via `setProperties` on the `Label` component property), removing the other 2, then appending a `Menu Divider` instance followed by one more `Menu Item` instance labeled "Log out" — demonstrating the documented use case (grouping a destructive-feeling action apart from the rest).

**Live-collaborator edit noticed mid-build, same risk class as the Typography incident earlier this session.** The original outer "Menu" wrapper frame (`5071:4985`, a plain organizational frame with no real function) was gone by the time this task started, and an untouched `Menu` instance (`5147:5112`) had appeared directly on the page that I never created — someone was editing the file live. Nothing of substance was lost (the wrapper held no content of its own), so this was just re-discovered via a stale-ID error and worked around; the unexplained instance was left exactly as found rather than touched or removed.

**Spacing re-check against the documented role table, not just the raw scale (2026-08-30).** User asked directly whether gaps/padding matched "our spacing guide" — checked the actual "Which token for which layout role" table on the `Spacing & layout guide` page (not just the 100–800 raw scale) and found real deviations: Menu Item's icon-to-label gap had been matched to Pagination Menu Item's own (8–12px) instead of the guide's explicitly named row for this exact case, **"Inline rhythm — icon + label" → `spacing/gap/100` (4px)**; Menu Item's own internal padding used `spacing/padding/200` (8px) instead of the guide's **"Component internal padding — button, input, tag" → `spacing/padding/300` (12px)**; the Menu container's item-stack gap used `spacing/gap/100` (4px) instead of **"Row rhythm — stacked list/form rows" → `spacing/gap/200` (8px)**. Fixed all three across all 12 Menu Item variants + the Menu container; verified no clipping/overflow via screenshot at all 3 sizes and on both the base Menu and the new Divider example.

**Same audit extended to Pagination Menu Item, since it was the reference point for the original (wrong) values — found it has the identical drift, plus a real independent bug.** Icon-label gap was `spacing/gap/200` (8px) at Small/Medium, and at Large was bound to **`size/4`, a raw global primitive, not a semantic token at all**. Padding was `spacing/padding/200` (8px) everywhere. And — found only by checking every variant individually rather than trusting one sample — **`State=Hover, Size=Medium` and `State=Pressed, Size=Medium` had both their padding *and* gap bound to `border/radius/md`**, a radius token, wrong property family entirely. Invisible today only because `border/radius/md` and `spacing/padding/200` happen to both resolve to 8px — exactly the kind of bug that silently breaks the moment either token is retuned independently, same class as Tag's raw-global-color mistake earlier this session. Also found **Pagination Dropdown Results' row-stacking gap was completely unbound (`itemSpacing: 0`)** — rows were touching with no gap at all, should be `spacing/gap/200` (8px) per "Row rhythm." Fixed all of it: all 12 Pagination Menu Item variants → `spacing/gap/100` (gap) + `spacing/padding/300` (padding), all 3 Pagination Dropdown Results variants → `spacing/gap/200`. Verified via screenshot: no clipping, checkmark alignment intact, rows now have real breathing room instead of touching.

### Page cleanup: `-> Page pagination` was visually scattered after repeated resizes this session (2026-08-30)

User flagged the whole page looked disorganized.
