Root cause: this page's 6 component groups (Pagination Control, Pagination Dropdown trigger, Pagination Menu Item, Pagination Dropdown Results, Table Pagination, and the never-before-touched **Pagination Page** numbered-page component) had each been resized/repositioned multiple times over the session (Menu Item gaining Pressed states and growing, Pagination Control quadrupling from 176×240 to 720×288, icon-size passes, spacing fixes) — but the ~20 loose caption-label text frames scattered around them (`Small`/`Medium`/`Large` row labels, `Default`/`Hover`/`Active` column headers) were never moved along with them, leaving them floating disconnected from what they used to label. Several were also just factually stale by this point — one still read "Default/Hover/**Inactive**" for Pagination Control, which has been `Default/Hover/Pressed/Disabled` for a while now.

**Decision: didn't try to forensically reposition ~20 already-stale, ambiguously-matched micro-labels back onto their components.** Several were duplicated, one set was orphaned with no clear owner at all, and reconstructing exact original pairing from position alone was unreliable. Removed all 20 and replaced them with one clean, correctly-worded title above each of the 6 groups instead (Haffer SemiBold 20px, `content/default`) — simpler, unambiguous, and nothing lost that wasn't already broken.

**Layout:** all 6 groups stacked in one left-aligned vertical column, gap between groups = `spacing/gap/700` (32px) — matches the guide's own "Major region gap — top-level sections in a page/panel" role exactly, which this literally is. Section resized to fit (800×2498). Re-verify this page's layout after any future resize of one of these 6 groups — the same drift will recur if a component grows without the page being re-run through this same cleanup.

## Definition of Done — process for auditing/completing any component

Follow this to get a component to the same bar as Button, with minimal round-trips:

1. **Read-only discovery first.** `get_metadata` on the component set, `get_variable_defs` on one instance, then a `use_figma` script that dumps every variant's name/x/y/fills/strokes/effects with bound-variable names resolved (not raw IDs) and full alias chains resolved to final hex/rgba. Cross-reference against the token guide's stated token for that component.
2. **Present a gap-analysis table before mutating anything** — wrong tokens, broken/unbound aliases, missing state variants, duplicate-named nodes. Flag any place where the doc and an already-polished part of the file (e.g. the Small size, usually built first) disagree — ask which wins rather than assuming the doc does.
3. **Fix existing bindings in size/type batches**, not all 40+ nodes in one script. Checkpoint with `get_screenshot` after each batch.
4. **Create missing variants by cloning the same-type/size Default variant** (preserves correct dimensions/spacing/radius automatically), then only touch what differs (fill/stroke/effects/text color).
5. **Final automated audit before declaring done** — a script that flags: any null/broken paint, any `remote: true` variable, any variable name starting with `button/` (or whatever this file's legacy-collection prefix is), and confirms every Focus variant has a non-empty `effects` array. Report the exact issue count (0, ideally) rather than asserting correctness from memory.
6. **Screenshot both the full component set and any Focus-state node in isolation** to visually confirm rings/contrast — but see gotcha below about isolated-node screenshots clipping overflow effects.

## Known gotchas (so you don't rediscover them)

- **`node.clone()` on a variant `COMPONENT` inside a `COMPONENT_SET` does NOT keep the clone in the set** — Figma drops it onto the page. Always explicitly `componentSet.appendChild(clone)` after cloning, then re-set `x`/`y` (coordinates reset relative to the new parent).
- **Resizing a `COMPONENT_SET`/frame after adding variants can trigger one stale/black render** on the very next `get_screenshot` call. Verify the underlying node data first (fills/effects on the actual nodes) before assuming it's a real bug — a second `get_screenshot` call usually resolves it.
- **An isolated `get_screenshot` on a single small node clips overflow effects** (e.g. a 2px-spread focus ring) to the node's own bounding box — it won't show. Screenshot the parent component set (with surrounding margin) to actually see ring/shadow effects.
- **`get_screenshot` can render a persistent visual glitch (garbled/overlapping text at one specific spot) that is NOT a real file problem.** Seen when adding new variant rows (Danger, Quaternary) to Button/Action — the same row glitched identically across 4 separate screenshot calls, including with `contentsOnly:true`. Verified via `get_metadata` and direct node-structure inspection (children, positions, counts) that the underlying data was completely clean each time. Don't trust a garbled screenshot region on its own — cross-check with metadata/node inspection before concluding there's a real overlap or duplicate, and if data checks out clean, ask the user to confirm in their live Figma desktop app rather than burning more calls re-screenshotting.
- **Adding a new value to an existing variant `Type` property can silently reflow other, already-positioned rows** (seen: appending a `Danger`/`Quaternary` type shifted pre-existing Medium/Large rows down by a constant offset, even though the component set's `layoutMode` is `NONE`). After adding a new Type/Size/State value to a component set, re-verify ALL variant positions via metadata (not just the ones you just touched) before screenshotting — don't assume untouched rows stayed put.
- **The icon glyph in Button/Icon is a stroke, not a fill** — `container.children[0].children[0]` (instance → Vector), rebind `strokes`, not `fills`.
- **`use_figma` mutations are live** against whatever the Figma desktop app has open — not a preview/dry-run.
- Never parallelize `use_figma` calls; run sequentially even on the same page.

## Button/Icon — Shape property added (2026-08-16)

Added a `Shape` variant property: `Round` (existing — `border/radius/lg` on all four corners) and `Square` (new — `border/radius/sm`, 4px, on all four corners). Every existing variant got `Shape=Round` appended to its name; then all 75 were cloned to create the `Square` set. **Total: 150 variants** (5 types × 3 sizes × 5 states × 2 shapes). Positions were fully rewritten into a clean grid organized as: Shape block (Round, then Square, offset by 1300px) → Size band within each shape (Small/Medium/Large, generously gapped) → Type row (`Primary, Secondary, Tertiary, Ghost, Danger` order — **remember Ghost is a real type now, include it in any `typeOrder` array or it silently gets skipped**) → State column (uniform x-position across all sizes for clean vertical alignment: Default=40, Hover=140, Inactive=240, Pressed=340, Focus=440).

## Major bug found and fixed: paint `opacity` doesn't auto-sync with a bound variable's embedded alpha

**This is the most important technical gotcha from today — likely to recur with any token whose color value has alpha < 1** (e.g. `background/action/secondary-default`, which is `color/darken/100` at 5% alpha in Light mode but a fully-opaque charcoal in Dark mode).

**Symptom:** Tertiary and Danger icon buttons' Inactive state rendered as a solid black circle with a completely invisible icon, while Primary's Inactive (same tokens) rendered correctly as a faint, barely-visible grey circle.

**Root cause:** a Paint object has two separate alpha channels — the color's own `a` component (which can be baked into a variable's value, like the 5% in `color/darken/100`) and the Paint's own top-level `opacity` field. `figma.variables.setBoundVariableForPaint(paint, 'color', variable)` binds the *color* but does **not** reliably keep the paint's `opacity` field in sync with the variable's alpha — and critically, the paint's `opacity` appears to get **frozen at whatever value was live at bind-time**, while the RGB channel stays dynamically live-bound. Primary's Inactive fill was bound early in the session (opacity correctly baked as `0.05`); Tertiary/Danger's were bound later via clone+rebind helper scripts that never explicitly set `opacity`, defaulting to `1` (fully opaque) — producing a solid, near-black circle instead of a barely-there 5% tint.

Compounding factor: this file's "Button" section (`4541:673`) has an **explicit variable-mode override** pinning the `semantic` collection to **Dark** mode (`node.explicitVariableModes`) — independent of whatever the rest of the file or the global theme toggle shows. This wasn't something any of my scripts set; it was either already there or set directly by the user. Screenshots and live rendering of anything inside that section reflect this pinned Dark mode, not necessarily "Light" as I sometimes assumed earlier in the session — re-check `node.explicitVariableModes` up the ancestor chain before trusting an assumed mode when debugging a color that looks wrong.

**Fix applied:** for any paint bound to a variable with alpha < 1, don't just call `setBoundVariableForPaint` and move on — verify the resulting `paint.opacity` afterward, or directly copy the fill array from a known-good reference node (`fills = [...knownGoodNode.fills]`) rather than reconstructing via the helper. Applied this to fix Danger and Tertiary's Inactive states across all sizes and both new Shapes.

**Checklist for next time this kind of bug is suspected:**
1. Compare the exact resolved color **and opacity** between a working node and a suspect node with `JSON.stringify(node.fills)` — don't just check the bound variable ID, check the literal paint object.
2. If a token's value has embedded alpha < 1, and the same-looking fill on two different nodes has different `paint.opacity`, that's the bug — not a mode issue, not a rendering artifact.
3. Fix by copying the working paint object directly, not by re-calling the binding helper (which reproduces the bug).
4. Don't confuse this with the *separate*, real screenshot-rendering-artifact gotcha above (garbled/missing content on large captures) — that one has no underlying data difference between "before" and "after" a re-screenshot; this one has a real, verifiable data difference (`opacity` field) between working and broken nodes.

## Button/Icon — resized to match Button/Action's scale (2026-08-16)

Icon buttons were running 8px smaller than the text buttons at every tier (24/32/40 vs Button/Action's 32/40/48). Resized all 150 variants to **Small=32, Medium=40, Large=48**, matching Button/Action exactly.

**Icon glyph scaling isn't linear** — the nested `light/plus` instance and its Vector don't scale 1:1 with the container (Medium/Large use a 0.5 icon-to-container ratio, Small uses ~0.667 so icons stay legible at small sizes — this was already true before the resize, not something introduced today). Since each new size exactly equals the *previous* tier's old absolute size, the fix reused each old tier's exact icon/vector proportions shifted up one level, and extrapolated a new Large from the existing 0.5 ratio pattern:
- New Small (32px container) ← old Medium's icon proportions (instance=16, inset=8, vec=10.5 @2.75 offset)
- New Medium (40px) ← old Large's proportions (instance=20, inset=10, vec=13.125 @3.4375)
- New Large (48px) ← extrapolated (instance=24, inset=12, vec=15.75 @4.125)

**Corner radius for Round shape was also rebound** from `border/radius/lg` (20px, fixed) to `border/radius/xl` (32px, fixed) — 20px would no longer produce a full circle at the new 48px Large (needs ≥24px radius), and overshooting a radius never hurts in Figma (it just caps at a perfect circle/pill), so `radius/xl` keeps every size fully round with margin to spare for any future size bump too. Square shape's `border/radius/sm` (4px) was untouched — still looks like a small rounded square regardless of container size.

**Gotcha: instance child position (`x`/`y`) cannot be set directly in the same call as its parent's resize, in some cases at all.** Setting `instance.x = ...` on the nested `light/plus` instance threw `"This property cannot be overridden in an instance: relative-transform"` — even in a separate, later `use_figma` call. Root cause: the instance has `constraints: {horizontal: 'MIN', vertical: 'MIN'}` — Figma's own constraint-resolution system, triggered automatically by calling `instance.resize(w, h)`, repositions it as a side effect, and manually overriding `x`/`y` afterward conflicts with that. **The fix was to just not set `x`/`y` at all** — call `resize()` on the instance (and the Vector inside, whose constraint is `SCALE`) and let Figma's constraint system auto-position it. It landed at the exact symmetric center inset intended, every time, across all 150 nodes (verified: 0 mismatches). **Lesson: if `set_x`/`set_y` on a nested instance throws "cannot be overridden", stop trying to force it — resize the instance/its children instead and verify the resulting position; constraint-driven auto-positioning is likely already doing the right thing.**

## Avatar / Avatar Group — retokened from foreign libraries (2026-08-16)

New component (`4627:1708` Avatar, `4627:1717` Avatar Group, `4627:1730` Assets — GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket icons + `_Git`/`_Icon` badge components) arrived **not themed to Gist Foundations at all** — every color was bound to remote variables from an unrelated external library called "Geist" (Vercel's design system — coincidentally similar name, unrelated file), plus a `brand-green/default` and a `border/selected` that both also turned out to be remote/foreign despite plausible-sounding names. **Lesson: a token name matching our own naming convention doesn't mean it's actually one of our local tokens — always check `variable.remote` before trusting a name.**

**Mapping applied** (preserving the original visual design, just retargeting to our tokens):
- Avatar circle background (`geist background`, #000) → `background/surface/inverse`
- Avatar circle border (`accents 2`, #333) → `border/default`
- `_Icon`/`_Git` badge wrapper background → `background/surface/inverse` (consistency with the avatar); border (`accents 3`, #444 — slightly lighter than accents 2) → `border/strong` (preserves the original's relative emphasis: badge border was already a touch more visible than the avatar's own border)
- "+N" overflow badge: was `brand-green/default` fill + foreign `border/selected` — **confirmed with the user this was not our own brand green**, just another leftover foreign binding. Changed to match the regular avatars (`surface/inverse` fill, `border/default` stroke, `content/inverse` text) for visual consistency within the group, per the user's choice.
- Placeholder icon contrast: the original accents-3-on-black badge border was very low contrast; **user explicitly said to use whatever the token guide recommends** rather than preserve the low-contrast original — this informed picking a real semantic token (`border/strong`) rather than trying to reproduce the muddy original tone.
- The actual GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket **logo vector paths** (the literal brand marks, nested inside `_GitHub`/`_GitLab`/`_Bitbucket`) were bound to `geist background`/`geist foreground` too — these were **unbound to literal black/white** rather than retargeted to a Gist token. Third-party platform logos are brand marks belonging to GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket, not us — they shouldn't be theme-dependent on *any* design system's semantic tokens, ours included (same principle as the guide's `brand/*` rule, applied to someone else's brand this time).
- Component-set/section canvas backdrops (the grey backing behind the whole "Avatar", "Avatar Group", "Assets" groupings in the Figma file — cosmetic organizational chrome, not real shipped UI) were also cleaned up to `background/surface/sunken`, just for overall file hygiene — low priority if this doc is stale by the time anyone reads it, but harmless either way.

Verified via a full recursive tree walk (not just the top-level components) — 0 remaining remote-variable references. **When auditing a new component for "is it themed to our DS," always do a full recursive walk of every descendant, not just the top-level component fills** — this file's issue was buried several levels deep in nested instances (badge → logo instance → clip-path group → vector).

**Follow-up catch: typography was missed on the first pass.** The initial audit only checked color bindings — the "+4" badge text was still literally set to `Geist SemiBold` (unbound, not even aliased to anything), not our own `Haffer`. **Going forward, "is this themed to our DS" audits must holistically check colors AND typography (and really everything else — spacing, effects, radii) in the same pass, not color-only** — the user explicitly asked for this to be standing practice, not a one-off fix. Fixed by setting `fontName = {family: 'Haffer', style: 'SemiBold'}` and binding `fontFamily` to `font/family/default` (both `font/family/default` and `font/family/accent` resolve to `Haffer` — same physical font, different semantic slots for future flexibility; `font/weight/Semibold` conveniently already resolves to the string `"SemiBold"`, an exact match for the existing style, so no weight change was needed — just the family).

**Gotcha:** our `font/weight/*` tokens are **STRING type** (e.g. `font/weight/Semibold` = the literal text `"SemiBold"`), not bindable to a text node's `fontWeight` property — attempting `setBoundVariable('fontWeight', ...)` throws `variable of resolved type 'STRING' cannot be bound to 'fontWeight'`, and since `use_figma` scripts are atomic, this rolled back the *entire* script including the `fontFamily` bind and `fontName` set that came before it in the same call. Fix: don't bind `fontWeight` at all — setting `fontName.style` directly (e.g. `'SemiBold'`) is sufficient; only bind `fontFamily` as a variable.

## Checkbox / Option-Checkbox — retokened + Indeterminate state added (2026-08-16)

Same "not themed to our DS" problem as Avatar, but this time it also surfaced **real semantic-correctness bugs**, not just remote-vs-local swaps — some tokens were bound to the *wrong category* of role entirely, not just a foreign library:

| State | Element | Was | Fixed to | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Default | border | `content/loading` | `border/interactive` | A **text/loading** token used as a border color; guide names checkboxes explicitly under `border/interactive` ("Form control borders at rest... checkboxes") |
| Hover | fill | `background/interactive/selected` (remote) | `background/interactive/hover` | Guide explicitly says: "For chips/checkboxes use `action`, not [`interactive/selected`]" — this was doubly wrong (foreign token, and the wrong semantic role even if it had been local) |
| Hover | border | `content/loading` | `border/interactive` | Same bug as Default |
| Pressed (checked) | fill | `content/default` | `background/action/primary-default` | A **text-color** token used as a fill; guide explicitly lists "checked checkboxes" under `action/primary` |
| Disabled | border | `border/interactive-strong` | `border/interactive-disabled` | Backwards — `-strong` is for *more* emphasis (e.g. secondary button outline), the opposite of what a disabled control needs |

Checkmark itself (`content/inverse`) was already correct — no change needed there.

**Fonts:** caption labels ("Default/Hover/Selected/Disabled") were unbound `Inter Semi Bold` → fixed to bound `Haffer SemiBold` (`font/family/default`). The "Content" option-label text was already the *correct* family (`Haffer Medium`) but completely unbound to any token — bound it properly rather than leaving it as a lucky-coincidence literal value. **`Haffer`'s exact available style strings** (verified via `listAvailableFontsAsync`, not guessed): `Bold`, `Bold Italic`, `Medium`, `Medium Italic`, `Regular`, `Regular Italic`, `SemiBold`, `SemiBold Italic` — no space in "SemiBold", unlike Inter's "Semi Bold".

**Gotcha: a text node's `fontFamily`/`fontWeight` etc. in `node.boundVariables` are stored as an *array* of range-bound variables, not a single object.** My first audit pass checked `boundVariables.fontFamily.id` and got false "UNBOUND" positives on nodes that were actually correctly bound — the real shape is `boundVariables.fontFamily[0].id` (arrays exist because text can have mixed styling across character ranges, even when, as here, the whole run is one style). Same caution applies to `fills` when checking a paint — that one really is a plain array of paint objects, but don't assume every per-property `boundVariables` entry has the same shape without checking.

**Indeterminate state added** (design reference grabbed from a separate node, then re-themed — not copied as-is): cloned the checked/Pressed state (same `background/action/primary-default` fill, same box structure), removed the checkmark instance, and added a simple new minus-bar rectangle (10×1.5px, corner radius 0.75, centered in the 18×18 box) bound to `content/inverse` — same color role as the checkmark, just a different glyph. Added to **both** the standalone `Checkbox` component set (new 5th column, frame resized 259→326px, matching caption label added) **and** `Option/Checkbox` (new row, height resized 200→245) — the latter's nested `Checkbox` instance already exposed the new state as a normal variant property, so switching it was a one-line `setProperties({ State: 'Indeterminate' })` rather than rebuilding the swap manually.

## Checkbox / Option-Checkbox — labels, Pressed state, and layout gotchas (2026-08-16, same day as retokening)

Follow-up work after the initial retokening above. The user completed a reference edit on `Option/Checkbox`'s `State=Default` themselves (adding a "Label" heading above the checkbox+text row, plus two new boolean component properties — `labelState` and `textState`, both default `true`) and asked me to replicate it to every other state.

**How the boolean wiring actually works** (useful for any future component with similar show/hide toggles): the properties are defined once at the **component SET** level (`labelState#4627:0`, `textState#4627:6` — the `#id` suffix is the property's unique key, stays constant across all variants). Individual nodes opt into being controlled by a boolean via `node.componentPropertyReferences = { visible: 'labelState#4627:0' }` — this is a **plain writable property**, confirmed by reading it back after assignment (it's not always documented as writable, verify empirically rather than assuming). **Cloning a node does NOT carry over its `componentPropertyReferences`** — every clone had to have this re-applied explicitly, even though clone() preserves almost everything else about a node.

**Auto-layout gotcha (the actual visual bug this caused):** naively reparenting the existing Checkbox+Cell into a new manually-positioned wrapper frame (no auto-layout, just `fills:[]` and explicit x/y) produced a completely broken visual — "Label" and the checkbox+text rendered **on one line** instead of stacked, because the *outer* frame (`Frame 2147225238`) still had its **old** `layoutMode: 'HORIZONTAL'` from before the edit (auto-layout overrides manual child x/y entirely once active). Default's actual structure: outer frame `layoutMode: 'VERTICAL'`, `itemSpacing: 8`, `counterAxisSizingMode: 'FIXED'`, `counterAxisAlignItems: 'MIN'` (critical — without this the label centers instead of left-aligning); wrapper frame `layoutMode: 'HORIZONTAL'`, `itemSpacing: 8`, both axes `AUTO` (hug). **Lesson: when replicating a structural change onto sibling variants, diff every auto-layout property (`layoutMode`, `itemSpacing`, sizing modes, `counterAxisAlignItems`) between the reference and the target — don't assume "reparent + manual x/y" is enough just because the reference is visually correct. A node that predates the edit will keep its old layout mode unless you explicitly change it.**

**Also added: a genuine "Pressed" state, plus a naming fix.** The user asked whether Checkbox should have a real Pressed (mousedown/active) state matching how Button/Action distinguishes Hover from Pressed everywhere else — yes, and it surfaced that the existing "checked" variant was **mislabeled `State=Pressed`** (its own caption already read "Selected", inconsistent with the variant name). Fixed by:
1. Renaming the checked variant `State=Pressed` → `State=Selected` (in both `Checkbox` and `Option/Checkbox`). Confirmed empirically that Figma auto-updates any instance's `State` property value to match after a source variant rename — no manual instance fixup needed.
2. Adding a real new `State=Pressed`: cloned from Hover (same unchecked, outlined structure), fill rebound to `background/interactive/pressed` (a token that already existed in the file, clearly intended for exactly this), border stays `border/interactive` matching Hover/Default.
3. Replicated to `Option/Checkbox` too — its own row structure, plus switching its nested `Checkbox` instance's `State` property to `'Pressed'` via `setProperties()`.

**Current final state:** both `Checkbox` and `Option/Checkbox` have 6 states each: `Default, Hover, Pressed, Selected, Disabled, Indeterminate` — verified identical naming on both sets, 0 remaining token/font issues, all 6 `Option/Checkbox` rows correctly wired to `labelState`/`textState`, no layout overlaps (consistent 52px row height, 16px gaps, matching Default's spacing exactly).

## Checkbox / Option-Checkbox — layering fix to match user's restructured Default (2026-08-16, same day)

User restructured `State=Default` again after the above: removed the intermediate `Frame 2147225238` wrapper entirely, promoting its children (`Content` label + `Frame 2147225239`) to be **direct children of the component itself**, with the component's own `layoutMode`/`itemSpacing`(10, not 8)/sizing properties absorbing what the wrapper used to do. They also (at some earlier point, not part of this specific ask) detached `Cell` from its `Type=Title` instance into a plain `FRAME` containing `Content` + a `light/info` icon directly — this was already consistent across all 6 states before this fix, not something introduced by this change.

Replicated the wrapper-removal to Hover/Pressed/Selected/Disabled/Indeterminate: `appendChild` each wrapper's children directly onto the state component, `.remove()` the now-empty wrapper, then copy Default's exact layout properties (`VERTICAL`, `itemSpacing: 10`, both sizing modes `AUTO`, `counterAxisAlignItems: 'MIN'`) onto each component.

**Note (not a bug, just flagging):** `Cell`'s `textState` property reference is **gone on Default too**, not just the other states — lost when Cell got detached from its instance at some point. I matched every state to Default exactly (per "fix this for the rest"), so none of the 6 currently have working `textState` toggling — only `labelState` (on the "Label" heading) still works. If you want the Cell/text visibility toggle back, that needs to be re-added intentionally (bind `Cell.componentPropertyReferences = {visible: 'textState#4627:6'}` again) — I didn't do this unprompted since Default itself doesn't have it, and matching Default was the actual ask.

**Also re-hit the known variant-reflow gotcha** (documented earlier for Button/Action) — after the wrapper removal, row spacing came out uneven (80px gap after Default, 68px for the rest) even though I never set those exact values, most likely Figma's own auto-arrange kicking in after a structural edit to one variant. Fixed with one more explicit, deterministic reposition pass (68px spacing throughout) rather than trusting whatever Figma's silent reflow produced.

## Input / Input Heading — retokened + redesigned Type semantics (2026-08-17)

Brought in from another file (same pattern as Avatar/Checkbox). **75 total variants** (`Input` component set: 72 = 3 Type × 4 Size × 6 State; `Input Heading` compound component: 3 sizes). Biggest, messiest import yet — 491 issues on first audit pass. Both are now at 0.

**Type semantics were wrong/meaningless as found, redefined per standard practice:** all three types (Primary/Secondary/Tertiary) rendered with a solid background fill (blue "selected," black "quaternary"-old-naming, pale blue "info") — clearly a bug, not a resting-input look, confirmed visually before touching anything. Asked the user what `Type` should mean; landed on the same 3-tier pattern Material/Ant/Chakra all converge on, matching the existing Button emphasis-scale logic:
- **Primary = Outlined** — `fills: []`, stroke `border/interactive` (guide names this exact use case: "Form control borders at rest. Text inputs, selects, checkboxes.")
- **Secondary = Filled** — fill `background/surface/sunken` (guide literally lists "Input wells" under this token's use case), no stroke at rest
- **Tertiary = Underlined** — `fills: []`, stroke on **bottom side only** via `strokeTopWeight/strokeLeftWeight/strokeRightWeight = 0`, `strokeBottomWeight = 1` (Figma per-side stroke, not a separate line element)

**State token map** (applies within each Type): Default/Filled use `border/interactive`; Hover/Active use `border/interactive-strong`; Inactive uses `border/interactive-disabled`; Warning uses `border/status-warning`. Text color: placeholder-not-yet-filled states (Default/Hover/Active/Warning) use `content/secondary`; Filled (has a real value) uses `content/default`; Inactive uses `content/disabled`. Active state additionally gets the same focus-ring `DROP_SHADOW` effect (bound to `border/focus`) used on Button's Focus state, plus a "Line 2" cursor-indicator element (stroke `content/default`) — skipped the ring on Tertiary to preserve its minimal character.

**Dead-weight removal** (per explicit user instruction to remove anything unnecessary): every variant carried 5 invisible, never-toggleable sub-elements — two `Button/Icon` instances, `light/sparkle-2/3`, `light/search`, and a `Favicon` instance — leftovers from a richer "address bar" component this Input never actually uses. **Verified via a script scanning all 72 variants that none were ever visible anywhere** before deleting 266+ nodes. The Expanded (textarea) size had the same dead elements one level deeper, nested inside `Buttons & Icons`/`Buttons` wrapper frames whose own `visible` flag (not just the leaf elements') was false — re-learn this lesson: **check the whole ancestor chain's visibility, not just the leaf node's own flag**, or a live-looking child under a hidden parent reads as a false positive. Also removed on Input Heading: a duplicate hidden `Input/Default/Desktop` preview, a hidden `Alert` section (icon+title+message+link+success-tag) with zero component-property wiring to ever show it, and a `Responsive` variant property that only ever had one possible value (`Desktop`) — confirmed via `componentPropertyDefinitions` before removing; renamed all 3 variants to drop the dead axis.

## Major new gotcha: text STYLES can silently govern font properties, defeating node-level fixes

**This will recur on any future component that uses named Figma text styles (not all of them do — Button/Checkbox/Avatar didn't hit this, Input did).** Symptom: `textNode.setBoundVariable('fontFamily', localVar)` reports success, `textNode.boundVariables.fontFamily` even reads back correctly *immediately after* — but a fresh audit call later still reports the font as remote/wrong, as if nothing happened.

**Root cause:** the text node has a `textStyleId` pointing to a named Text Style (check via `node.textStyleId` → `figma.getStyleByIdAsync(id)`). When a style governs a node, the node's own property is kept in sync *from the style*, not the other way around — a direct node-level `setBoundVariable` gets silently re-overwritten back to whatever the style dictates. **The fix has to happen at the right level:**
1. First check whether the *style itself* is already correctly bound (`getLocalTextStylesAsync()`, find by name, check `style.boundVariables.fontFamily.id` against `getLocalVariablesAsync()`) — if the style is already correct, node-level edits are fighting a losing battle against resync, not fixing a real problem.
2. If the style is correct but the node still reads wrong, the node's mirrored property is just stale — **force a resync by looking up the canonical style object fresh and reassigning `node.textStyleId = style.id`** (not `node.fills`/`setBoundVariable` directly).
3. **Self-reassignment of the node's own already-stored `textStyleId` value is unreliable** — tried `node.textStyleId = node.textStyleId` as a shortcut, it fixed some nodes and silently failed on others in the exact same batch. Always fetch the style fresh via `getLocalTextStylesAsync().find(s => s.name === '...')` and assign `style.id`, never the node's own potentially-stale copy of that id.
4. A style's own `boundVariables.fontFamily` is a **plain object** (`{id: ...}`), not array-wrapped like a text *node's* `boundVariables.fontFamily` (which is an array of range-bound entries, per the earlier Danger-button-era gotcha). Don't reuse the array-indexing check for styles — that alone produced a false "unbound" reading before this was sorted out.
5. The `remote` flag on a style/variable object returned by `getStyleByIdAsync`/`getVariableByIdAsync` **is not reliable** — it flatly contradicted the ground-truth "is this ID in `getLocalVariablesAsync()`'s list" check twice this session, on two unrelated objects. Always cross-check against the local list; never trust `.remote` alone.

## Input placeholder text — new content/placeholder token (2026-08-17)

User felt `content/secondary` (80% opacity — what the placeholder text was using) read as too dark for a placeholder, and created a new semantic token themselves: **`content/placeholder`** (Light: `color/darken/500` @ 50% opacity; Dark: `color/content/500` @ 50%). Computed contrast before applying it (habit worth keeping): passes comfortably in Dark mode (5.3 / 4.69) but **Light mode is below AA for normal text** — 3.73:1 (Primary/Tertiary, transparent-over-page) and 3.35:1 (Secondary, over `surface/sunken`). Found that 60% opacity would clear 4.5:1 with margin (5.09:1 on the tightest background) while still reading lighter than `content/secondary`. **User's explicit call: keep it at 50% as defined — they'll adjust the underlying primitive later themselves if they decide to.** Applied to the Default/Hover/Active/Warning states' `Value` text across all 72 Input variants (48 nodes) — Filled still correctly uses `content/default` (real typed value) and Inactive still uses `content/disabled`, neither touched.

**Do not "fix" this contrast gap unprompted if it comes up again** — it's a known, explicitly-accepted tradeoff, not an oversight. If the user later swaps the primitive, re-run the same contrast check against all 3 Type backgrounds (Primary/Tertiary transparent-over-page, Secondary over `surface/sunken`) in both modes before considering it resolved.

## Input — Leading/Trailing icon slots restored as real boolean capability (2026-08-21)

User caught a real regression from the dead-weight removal above: deleting the unused icon elements also deleted the *capability* to show a leading/trailing icon at all, not just unused cruft. Confirmed with the user: both slots (leading **and** trailing), across **all 3 Types and all 4 Sizes** (including Expanded), both new boolean properties defaulting to **off/false** — the ask was just that the toggle capability exists, not that icons show anywhere by default.

**What was built:** two `BOOLEAN` component properties on the `Input` set (`4713:2114`) — `Leading Icon` (key `Leading Icon#4734:0`) and `Trailing Icon` (key `Trailing Icon#4734:73`), both `defaultValue: false`. Every one of the 72 variants got two new elements: `Leading Icon` inserted as the **first** auto-layout child, `Trailing Icon` **appended last**, each 16×16, `layoutSizingHorizontal/Vertical = FIXED`, `componentPropertyReferences.visible` bound to the matching key. Icon shape is a placeholder — the `light/info` glyph, stroke bound to `content/secondary` (`VariableID:4509:717`) — swap to a more semantically correct icon (search for leading, clear/chevron for trailing) whenever that's prioritized; the slot mechanism doesn't depend on which glyph fills it. This worked cleanly because Input's variants already use real `HORIZONTAL` auto-layout (`itemSpacing: 4`), so text reflows automatically as icons toggle on/off — no manual repositioning needed. Verified visually across default/leading-only/trailing-only/both-on, including the Expanded (multi-line) size.

**New gotcha: `component.createInstance()` on a component whose defining page was never loaded in the current script produces a silently empty instance shell.** The `light/info` icon component (`4713:1863`) lives on a page context that these scripts never explicitly loaded via `setCurrentPageAsync`. Calling `.createInstance()` on it appeared to succeed (returned a valid node, id, name) and even looked fine when inspected *within the same script* right after creation — but a **fresh `use_figma` call re-fetching that same node by ID found zero children**, every time, across every batch. This is a step beyond the known "pages load incrementally" rule: it's not just that reads of an unloaded page's nodes come back empty — a *write* (an instance created from such a node) can come back empty too once the script context resets, even though the mutation is otherwise live in the file. Cloning an already-**instantiated**, on-loaded-page instance (`swapIcon = 4713:2054`, the Input Heading's own info icon, sitting on the same `-> Inputs` page as Input itself) had the identical symptom the first time — the fix that actually held was **cloning the inner `VECTOR` node itself** (`swapIcon.findOne(n => n.type === 'VECTOR')`), not the wrapping `INSTANCE`. A plain `VECTOR` clone persisted correctly across script boundaries every time; instance clones/creations sourced from that component did not, in this file, in this session. If a future icon-slot-style task needs true instance-swap flexibility (so users can pick any icon from the library via the properties panel, not just show/hide a fixed glyph), **budget time to first confirm the source component's page loads and instances survive a fresh re-fetch before building 70+ variants on top of the assumption** — don't take one successful prototype call as proof; verify with a second, separate `use_figma` call before scaling.

**Also re-confirmed the "local variable" lookup-by-name gotcha from the font-styles section above applies to color variables too, not just styles:** the very first prototype variant's icon stroke was bound to a decoy `content/secondary`-*named* variable from a linked library (`VariableID:7e2c...cf/4171:2373`, hash-prefixed = library-sourced) rather than this file's real local one (`VariableID:4509:717`, plain `4509:717` format) — both satisfied `allVars.find(v => v.name === 'content/secondary')` in different script runs. Confirmed via direct inspection that only one variable named `content/secondary` shows up in `getLocalVariablesAsync()` at any given moment, so this was a same-session non-determinism, not two permanently-coexisting locals — but it means **matching by name alone is not safe even against `getLocalVariablesAsync()`'s own results**; when a specific, already-correct binding exists elsewhere in the file (Input Heading's icon, in this case), copy that exact ID rather than re-deriving by name.

## Bug found and fixed: 11 Input variants had a stale black border despite correct variable binding (2026-08-21)

User spotted it visually: Medium/Primary/Default's border looked "too dark" next to Secondary/Tertiary. Root cause was **not** a rendering artifact — `variant.strokes[0].boundVariables.color.id` correctly pointed to `border/interactive` on all 11 affected variants, but the paint's own raw `color` field was still hardcoded pure black (`{0,0,0}`) from some earlier state, predating the Type-semantics redesign in the section above. Figma's real canvas render honors that stale raw value, not just the binding — so the bug was fully visible, not cosmetic-in-the-API-only.

**Affected:** Primary/Medium/Default, Primary/Large/Default, Primary/Expanded/Default, plus the **Filled** state across Primary/Tertiary × Small/Medium/Large/Expanded (11 total, all sharing the one root-stroke paint). Found by resolving `border/interactive`'s full alias chain to its real Light-mode RGB (`border/interactive` → `color/neutral/50` → `{0.839, 0.855, 0.890}`) and comparing every bound stroke/fill's raw color against that resolved value across all 72 variants, recursively — this should be a standard step in the Definition-of-Done audit going forward, not just checking that `boundVariables` exists.

**The real gotcha, sharper than the earlier "opacity doesn't auto-sync" one: `figma.variables.setBoundVariableForPaint(paint, 'color', variable)` does NOT resolve or correct the paint's raw `color` field — it only attaches the binding metadata on top of whatever raw color you hand it.** First fix attempt called it on the existing (stale-black) paint object and got back a paint that was still black, still "correctly bound." The actual fix: resolve the variable's real value yourself first, seed `color` on the paint object *before* passing it to `setBoundVariableForPaint` (i.e. `{...oldPaint, color: resolvedColor}`), then bind. Same manual walk of `valuesByMode`/alias-chains as the font-style gotcha above, just applied to paints instead of styles.

## Input — Active state moved off `border/interactive-strong` onto the dedicated `border/interactive-pressed` token (2026-08-21)

User caught that Active/Selected didn't feel visually distinct from Hover — both were bound to the literal same token (`border/interactive-strong`), differing only by a barely-perceptible 0-blur/2-spread focus ring. Resolving the actual chains confirmed the token hierarchy was muddled: `border/interactive` (Default) = neutral/50 @ 100% opaque light gray-blue; `border/interactive-strong` (Hover) = darken/200 @ **10%** alpha (very faint tint); `border/interactive-disabled` (Inactive) = darken/400 @ **40%** alpha — meaning **disabled read darker/more prominent than "strong,"** backwards from what the naming implies. No variable had a `description` set to pin down intended usage, and the only precedent for `border/interactive-strong` from the token guide was Button/Secondary's stroke — Input's Hover/Active pairing was never a deliberate decision.

Found `border/interactive-pressed` (darken/1000 @ **100%**, solid near-black) sitting completely unused in the semantic collection — a real, purpose-built token following the same `*-pressed` pattern already used elsewhere (`background/action/primary-pressed`, `-secondary-pressed`, etc. for the most-emphasized interaction state). Switched all 12 Active variants (3 Types × 4 Sizes: Small/Medium/Large/Expanded) from `border/interactive-strong` to `border/interactive-pressed`, using the seed-raw-color-before-binding pattern from the bug above. Result: a clean, correct hierarchy — Default (light, opaque) → Hover (faint tint) → Inactive (muted-but-present) → Active (solid, unmistakable) — confirmed via side-by-side screenshot of Hover/Active/Inactive at Primary/Medium.

**Scope note:** this was Input-only. Button/Checkbox/other components using `border/interactive-strong` for their own Hover states were not touched or re-evaluated — if the same "Active feels like Hover" complaint comes up elsewhere, don't assume this fix generalizes; check what tokens that component's Active/Pressed state actually uses first.

## Input — full final QA pass, 0 issues (2026-08-21)

Ran the complete Definition-of-Done audit after the icon-slot build and the two border-token fixes above, specifically extended to catch the exact class of bug found earlier (correctly *bound* but stale *raw* color) rather than just checking bindings exist:

1. **Colors/effects** — recursive walk of every fill, stroke, and shadow effect across all 72 variants: resolved every bound variable's **full alias chain to its actual Light-mode RGBA**, compared against the paint/effect's raw `color` (not just checked that `boundVariables` was set), and flagged any non-local/remote variable. **0 issues.**
2. **Typography** — every `TEXT` node's `textStyleId`/`fontName`, style-level font bindings checked against local variables, flagged anything outside `Haffer`/`Inter`. **0 issues.**
3. **Geometry** — corner radius (Tertiary confirmed still 0 on all sides), per-side stroke weights (Tertiary confirmed still bottom-only; Primary/Secondary confirmed uniform), and padding/spacing/height consistency across all 4 sizes. **0 issues.**
4. **Structural** — icon slot wiring re-confirmed (both booleans still `BOOLEAN`/`defaultValue: false`, every variant still has exactly one correctly-bound Leading/Trailing icon), scanned for any lingering `component/`-collection (legacy) bindings. **0 issues.** The 4 "Active variants missing focus ring effect" the script initially flagged are **expected, not bugs** — Tertiary's Active deliberately skips the ring per the original Type-redesign decision to preserve its minimal underline character.
5. **Visual** — full component-set screenshot plus targeted re-screenshots of every previously-buggy variant (the 11 stale-black-border ones, the 12 re-tokened Active ones) at the Expanded size specifically, since that size is structurally most different from the single-line sizes. All confirmed visually correct — no stale black borders, Active reads as a clearly stronger/darker state than Hover across all 3 Types including Tertiary's bottom-only stroke.

**Net: Input is at 0 known issues** across token binding, raw-value accuracy, typography, geometry, and structure. If a future "looks off" report comes in, re-run this exact 4-part script pattern rather than re-auditing from scratch — the alias-chain-resolution-vs-raw-color check (part 1) is the one that actually catches what eyeballing a screenshot can miss.

## Typography — full audit pass, line-height standardization, and Default/Tight activated (2026-08-21)

Prompted by colleague feedback (Linda/Jane) on an incomplete first-pass typography section, ran a full pass over all 54 local text styles (families: `Accent/Tight`, `Accent/Comfy`, `Default/Tight`, `Default/Comfy` × Desktop/Mobile × numeric steps 100–900, Accent going higher since it's for headings).

**Built two new sections directly on the Typography page** (per explicit instruction: documentation lives in Figma itself, not a separate doc):
- **"Type Scale — Semantic Mapping"** — H1–H6, Body, Caption, and now **Dense/Data** rows, each self-demonstrating (rendered in its own real bound style, both Desktop and Mobile), plus an accessibility callout (one H1 per page, never skip levels, heading level ≠ visual size, HTML/WCAG stops at H6). Also includes a "Compact ladder" mini-example (Compact H1 = normal H2's size, Compact H2 = normal H3's size, Compact H3 = normal H4's size) — **first attempt at this got it wrong**: shrinking only H1 in isolation (to H3's size) while H2 stayed at its normal, bigger size would make H2 visually outsize H1, an inverted hierarchy. The real rule, caught by the user: if a screen needs a smaller heading scale, shift the *entire* H1–H6 ladder down together by the same number of steps — never compact one level in isolation.
- **"Example Usage — Article Page"** — a full mock article (eyebrow → H1 → byline → intro → H2 → body → H3 → body → H4 → **a dense data table**) so the mapping isn't just isolated swatches.
- Fixed the pre-existing "Section 1" swatch grid in place: one node (`Accent/Tight/700`) was completely unbound (foreign Roboto font, wrong size), the "Desktop"/"Mobile" column headers were mislabeled (columns actually held Tight/Comfy content, not a Desktop/Mobile split), and all 18 sample labels were stale strings that didn't match their actual bound style. **First fix attempt got this wrong too** — labeled nodes based on assumed intent instead of resolving each node's actual bound `textStyleId` first; caught and fixed properly on a second pass. Lesson: same discipline as the raw-color bug — verify the *actual* bound value, don't assume from position/name.

**Line-height standardization:** all 54 styles previously used a completely flat ratio — 140% for every `Tight` style regardless of size, 160% for every `Comfy` style — which is fine at small/body sizes (10–18px, matches standard practice) but badly diverges from convention at heading sizes, where standard practice (Material, Tailwind) tightens the ratio as size grows. Checked real usage file-wide first (fanned out one `use_figma` call per page, per the skill's page-switching rule) — confirmed the large heading steps (600–900) are used **only** in documentation, never in a shipped component, so this was safe to change without touching Button/Input/Checkbox. Applied a graduated curve to the 13 affected styles (≥20px, both families, both platforms):

| Size | Old ratio | New ratio (Tight / Comfy) |
|---|---|---|
| 20px | 140% | 135% / — |
| 24px | 140% / 160% | 128% / 142% |
| 28px | 140% / 160% | 120% / 133% |
| 36px | 140% / 160% | 113% / 125% |
| 40px | 140% / 160% | 110% / 122% |

**Redundancy audit:** grouped all 54 styles by actual visual properties (not name) — 33 unique property sets from 54 named styles. Most overlap is benign (Desktop/100 ≈ Mobile/100 at the smallest size, a fine coincidence). One real problem found: on Mobile, `Default/Tight` steps 200/300/400 were **pixel-identical** (all 14px) — the numbered scale wasn't doing anything in that range. Also found Desktop/600 ≈ Mobile/800 and Desktop/700 ≈ Mobile/900 (a *different* issue — cross-platform step misalignment, not true redundancy; not touched).

**`Default/Tight` activated, not removed:** had zero bindings anywhere in the file before this — confirmed via the same file-wide usage scan. Rather than deleting it, the user confirmed Table/List/Dropdown-style dense UI is on the roadmap, so this family now has a real assigned role: **Dense/Data** (table cells, list rows, dropdown items — regular weight, compact leading, not prose, not emphasis). Fixed its Mobile-step collapse first (200/300/400 all being 14px) by mirroring Desktop's clean 10/12/14/16/18 progression (200→12, 400→16, 500→18) before assigning the role, since a role that's about to see real use shouldn't inherit a known scale bug.

**Mid-task hazard, worth remembering:** partway through this, the user accidentally renamed ~36 of the 54 styles live in the Figma app while I was mid-audit (moved `Default/*` under `Accent/Default/*`, dropped the `Accent/` prefix from `Accent/Comfy/*`). Caught it because a repeat audit found node labels no longer matching their bound style names. Since Figma styles are IDs under the hood, nothing that was already bound anywhere broke — only the display names drifted, and reverting by name (matching old ↔ new 1:1 since count and IDs were unchanged) fully restored it. **If style names look wrong again, check IDs before assuming corruption** — a live collaborator renaming in the app is a real, recurring risk on this file while work is in progress here.

**"Compact ladder" confirmed as standard practice, then the section reorganized.** Researched whether shifting the whole heading scale down together (rather than shrinking one level) is a recognized pattern, not something invented for this file: [Shopify Polaris did exactly this](https://github.com/Shopify/polaris-react/issues/6967) — shifted their entire heading scale down a tier for denser UI (`headingLg`→`headingMd`, etc.) rather than adjusting one size in isolation; [Bogdan Cerovac's accessibility writeup](https://cerovac.com/a11y/2022/03/visual-and-semantic-heading-levels-can-be-different-but/) confirms semantic level and visual size are allowed to diverge but warns to keep them as consistent as possible so a lower heading never visually outsizes a higher one; [CMS.gov's design system](https://design.cms.gov/foundation/typography/headings) documents the same semantic/visual separation as an explicit principle. Confirmed standard — then reorganized the "Type Scale — Semantic Mapping" section into clearly labeled groups (Headings H1–H6 → Compact ladder → Body & Caption → Dense/Data) instead of the interleaved patchwork that had built up from several rounds of incremental fixes (the compact-ladder rows were wedged between H1 and H2, notes were orphaned at the bottom far from the row they applied to). Each group now has its own small label header, matching the pattern the compact-ladder note already used.

**Closed out the remaining duplicate-style findings (2026-08-21).** Two more within-platform collisions from the earlier redundancy audit, same root cause and same fix pattern already applied to `Default/Tight`: `Default/Comfy/Mobile/300` and `/400` were both 14px (fixed `/400`→16px, and had to also bump `/500`→18px since 16 would've otherwise collided with the newly-fixed `/400`); `Accent/Comfy/Mobile/300` and `/400` were both 14px (fixed `/400`→16px). Checked usage first — neither `/400` step had any bindings anywhere in the file, so zero blast radius. This also retired the earlier `Body/Mobile → /500` workaround from the original line-height pass: since the real `/400` mobile bug is now fixed, Body/Mobile points back to `/400` (16px, matching Desktop) like it always should have, and the stale "flagged, not fixed" note on the Typography page was removed since it no longer describes reality. Re-ran the full duplicate scan afterward: 0 remaining within-platform collisions across all 54 styles (cross-platform coincidences like Desktop/100 = Mobile/100 are expected and left alone — only same-platform, same-step-family collisions are the real bug class).

**Naming convention (numeric vs. semantic H1/H2) — deferred, not changed:** discussed with the user; recommended keeping the numeric scale as the source of truth (matches the rest of the token system, more reusable) with the semantic mapping section as the translation layer, rather than renaming 54 already-bound styles. User didn't push back but also didn't explicitly confirm — treat as not yet decided if it comes up again.

## Related Figma libraries in this org (context, not yet reconciled)

Gist Foundations feeds into (or is being reconciled with): "Gist Design System", "Gist Component Library" (×2 — possible migration in progress), "ProRata Design System", "Gist Answers Component Library", "Unthemed DS Foundations/Components", plus several deprecated/archived libraries. Not investigated this session — worth asking the user for the current relationship between these before assuming Gist Foundations is the sole source of truth.

## Code side

`~/design-system` (this repo) was scaffolded as a React+TS+Vite project for a code-side component library, but work pivoted to fixing the Figma file directly instead. It's currently just Vite boilerplate — no design-system code has been written here yet. Ask the user whether/when to pick this back up.

**Now has its own GitHub repo** (`siddharthhardikar7/design-system`, private) and Storybook (`npm run storybook`, port 6006) — both set up 2026-09-02, separate from `gist-geo-demo`.

### Token export: Figma variables → real CSS custom properties (2026-09-02)

Before writing any real components, built the actual bridge from Figma's `semantic` variable collection (96 variables, Light/Dark modes) to code — the missing piece flagged as the real prerequisite ("components would get hand-typed hex values that silently drift from Figma"). Read every semantic variable, fully resolving alias chains to real values for both modes (not just checking a binding exists), and generated `src/styles/tokens.css`: `:root` holds all 96 tokens at their Light values (kebab-cased, e.g. `background/surface/raised` → `--background-surface-raised`), colors get a `prefers-color-scheme: dark` override plus an explicit `[data-theme="dark"]` override (same pattern used for artifact theming — automatic OS-level switching, with a manual override available), and non-color tokens (spacing, radius) aren't mode-dependent so they only appear once. Imported into `main.tsx`.

Source data + generator live in `scripts/`: `figma-tokens.json` (the raw resolved export) and `build-tokens.mjs` (regenerates `tokens.css` from it) — re-run the Figma read + regenerate whenever a semantic variable changes, rather than hand-editing the CSS.

**Bug caught before it shipped:** the first read script returned every token's value as `undefined` (silently serialized away to `{}` by `JSON.stringify`, so it looked like the read had failed entirely rather than errored) — root cause was using `mode.id` instead of Figma's real property name, `mode.modeId`, when indexing `variable.valuesByMode`. Caught by noticing all 96 `perMode` objects were empty, not by an error.

Verified both directions with a real dev server, not just by reading the file: started `design-system-dev` (Vite, port 5188 — separate from Storybook, which loads `.storybook/preview.tsx` and never touches `main.tsx`), read `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)` for a sample of tokens in light mode, then emulated `prefers-color-scheme: dark` and re-read the same tokens — confirmed `--content-default` flips from near-black to white and `--background-surface-raised` flips from white to dark grey, exactly matching the Figma Light/Dark values.

### Icon library decided: Phosphor Icons, not "Asset & Icon Library" (2026-09-02)

Every icon slot built this session (Input, Alert, Menu Item) had the same standing blocker: "no confirmed local icon library yet." Resolved properly instead of guessing — checked `get_libraries` on the Figma file and found **two already-subscribed candidates**, not zero: **Asset & Icon Library** (a private, org-only Figma library — the actual source of the `light/user-group`/`light/info` icons that caused the earlier blank-icon bug, and almost certainly also of the mysteriously-named "Acorn" default Menu Item shipped with) and **Phosphor Icons (Community)** (the real open-source project at [github.com/phosphor-icons/homepage](https://github.com/phosphor-icons/homepage)).

**User's call: Phosphor, explicitly avoiding Asset & Icon Library.** The deciding factor: Phosphor has an official npm package (`@phosphor-icons/react`) with exact 1:1 parity to this same Figma community library — meaning Figma and code can reference the *same* icon set with guaranteed consistency. Asset & Icon Library is Figma-only and proprietary; using it would mean either hand-exporting SVGs into code (ongoing drift risk) or guessing at a public lookalike. Each Phosphor icon is a real component set with `Format` (Outline/Stroke) × `Weight` (Thin/Light/Regular/Bold/Fill/Duotone) variants — confirmed this is exactly the shape "Acorn" was already using (`Format=Stroke, Weight=Regular`), so nothing about Menu Item's structure needed to change, just which library backs it.

**Applied immediately, not just decided:** imported Phosphor's `User` icon (component key `f08c275af13b4584452b6a2c98bba8bcd781188c`), confirmed its default variant frame is 32×32 (matches the user's "icons seem too large" read — same oversized-default pattern as the earlier 24px issue, fixed the same way: resize on instance, not on the master, since remote masters can't be edited directly — attempting to rebind the master's own vector strokes threw `Cannot write to internal and read-only node`; the fix is always to override at the instance level). Swapped all 24 of Menu Item's existing icon instances (2 slots × 12 variants) onto this new master, resized to 20×20 (matching the size already settled on this session), and rebound each instance's stroke to `content/default` individually — master-level color bindings don't propagate to instances, so this per-instance rebind has to be repeated for every fresh icon swap, same as the very first Acorn setup. Updated the `Leading Icon`/`Trailing Icon` property's registered `defaultValue` and `preferredValues` to point at the real Phosphor icon too, not just the placed instances — otherwise the properties panel would keep suggesting the old (undocumented, unlabeled) default.

**Found gone while fixing this: the "Menu — Example (With Divider)" composition no longer exists on the page** — not moved, not renamed, absent from a full page search by name. This is the same live-collaborator-editing risk flagged earlier in the Typography section (someone else editing this file concurrently causes nodes to appear/disappear mid-session). Did not attempt to reconstruct it under this task's scope; the real `Menu Item`/`Menu`/`Menu Divider` components themselves were all confirmed still intact.

**Established going forward: Phosphor Icons (Community) is this system's icon library.** Any new icon slot (instance-swap or otherwise) should default to a Phosphor icon, not Asset & Icon Library. Existing Alert/Input icon placeholders were not touched in this pass — they still reference the old ambiguous sources and should get the same treatment next time either component is revisited.

### Code-side plan for icons, once real components get built

`@phosphor-icons/react` is the npm counterpart — `npm install @phosphor-icons/react`, then e.g. `<User size={20} />`, matching the Figma icon's resized 20×20 default. Each icon component takes `size` (number, any value — not locked to Figma's 32px default) and `weight` (thin/light/regular/bold/fill/duotone, matching the Figma `Weight` variant), so a component's icon slot in code should expose the same two knobs, not hardcode one.

**Scoped to color/spacing/radius only for this pass** — typography (the named `Default/Comfy/Desktop/*` text styles, font family/weight/size) wasn't included and would be a natural next export once real components need it. Chart/brand tokens were exported since they were already in the same collection, but nothing yet consumes them.

### First real component: MenuItem (2026-09-02)

Coded the first component to actually consume `tokens.css` and Phosphor — `src/components/MenuItem/` (`MenuItem.tsx` + `.module.css` + `.stories.tsx`), translating the Figma component directly: `size` prop (small/medium/large, same height/font-size mapping as Figma), `label`, `leadingIcon`/`trailingIcon` accepting any `ReactNode` (mirrors the instance-swap flexibility — pass any `@phosphor-icons/react` icon), native `disabled` (standard button attribute, not a separate variant prop). Hover/Pressed are real `:hover`/`:active` CSS, not props — they're native browser states here, unlike Figma where they have to be separate static variants.

Icons pull color via CSS `currentColor` inheritance exactly as planned — set `color` once on the button (`content/default`, `content/disabled` on `:disabled`) and both the label text and the icon dim together, no per-icon styling needed in code (this is the asymmetry with Figma discussed earlier: Figma needs a manual per-instance stroke rebind on every icon swap; code gets it for free).

**Real bug caught by actually testing in Storybook, not just eyeballing a screenshot:** the Disabled story rendered with the icon and text in solid black instead of the expected translucent grey — checking `getComputedStyle` confirmed `--content-disabled` was completely undefined in that context. Root cause: **Storybook has its own separate entry point (`.storybook/preview.tsx`), which never imported `tokens.css`** — only `src/main.tsx` did. The CSS variables exist for the real app but not for anything rendered inside Storybook. Fixed by importing `tokens.css` in `preview.tsx` too; re-verified `getComputedStyle` afterward and got the real resolved value (`rgba(2, 9, 18, 0.4)`) instead of the browser's black default. **Any future global stylesheet (typography, resets, etc.) needs to be imported in both places** — `main.tsx` for the real app, `.storybook/preview.tsx` for Storybook — since they're two independent entry points into the same component code.

Verified three ways before calling it done: `npx vitest --project storybook run` (15/15 tests passing, including a `Clickable` story with a real `play` function asserting `onClick` actually fires on click — not just a render-only smoke test), `npx tsc -b --noEmit` (clean — the only 2 errors are pre-existing unused-import issues in Storybook's own default demo files, not anything touched here), and a live visual check in the browser confirming the icon/label/chevron layout, sizing, and the disabled-state color dimming all match the Figma component.

### Real typography tokens, real Haffer font files, and two real bugs the user caught by actually looking (2026-09-02)

User pushed back hard, and correctly, on the first MenuItem pass: "the font looks too thick — are you sure you built correctly?" Checked with evidence rather than assuming: `getComputedStyle` on the *actual* rendered button (not a stale/wrong element — see the false alarm below) confirmed my CSS was applying exactly as written (`font-family: Haffer, ui-sans-serif...`, `font-weight: 400`). So the CSS was correct — the real gap was that **typography had been hand-typed instead of exported from Figma**, unlike colors/spacing which went through the proper token pipeline. Fixed the inconsistency: read the real `Default/Comfy/Desktop/200/400/500` text styles (font family, weight, size, line-height) directly from Figma, built `src/styles/typography.css` the same way `tokens.css` was built, and pointed `MenuItem.module.css`'s per-size classes at those tokens instead of numbers I'd typed by hand.

**False alarm caught before it mattered:** a first check of the rendered font via `document.querySelectorAll('button')` at the top-level document returned "Nunito Sans" / weight 700 — which looked like proof of a real bug, but was actually querying Storybook's own manager-shell DOM, not the nested preview iframe where the component actually renders. Re-queried by descending into `iframe.contentDocument` and got the real answer (Haffer, 400) — a reminder that Storybook's docs/canvas views are nested iframes, and a `document.querySelector` from the top frame can silently match the wrong element with a coincidentally-matching text string instead of erroring.

**User then supplied real Haffer font files** (`Haffer-Regular` and `Haffer-Medium`, each as otf/ttf/woff/woff2) — dropped into `src/assets/fonts/Haffer/`. Built `src/styles/fonts.css` with real `@font-face` rules (woff2 primary, woff fallback, `font-display: swap`), imported into both `main.tsx` and `.storybook/preview.tsx` (learned that lesson already, see the earlier `content-disabled` bug). Verified with `document.fonts` that Haffer 400 actually loads (`status: "loaded"`), not just referenced — confirmed a real, visible difference in the rendered weight versus the earlier system-font substitute.

**Second real bug, caught by the user looking at the Small size specifically: icon/label misalignment.** Root cause: the icon wrapper `<span>` was correctly sized to 20×20, but the actual Phosphor `<svg>` inside it was never resized — Phosphor defaults its icons to `width="1em" height="1em"` (HTML attributes, not inline style), meaning the glyph was actually rendering at the *row's font-size* (12px at Small, 18px at Large) inside an oversized, uncentered 20px box, most visible at Small where the gap between "1em" (12px) and the wrapper (20px) is largest. The "fixed 20×20 regardless of size" claim in the component's own JSDoc was wrong in practice — only the wrapper was fixed, not the icon itself. Fixed by adding `.icon svg { width: 100%; height: 100%; }` (a real CSS property beats a presentation attribute, no `!important` needed) plus `align-items/justify-content: center` on the wrapper for robustness. Verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` on the actual `<svg>` — confirmed 20×20 post-fix, not just the wrapper.

**Also built `Menu` and `Divider`** (the two other pieces of the Figma "Menu" system the user flagged as missing) — `src/components/Menu/` (a `role="menu"` div: `background/surface/raised`, `border/default`, `border/radius/md`, `spacing/padding/100` padding, `spacing/gap/100` gap between rows) and `src/components/Divider/` (a plain `<hr>` on `border/default`). Added `role="menuitem"` to `MenuItem`'s button to match — this broke the `Clickable` story's own test (`getByRole('button', ...)` no longer matched, since an explicit `role` attribute overrides the element's implicit accessible role), fixed by querying `getByRole('menuitem', ...)` instead. **Note, not yet resolved:** the Figma `Menu` container's own `itemSpacing` read back as `spacing/gap/100` (4px) when re-checked for this work, not the `spacing/gap/200` (8px) it was corrected to earlier in the "Row rhythm" spacing-guide pass — possibly reverted by the same live-collaborator-editing risk already flagged twice this session (the vanished Divider example, the reappearing/disappearing nodes). Coded against the currently-true Figma value rather than memory of the earlier fix; worth reconciling next time this page is open in Figma.

All of the above re-verified together: `npx vitest --project storybook run` → 19/19 passing (added a `StructureCheck` play test on `Menu` asserting `role="menu"` renders with the right count of `role="menuitem"` children), `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Second real component: Button (2026-09-02)

Coded `src/components/Button/` (5 `type`s × 3 `size`s, matching Figma's `Button/Action` component set exactly) — same translation approach as MenuItem: `type`/`size` props, `leadingIcon`/`trailingIcon` as `ReactNode`, native `disabled`, Hover/Pressed/Focus as real CSS pseudo-classes rather than props. Pulled every token fresh from Figma rather than trusting memory, given the live-editing-drift risk already seen twice this session — confirmed font is **Haffer Medium** (not Regular, unlike MenuItem), sizes 14/16/16px (Small/Medium/Large — Medium and Large share the same font size, only height/padding differ), and added the two new `Accent/Tight/Desktop/300`/`400` styles to `typography.css` alongside the existing `Default/Comfy/*` family already there for MenuItem.

**Focus ring translated as a real cross-browser CSS pattern.** Figma implements it as a 2px `background/surface/page`-colored stroke (a "gap" ring) plus a 2px-spread drop-shadow effect in `border/focus` (or `border/status-error` specifically for Danger — confirmed by reading `.effects`, not just `.strokes`; the strokes alone only show the gap ring, the colored ring is a separate effect layer). Reproduced with two stacked `box-shadow`s on `:focus-visible` (`0 0 0 2px page-color, 0 0 0 4px ring-color`) — `:focus-visible` specifically, not `:focus`, so the ring only shows for keyboard navigation, not mouse clicks. Verified via actual Tab-key keyboard navigation in the browser (not just inspecting CSS) — confirmed black ring on Primary/Secondary/Tertiary/Ghost, red ring on Danger.

**Naming collision caught before it shipped:** `ButtonProps` initially just extended `ButtonHTMLAttributes`, but Figma's `Type` property (Primary/Secondary/etc.) and the native HTML `<button type>` attribute (submit/reset/button) share the same prop name. Fixed by `Omit<ButtonHTMLAttributes<HTMLButtonElement>, 'type'>` and exposing the native behavior separately as `htmlType`, defaulting to `'button'`.

**Danger's per-state text-color switch, already documented from the original Figma design work, carried over faithfully:** Default/Hover/Focus use `background/action/danger-subtle-content` (near-black), but Pressed switches to `background/action/danger-content` (white) — matches the deliberate contrast-driven decision logged earlier this session (`background/action/danger-subtle-pressed`'s fill is dark enough that only white text keeps reading, everywhere else needs the opposite). Reproduced via `--button-fg-pressed` overriding `--button-fg` only inside `:active`.

**Real Figma token bug found (not a code bug) while testing the new dark-mode toggle (see below):** `background/action/primary-content` (Primary's text color) is white in **both** Light and Dark mode, but Primary's *background* correctly flips from dark (Light mode) to near-white (Dark mode) — meaning Dark-mode Primary buttons render as white text (`rgb(255,255,255)`) on a near-white background (`rgb(254,253,251)`), functionally invisible. Confirmed by checking the actual rendered button's computed styles directly (not just the token value) — classes and structure were correct, the token itself is the problem. **Not fixed yet** — flagged to the user; Button (unlike Alert) has never had a dark-mode audit pass, so the same class of bug may exist on other Types too.

### Storybook theme toggle added (2026-09-02)

User asked how to preview dark mode in Storybook — there wasn't a way; `tokens.css` already supported `[data-theme="dark"]` as an override hook (built during the original token-export pass), but nothing in Storybook ever set that attribute. Added a `globalTypes` toolbar control (`.storybook/preview.tsx`) with System/Light/Dark options and a decorator that sets/removes `data-theme` on `document.documentElement` — `system` removes the attribute entirely, falling back to the existing `prefers-color-scheme` media query.

**Second real gap found via this, fixed immediately:** toggling to Dark correctly recolored the button itself, but the surrounding Storybook canvas stayed hardcoded white — any dark-styled component would look broken sitting on a light backdrop regardless of whether its own tokens were correct. Root cause: nothing had ever applied `background/surface/page` to the actual page/canvas background — only components consumed tokens, the page shell never did. Fixed with a new hand-maintained `src/styles/base.css` (`body { background-color: var(--background-surface-page); color: var(--content-default); }`) — kept separate from the auto-generated `tokens.css` specifically so it survives a token re-export. Imported into both `main.tsx` and `.storybook/preview.tsx`, per the now-familiar two-entry-point rule. Verified: Secondary button (unaffected by the Primary bug above) now renders with correct contrast against a properly dark canvas after toggling.

**Debugging gotcha repeated from earlier, worth internalizing:** twice while investigating this, a `document.querySelectorAll('button')` from the outer Storybook document matched the wrong element (an empty-class button, not the real component) — same nested-iframe trap as the "Nunito Sans" false alarm from the font investigation. The reliable pattern: find the real preview iframe via `Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('iframe')).find(f => f.src.includes(story-id))`, then query `iframe.contentDocument`, and confirm the match by checking `className` actually contains the component's own CSS-module class before trusting any computed style read.

### Correction: the "Primary button invisible in dark mode" bug was mine, not Figma's (2026-09-02)

User pushed back on the "Figma token bug" claim from the audit above: "in my Figma's foundations it looks correct? it uses primary content which uses inverse colors." That was the right instinct, and checking the *raw* variable binding (not the resolved color) confirmed it — **`background/action/primary-content` really is aliased to `content/inverse` in both modes**, and `content/inverse` itself correctly aliases to a different underlying variable per mode (confirmed via `variable.valuesByMode`, not just the flattened color). Figma's own setup was right the whole time.

**Root cause, found by re-reading my own resolution helper line by line:** every alias-walking script used all session (`resolveColor`, `resolveValue` — the pattern used to seed correct colors before `setBoundVariableForPaint`, and the one that built `figma-tokens.json`) advanced to the next hop with `Object.keys(varObj.valuesByMode)[0]` — i.e. "whichever mode key happens to be first" — instead of continuing to resolve the mode that was actually being asked for. This is invisible for single-hop resolutions (asking a variable for its own Light/Dark value directly) and for anything only ever resolved for its default/Light appearance (which was nearly everything else fixed this session, since Figma's canvas edits by default in Light mode). It only produces a wrong answer for a **multi-hop Dark-mode lookup where an intermediate alias target's own mode order doesn't happen to put Dark first** — exactly `background/action/primary-content → content/inverse → [mode-specific primitive]`, a 2-hop chain, which is why this specific token broke and single-hop ones like `content/default` never did.

**Fixed the resolver properly:** re-implemented it to track the *mode name* ("Light"/"Dark") across every hop, matching by name in each alias target's own collection (falling back to that collection's first mode only if the name genuinely doesn't exist there, e.g. crossing into a single-mode `global` collection). Re-ran the full 96-variable semantic export with the corrected resolver.

**Then verified the fix the way the user explicitly asked for — independently, not by re-trusting my own (now-changed) code a second time.** Built two temporary offscreen frames, force-set each to Light and Dark respectively via `frame.setExplicitVariableModeForCollection(semanticCollection, modeId)`, bound all 75 color variables to real rectangle fills inside them, and read back what **Figma's own native resolution engine** actually produced — completely independent of any JS alias-walking code of mine. Diffed both the Light and Dark native-resolved sets against the corrected export programmatically (not by eyeballing): **0 mismatches across all 75 color tokens, in both modes.** Deleted the temporary frames and the scratch comparison files afterward, keeping only the corrected `figma-tokens.json`.

**Net effect on the Button audit above:** every previously-"broken" pair is now fixed — Primary (all 3 states) and Secondary (Default + Pressed) jumped from ~1–1.4:1 to 13.99–19.46:1. Danger's Default/Hover/Pressed remain "borderline" (3.08–4.45:1) — that's unchanged by this fix and is a separate, legitimate design question (same category as the already-documented Alert/Warning contrast tradeoff), not something this resolver bug was masking.

### Button and Tag's own Figma icon instances migrated to Phosphor (2026-09-02)

User caught a real gap: I'd flagged Tag's icon slots as "not yet migrated to Phosphor" but never checked whether Button had the same issue — it did. Both `Button/Action`'s and `Tag`'s own Figma icon instances still referenced `light/sparkle` (Button) and `light/check-circle`/`light/x` (Tag), all from the deprioritized Asset & Icon Library, despite the icon-library decision being settled earlier this session. Code-side stories already used real Phosphor icons; Figma itself didn't.

Fixed both: imported Phosphor's `Sparkle` (Button, both slots), `CheckCircle` (Tag leading) and `X` (Tag trailing), swapped onto all icon instances — **150 for Button** (75 variants × 2 slots) and **24 for Tag** (12 variants × 2 slots) — preserving each slot's original size, and recoloring each icon's stroke to **that exact variant's own bound text-color variable** (not a single fixed token), since Button's text color varies significantly by Type/State (Primary/Danger especially). Verified by temporarily forcing visibility on one Button and one Tag variant, screenshotting, then reverting visibility back to the correct hidden-by-default state (component masters, not just the boolean property definition, needed reverting too — toggling `.visible` directly on a variant doesn't disconnect its boolean binding, but does change that master's own default appearance if left changed).
