### A real icon-size + weight system, instead of per-component guessing (2026-09-02)

User called out the actual pattern: every component so far had its icon size settled through trial-and-error (Menu Item: 24→16→20px; Button copied 20px; Tag guessed 16px/8px then got corrected) with no shared system behind any of it — plus a second, related complaint: icons looked "too thick" specifically at small sizes. The two aren't separate problems. Phosphor icons aren't live stroke paths with an adjustable width — each `Weight` (Thin/Light/Regular/Bold/Fill/Duotone) is a distinct pre-drawn shape, and a weight that reads fine at 20px reads muddy at 12–16px, the same optical-sizing problem typefaces have in reverse (small text needs proportionally thicker strokes to stay legible; small icons need proportionally thinner ones to stay crisp).

**Built a real 4-step scale as actual Figma variables** (not just CSS constants) — `size/icon/xs` (12px), `sm` (16px), `md` (20px), `lg` (24px, not yet claimed by anything) — added to the `semantic` collection as FLOAT variables alongside `spacing/*` and `border/radius/*`, so they show up in Dev Mode the same way every other token does. Mapped what already existed onto it rather than inventing new numbers: Menu Item and Button's existing 20px icons → `md` (no visual change, just properly tokenized — previously a hardcoded 20 with no variable behind it), Tag's leading icon → `sm` (16px, was already close), Tag's trailing "×" → `xs` (12px, standardized from an inconsistent 8–12px across sizes).

**Weight paired with size, applied in Figma directly:** `xs`/`sm` → Phosphor's **Light** weight, `md`/`lg` → **Regular**. Since weight is baked into the icon shape rather than being a CSS-adjustable property, this had to be fixed at the source — re-swapped Tag's `CheckCircle`/`X` instances onto their `Format=Stroke, Weight=Light` variants (Menu Item and Button already used `Regular`, correctly, at `md`). Verified visually at 4x zoom: Light reads noticeably cleaner and less muddy than Regular did at the same 16/12px sizes.

**The real limit of this system, worth remembering:** weight can't be enforced from outside by the component — `MenuItem`/`Button`/`Tag` all accept a plain `ReactNode` for their icon slots, so there's no way to force whatever a consumer passes in to use the right weight. The fix only reaches as far as (a) the Figma source components (owned, directly fixable) and (b) the code *stories*, which now pass `weight="light"` explicitly on Tag's icons as the demonstrated reference pattern — anyone building a new usage has to copy that convention deliberately, it isn't structurally guaranteed the way `size/icon/*` is once wired into a CSS variable.

**Consequence check done in the same pass, not applied:** user separately asked whether Button's icon-label gap should move from 4px to 8px. Checked what that would actually cost: 4px is `spacing/gap/100`, the exact value the spacing guide's own role table assigns to "Inline rhythm — icon + label" — the same token Menu Item and Tag both already use for the identical reason. Declined per the user's own call, specifically to avoid Button silently drifting from a rule every sibling component still follows; left at 4px, unbound-in-Figma status untouched (a separate, already-known minor gap, not addressed in this pass).

Regenerated `tokens.css` (100 light tokens now, up from 96) and `figma-tokens.json`, updated `MenuItem.module.css`/`Button.module.css`/`Tag.module.css` to reference `var(--size-icon-*)` instead of hardcoded pixel values, updated Tag's stories to pass `weight="light"`. Fixed one real pre-existing TypeScript error surfaced by an unrelated `tsc` run in `Radio.stories.tsx` while in there (CSF3's `Story` type requires `args` even when a story fully overrides rendering via `render()` — two stories were missing it). Verified: 45/45 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live in Storybook.

### Fourth/fifth real components: Radio (+ RadioIndicator) and Checkbox (+ CheckboxIndicator) (2026-09-02)

Same strict process, fresh pull each time. **Radio Indicator** had a real bug: its Hover state carried two identical, fully-redundant fill layers on the outer ring (same token, same opacity) — harmless visually (stacking two 10%-alpha layers of the same color mathematically compounds to ~19% rather than staying at 10%, so it wasn't *quite* inert, but clearly an accidental duplicate rather than a deliberate design), de-duped to one. **Option/Radio**'s icon-to-label gap was bound to `border/radius/md` — the same wrong-token-family bug as Pagination Menu Item and Tag, rebound to `spacing/gap/200` (8px, matching the value already there).

**Checkbox had a more serious structural bug: `Option/Checkbox`'s Disabled variant's checkbox instance pointed at `State=Default`, not `State=Disabled`.** The composed row would have rendered with a normal-looking, active-styled checkbox even while functionally disabled — caught only by explicitly comparing each variant's `checkboxMainComponent` name against its own row name, not by any visual check (a screenshot of just the Disabled row alone wouldn't reveal it's pointing at the wrong master unless compared against what Default actually looks like). Also found: `Checkbox`'s own corner radius (4px) unbound to any token, Disabled's stroke weight (1.5) inconsistent with every other state (1), and its gap (10px, unbound) not matching any token in the spacing scale — snapped to `spacing/gap/200` (8px) to match Radio's own precedent for the same "control + label" role, rather than the "icon + label" 4px role, since a real interactive form control paired with its label is a different case than a decorative icon next to text. Two flagged-but-inert findings from the audit script turned out to be harmless: a stray unbound fill on Checkbox's root component and a `light/info` icon nested in Option/Checkbox's label frame — both have `visible: false`, the second being the same "hidden, unexposed capability" pattern as Alert's once-hidden Title/Actions slots. Left both untouched.

Migrated `Checkbox`'s check/minus icons to Phosphor (`Check`, `Minus`, Regular weight — matching the `md`-tier convention, though sized smaller at 14px to match the original proportions inside the 20px box) the same way Menu Item/Button/Tag's icons were earlier.

**Coded both as real, accessible native inputs, not just styled `<div>`s** — a genuinely different approach from every component built so far, since these are actual form controls: `<input type="radio">`/`<input type="checkbox">`, visually hidden (`opacity: 0`, absolutely positioned over the visual indicator) but still focusable, keyboard-operable, and screen-reader-announced, with `RadioIndicator`/`CheckboxIndicator` as a purely-visual sibling driven by real `:checked`/`:disabled`/`:focus-visible` pseudo-classes via sibling CSS selectors — not JS-computed classNames faking interactivity. `Checkbox` additionally handles `indeterminate`, which isn't a real HTML attribute (only a DOM *property*) — set via a `useRef` + `useEffect`, the standard React pattern for it, since passing it as a JSX prop directly does nothing.

**Real cross-component CSS bug caught before it shipped, worth remembering for any future composed pair:** `Checkbox.module.css` first tried to style `CheckboxIndicator`'s box directly via bare selectors like `.indicator:not(.filled)` — but CSS Modules scope class names per-file, so those names only exist in `CheckboxIndicator.module.css`'s own compiled output; a *different* module's selectors referencing the same bare name silently match nothing. Fixed by having `Checkbox` pass its own composed marker classes (`indicatorSlot`/`indicatorSlotFilled`, computed in JS from `checked`/`indeterminate` rather than relying on cross-module class detection) into `CheckboxIndicator`'s `className` prop — both a component's own scoped classes and a parent's marker classes land on the same DOM element simultaneously, so each module's CSS can select against the classes it actually owns. Verified live: read `getComputedStyle` before/after a real mouse hover and confirmed the wash genuinely toggles (`rgba(0,0,0,0)` → `rgba(2,9,18,0.05)`, exactly `background/interactive/hover`'s resolved value) — not just that the code compiled.

Dark-mode contrast checked for both (checkmark/dot vs. their own fill): 13.75–20.02:1 across Light/Dark, comfortably clear. Verified: 53/53 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live in Storybook including real click-driven radio-group switching and checkbox toggling (not just static story rendering).

### Checkbox: two real bugs the user caught by using it, one false alarm resolved by measuring rather than guessing (2026-09-02)

User reported two things after trying Checkbox live: label text not vertically centered with the box, and the row visibly "jumping" on every toggle. Also mentioned separately they'd made Figma edits to Checkbox — re-pulled fresh rather than assuming what changed.

**Alignment — a real mismatch, and the Figma re-pull explained it directly.** `Option/Checkbox`'s own `counterAxisAlignItems` is `"MIN"` (Figma's term for top-alignment) — not center. This is exactly the update the user made in Figma, and the code had `align-items: center` from the start, since the original build never checked that specific property. Fixed by matching `align-items: flex-start` in `Checkbox.module.css`.

**Misalignment's other real cause, found while investigating: `box-sizing: content-box` (the CSS default) on `CheckboxIndicator`'s `.indicator`.** The box was declared `width: 20px; height: 20px` with a `1px` border, but content-box adds the border *on top* of the declared size — so it was actually rendering at 22×22, 2px larger than its sibling `.indicatorWrapper` (which stayed a true 20×20). Confirmed via `getComputedStyle` before assuming — the real box-model measurement doesn't lie the way eyeballing a screenshot can. Fixed with `box-sizing: border-box`.

**The "jumping" turned out not to be a bug in the component at all — proven by measurement, not assumed.** Clicking the checkbox (both via real mouse clicks and programmatically) showed the label's *viewport* position shifting by a consistent ~0.8px between checked/unchecked. But measuring the label's position *relative to its own immediate parent* (`getBoundingClientRect()` diffed against `#storybook-root`, not the viewport) showed it locked at exactly 16px in every single state, no exceptions. What actually moved was `#storybook-root` itself — Storybook's `layout: 'centered'` parameter wraps every story in a container that recenters within the full viewport on every re-render, and is sensitive enough to sub-pixel differences in rendered content (e.g. an SVG icon appearing/disappearing) to visibly shift the whole centered block by a fraction of a pixel. Switched the story's `layout` parameter from `'centered'` to `'padded'` (top-anchored, no recentering) and confirmed the shift disappears entirely — `top: 16` in both states, measured, not assumed. **This means the "jump" would never have appeared in a real app** (which doesn't auto-recenter its whole layout on every render) — it was purely a side effect of how this one story happened to be configured, not a defect in `Checkbox` itself.

Re-verified after all three fixes: 53/53 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live via real mouse clicks in the browser (not just re-reading the code).

### Third real component: Tag (2026-09-02)

Continued the "code the well-audited Figma components" pass, same strict process each time: fresh Figma pull (never trust memory of an earlier session pass, given the live-editing-drift risk already seen twice), full 0-issue token audit, dark-mode contrast check with the now-fixed resolver, then translate.

**Found and fixed two real Figma bugs before writing any code** — exactly the outcome "stay true to Figma" is supposed to catch, not something to silently carry into new code:
- **`paddingTop`/`paddingBottom` bound to the wrong token family** on 10 of 12 variants: `size/1` (a raw global primitive) on all 4 Default-type variants, `border/radius/sm`/`md` (a radius token!) on all 8 Success/Warning/Error variants — the exact same class of bug as Pagination Menu Item's padding-bound-to-radius mistake from earlier this session. Fixed by properly binding to `spacing/padding/100` (Small/Medium, 4px) and `spacing/padding/200` (Large, 8px) — **first attempt at this got the tier mapping backwards** (bound Small/Medium to `padding/200` and Large to `padding/300`, growing every Tag taller than designed — Small went from 22px→30px). Caught by comparing the post-fix height against the original reading before moving on, not just trusting the bind succeeded; corrected to match the real original pixel values.
- **A stray `Checkbox` instance (20×20, remote) only on the 4 Large variants** — completely absent from Small/Medium, serving no purpose in the design. Removed; confirmed via screenshot that Large's layout was unaffected (the checkbox was an inert, non-rendering leftover, not a real content contributor).
- **Inconsistent icon-label gap** (`spacing/gap/300` on Default, `spacing/gap/100` on the 3 status types) and **an inert 6px unbound gap** on a single-child wrapper frame inside Large (same "meaningless leftover value" pattern as Table Pagination's earlier `itemSpacing: 1110` bug) — standardized to `spacing/gap/100` and zeroed out respectively.

**Icon structure, not yet migrated to Phosphor:** Tag has two icon slots — a leading status icon (`light/check-circle`, sized 14–16px across Small/Medium/Large) and a smaller trailing dismiss icon (`light/x`, 8–12px) — both still from the deprioritized Asset & Icon Library. Left as-is in Figma for now (same deferred status already logged for Alert/Input's icon placeholders) since it doesn't block coding — the *code* component accepts any `ReactNode` for both slots regardless of what backs Figma's own default instance, and correctly uses Phosphor (`CheckCircle`, `X`) in its own stories.

**Dark-mode contrast checked for all 4 types before coding** (now trustworthy, using the corrected resolver): Default 16.27:1, Success 6.84:1, Warning 9.07:1, Error 5.90:1 — all comfortably pass, no borderline cases like Button's Danger.

Coded `src/components/Tag/` — `type`/`size` props, `leadingIcon`/`trailingIcon` as differently-sized `ReactNode` slots (16px/12px, matching the real Figma proportions above, not a guessed uniform size), added two new typography tokens (`Accent/Tight/Desktop/100` and `/200`, 10px and 12px — Large already reuses Button's existing `/300`). Verified: 39/39 tests passing (added a `StructureCheck` play test), `tsc --noEmit` clean, visual check against both the flat 4-type grid and the icon variant — icon color correctly inherits via `currentColor`, matching every other component built this session.

**Scope note — this bug could theoretically have affected other Dark-mode work done earlier this session** (e.g. the original Alert dark-mode border fix), wherever a *multi-hop* alias was resolved specifically for Dark mode. It does **not** affect anything resolved for Light mode, and does not affect single-hop resolutions regardless of mode. Not retroactively re-audited across the whole file in this pass — flagging as a known risk if a similar "looks right in Figma, wrong in code" report comes up again for an already-fixed Dark-mode component.

### Sixth real component: Input (+ InputHeading) — ✅ built, then two real interaction bugs and one Figma redesign from live testing (2026-09-02)

Same process as every component this session: fresh Figma pull, full token audit, dark-mode contrast check, then translate. Audit found the same recurring pattern as every prior component — `gap`/`paddingTop`/`paddingBottom` bound to the wrong token family across most of the 72 variants — fixed in Figma before coding, plus ~90 inert single-child-wrapper gaps zeroed (same "meaningless leftover value" pattern as Tag/Pagination). Placeholder/disabled contrast on Secondary's sunken background confirmed as an already-accepted tradeoff, not a new bug. Coded `src/components/Input/` (`type`: primary/secondary/tertiary, `size`: small/medium/large/expanded, native `<input>`/`<textarea>` switch for `expanded`) and `src/components/InputHeading/`.

Three issues surfaced only once the user actually used the built component — none were visible from a static Figma read or from screenshots alone:

**1. Tertiary showed an unwanted focus box-shadow ring ("creating a black box on selection which isn't what we wanted").** The code applied the same focus ring to all three types, but Figma's own Tertiary Active state had zero effects — Primary/Secondary Active both had a `DROP_SHADOW`, Tertiary didn't (confirmed via direct read: `tertiaryActive: []`). Tertiary is the underlined type with no border box at all, so a full ring around it read as a foreign shape. Fixed by scoping the ring rule off of Tertiary.

**2. Hover's border-color incorrectly outranked focus's whenever the mouse stayed over a focused field** ("the black border feels thicker when the cursor goes out of the area of the box" — i.e. it only looked *correct* once the mouse left). Root cause was a `:not(:has(.input:disabled))` guard on the hover rule that, incidentally, raised its specificity above the plain `:has()` focus rule — so hover won regardless of source order the entire time the mouse and focus overlapped. Fixed by dropping the guard so hover/focus/disabled sit at equal specificity and source order (hover → focus → disabled) decides precedence correctly. Verified live post-fix by tabbing a real keyboard focus into the field while the mouse remained over it (`:hover` and `:focus-visible` both `true` simultaneously) and confirming the border resolved to the focus color, not hover's.

**3. The Active-state ring itself was reported as looking too thick on Primary/Secondary** ("is the black stroke feeling too thick... i thought focus ring was for accessibility?"). Checked directly against Figma rather than guessing: stroke weight was genuinely unchanged at 1px between Default and Active (not a code bug), but Active *also* added a hard-edged (`radius: 0`) `spread: 2` drop-shadow, and both that ring color (`border/focus`) and the Active border color (`border/interactive-pressed`) resolve to fully opaque near-black (`rgb(8,8,9)` / `rgb(10,10,10)`) — no separate translucent "ring" token exists in the file. Two adjacent opaque near-black strokes read as one solid ~3px line rather than a genuine ring, which is exactly what was reported. After talking through the accessibility tradeoff (a ring only earns its keep as a *distinct* signal from the border-color change; two stacked strokes in the same shade don't add one), the user's call was that the border-color darkening on focus is sufficient on its own — **removed the `DROP_SHADOW` effect from all 8 Primary/Secondary Active variants in Figma** (all 4 sizes × 2 types; Tertiary Active already had none) and removed the corresponding `box-shadow` from `Input.module.css`, along with the now-unneeded Tertiary-specific override from fix #1.

Re-verified after all three fixes: 67/67 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live via real Tab-key keyboard focus (not scripted `.focus()`, which doesn't trigger `:focus-visible`) on Primary and Secondary — both now show only the darkened border with `box-shadow: none` on focus.

### Seventh/eighth/ninth real components: PaginationArrow, PaginationPage, PaginationDropdown (2026-09-02)

Continued the same process on the Pagination family. Two of its four component sets (`pagination control`, the earlier "3 component sets") had already been through prior audit passes this session (2026-08-21, 2026-08-30) — re-pulled fresh anyway per standing rule rather than trusting the old notes, since this file has a track record of drifting between sessions. `pagination page` (numbered page buttons) had never been audited before at all.

**`pagination page` — real bugs found on first audit:**
- **Default/More background bound to a *content* (text-color) token chain**, not a background token — `button/content/primary → content/inverse → color/content/1000`, which happened to resolve to opaque white so it looked correct, but was the wrong token family entirely (same class of bug as almost every other component this session). Rebound to `background/surface/raised`, which resolves to the identical white.
- **Small/Large's Hover background pointed at a remote/foreign library variable** (`button/background/tertiary`) while Medium's equivalent correctly used a local one (`background/surface/sunken`) — both happened to resolve to the same final pixel value by coincidence (both chains bottom out at `color/darken/100`), but only one was a real local token. Rebound Small/Large to match Medium's local token.
- **Inconsistent "More" hover-state naming**: Small/Large called it `More-hover`, Medium called the identical treatment `More-press` — cosmetic but confusing given the fill/opacity was identical across all three. Renamed Medium's to `More-hover` for consistency.
- **`paddingTop`/`paddingBottom` bound to `size/1`** (raw global primitive) on all 15 variants, and one stray variant (`More-hover, Size=small`) had `paddingLeft`/`Right` bound to a `spacing/gap/*` token instead of `spacing/padding/*` — both the same "wrong token family" pattern as everywhere else. Fixed to `spacing/padding/100` and `spacing/padding/200` respectively.

**Dark-mode bug found via contrast check, not visible from a static read:** `background/interactive/selected` — the fill used by the "you are on this page" Active state — resolved in Dark mode to `color/blue/550`, an **opaque dark navy**, giving ~1.0–1.1:1 contrast against the surrounding dark surfaces (functionally invisible; WCAG 1.4.11 wants 3:1 for UI-component boundaries). Compared against its sibling interactive-state tokens (`background/interactive/hover`, `/pressed`, `border/default`, `border/interactive*`) — every one of them uses a **white-alpha wash on `color/content/*`** in Dark mode (hover=`content/100` 5%, pressed=`content/200` 10%), so `selected` landing on an unrelated opaque navy blue breaks its own token family's established pattern. Notably, `color/blue/550` was also the exact wrong value found and fixed for Alert's Success border earlier this session (2026-08-21) — the same primitive shows up as a wrong Dark-mode value in two unrelated places, suggesting a shared copy-paste origin rather than two independent mistakes. Fixed by re-pointing Dark mode to `color/content/300` (30% white) — the natural next step up from pressed's 200 in the same established ladder, giving ~2.7–3.0:1 contrast (a dramatic improvement from ~1:1) while staying inside the same primitive family as its siblings rather than inventing a new value. Light mode was untouched. This is a token-level fix, so it also applies wherever else `background/interactive/selected` gets used, not just here.

**`pagination control` (arrows) and `pagination dropdown` (results-per-page trigger) — re-verified clean on fills/strokes** (no drift since the 2026-08-21/08-30 fixes), but a fresh binding-family check (not previously done) turned up the same recurring pattern:
- **Control**: `paddingTop`/`paddingBottom` bound to `size/1` (raw primitive) across all 48 variants — fixed to `spacing/padding/100`, matching the already-correct `paddingLeft`/`Right` (`spacing/padding/200`).
- **Dropdown**: `itemSpacing` bound to **`border/radius/md` on Small/Medium, and `font/size/xxxs` on Large** — a spacing property pointed at radius and font-size tokens respectively, not a spacing token at all. `paddingLeft`/`Right` on Medium bound to a raw `size/4` primitive instead of `spacing/padding/*`. Fixed all to the correct `spacing/gap/100|200|300` and `spacing/padding/200|300|400` per size tier, verified against measured pixel values before and after (unchanged).

**Also flagged, not fixed:** Disabled arrows have no visual differentiation from Default in Figma — the icon is a remote nested instance with an empty local `fills` array, so its color can't be rebound at the token level, and the component's own background/border don't change for Disabled either. Since this is a known, already-accepted limitation for this icon (documented as "genuinely remote... left as-is" in the 2026-08-30 entry), the code applies a standard `opacity: 0.4` + `cursor: not-allowed` on `:disabled` — the conventional fallback most design systems use for icon-only disabled controls — rather than leaving Disabled indistinguishable from Default.

**New typography tokens added**, matching real Figma text styles that didn't have a code equivalent yet: `Default/Tight/Desktop/200|300|400` (12/14/16px, 140% line-height — pagination page's numbers) and `Default/Comfy/Desktop/300` (14px, 160% — pagination dropdown's Medium label).

Coded `src/components/PaginationArrow` (Type=first/back/next/last via Phosphor `CaretDoubleLeft/CaretLeft/CaretRight/CaretDoubleRight`, Size=small/medium/large mapped to the established `size/icon/xs/sm/md` tiers), `src/components/PaginationPage` (page-number button with `active`/`more` states, `DotsThree` for the ellipsis), `src/components/PaginationDropdown` (results-per-page trigger with `CaretDown`). Verified: 88/88 tests passing (up from 67), clean typecheck, confirmed live in Storybook — all four arrow types, a full page-number bar with active/more states, and the dropdown trigger — plus a dark-mode check specifically confirming the `background/interactive/selected` fix (Active page number is now clearly visible against the dark canvas, where it would previously have been indistinguishable).

### Tenth real component: Alert — closing out two items left open since 2026-08-21 (2026-09-02)

Re-pulled fresh per standing rule even though this component had already been through a thorough audit earlier in the session. Confirmed no drift on any of the prior fixes (border/status-*, content/status-*, radius all still correctly bound). One new finding from a binding-family check that hadn't been done before: **the container's padding (10px) and gap (12px) weren't bound to any variable at all** — raw hardcoded values, not even the "wrong family" pattern seen elsewhere, just plain unbound numbers. 10px didn't cleanly match an existing `spacing/padding/*` step, so bound both padding and gap to `spacing/padding/300`/`spacing/gap/300` (12px) — matching each other and the nearest existing token, with no visual regression (10→12px is a 2px, largely imperceptible change).

**Closed one of the two items the 2026-08-21 entry left explicitly open:** "Warning and Error currently share the exact same icon (`light/alert-triangle`), differing only by color... no clear alternative icon was located." Since Alert's icon is a `ReactNode` slot (same pattern as Tag/Input, not hardcoded), this doesn't require a Figma change — the code now defaults Warning to Phosphor's `WarningCircle` and Error to `WarningOctagon` (Info keeps `Info`, Success keeps `CheckCircle`), giving each status a genuinely distinct shape rather than relying on color alone, addressing the underlying WCAG 1.4.1 concern.

**The other open item — Warning's text failing AA contrast (4.01:1) even on plain white — is still open**, unchanged from 2026-08-21: fixing it means creating a new, darker `color/warning/700` primitive, a foundational palette change beyond what a component-level pass should do unilaterally. Carried forward as a known, documented tradeoff rather than silently patched.

Coded `src/components/Alert/` — `status` (info/warning/error/success), optional `title`, required `children` (description), optional `icon` override, `onDismiss` (renders a close button when provided, using the same Phosphor `X` as Input/Tag). Verified: 96/96 tests passing (up from 88), clean typecheck, confirmed live in both Light and Dark mode — all four statuses clearly legible in Dark mode (the borderless/border-only design decided on 2026-08-21 composites cleanly over a dark canvas), Warning/Error now visibly distinct by icon shape, and the dismiss button's click handler firing correctly.

### Eleventh real component: Template Card (2026-09-02)

Re-pulled fresh; confirmed no drift on any of the 2026-08-21 fixes (correct Tag/Button instances, `content/inverse`→`content/default` surface correction, all spacing/radius bindings). No new bugs found on re-audit — this component was left in genuinely clean shape last time.

Coded `src/components/TemplateCard/` composing the already-built `Tag` and `Button` components: `title`, optional `description` (rendered as a Default-type Tag, matching the "Using '...' default" caption), optional `status` (a second Tag, type-configurable — generalizes the Figma instance's hardcoded Error-type placeholder), optional `ctaLabel`/`onCtaClick`, optional `onMenuClick` (renders the "···" overflow button only when provided). The illustration is a `thumbnail?: ReactNode` slot defaulting to a simplified concentric-circle placeholder — this is the natural resolution to the "preview illustration has no swap mechanism, deferred until real preview components exist" item flagged 2026-08-21: React's plain `ReactNode` prop *is* that swap mechanism, no Figma `INSTANCE_SWAP` property needed. Verified: clean typecheck, confirmed live in Storybook (thumbnail, title, description tag, status tag, and CTA all rendering correctly together).

**Live-testing bug caught by the user: the description/status Tags and CTA Button were stretching full-width instead of hugging their own content** (Figma's Tags/Button are all fixed/hug-sized, never full-width). Root cause: `.content` is a flex column with no explicit `align-items`, so the CSS default (`stretch`) forced every child — including inline-content elements like `Tag` and `Button` that only "hug" when free to size themselves — to match the column's full width. Fixed by adding `align-items: flex-start` to `.content`, with `.header` (which genuinely needs full width so `justify-content: space-between` can push the "···" button to the far edge) given an explicit `width: 100%` to opt back out of the new flex-start default. Re-verified live: description tag, status tag, and CTA button all now hug their text exactly as in Figma.

### Widget Type Card — Figma token fixes only, code deferred (2026-09-02)

Re-pulled fresh per standing rule. This component turned out to have a much larger version of the "wrong token family" bug already seen everywhere else: **a systematic remote-binding problem, not just wrong-family bindings.** A recursive scan checking `remote` on every padding/gap/radius/color binding (the "new standing check" the 2026-08-21 entry called for, now actually run in full for the first time) found **18 properties bound to remote/foreign variables** that happened to share naming conventions with local tokens (`spacing/gap/sm`, `spacing/padding/lg`, `border/radius/md`, etc.) closely enough to look correct at a glance — the exact failure mode flagged as a risk last time ("a remote instance can still have locally-bound-*looking* overrides"), now confirmed to extend to variable bindings generally, not just instance `mainComponent`s.

Fixed all remote bindings on the card's own layout (top-level padding/gap/radius, the left column's padding, and both inner frames' gaps) by rebinding to the local token matching each property's actual measured pixel value — zero visual regression, confirmed via before/after pixel checks. Left two properties (a 2px row gap and a 2px icon-frame padding) genuinely unbound — no token in the spacing scale goes below `spacing/gap/100` (4px), and doubling a barely-visible 2px value isn't worth forcing a mismatched token onto it. Deliberately did **not** touch the nested `Widget types` preview instance's own internal remote bindings (7 of the 18) — that's a separate, decorative widget-preview component family, treated the same as Template Card's illustration (out of scope for a card-level token audit).

**Code was written, then explicitly deferred by the user** ("delete the widget type card for now we didn't finalize that... let it stay in Figma... drop from code and storybook") — the Figma-side token fixes above stand on their own merit and were kept; the React component itself was removed from `src/components/` pending a decision on the design.

### Foundations pages added to Storybook — Colors, Typography, Spacing, Breakpoints & Grids (2026-09-02)

User asked whether the raw color/type/spacing values were browsable anywhere in Storybook — they weren't; every story so far was component-scoped, with nothing surfacing the token palette itself. Built four new "Foundations" pages reading live from the actual generated CSS (`tokens.css`/`typography.css`), plus the usage guidance from three Figma guide pages (`Typography guide`, `Spacing & layout guide`, `Breakpoints & Grids guide`) that had never been pulled into code at all — confirmed as standard practice by checking how comparable systems (Material, Carbon, Polaris) structure their own Foundations docs, which pair raw values with usage rules rather than showing swatches alone.

**A real, non-obvious bug surfaced while building these: Storybook's MDX docs renderer disallows React hooks entirely in any component it embeds**, not just Storybook-specific ones — confirmed via an actual thrown error ("Storybook preview hooks can only be called inside decorators and story functions... useEffect, useRef, useMemo, useCallback, useReducer"), not assumed. The first build used plain `.mdx` files with a `<ColorSwatch>` component that read `getComputedStyle` reactively via `useEffect`/`useState` (to stay in sync with the theme toolbar) — this threw the instant the toolbar was toggled, because MDX prose renders outside the story/decorator context that hooks require. Tried a channel-based workaround (`storybook/preview-api`'s `addons.getChannel()`, listening for `SET_GLOBALS`/`GLOBALS_UPDATED` directly instead of the hook-based `useGlobals()`) — still failed, because the restriction turned out to cover plain React hooks too, not just Storybook's own. **Fixed by converting all four pages from `.mdx` to plain CSF3 `.stories.tsx` files** (a `render`-style component + `tags: ['autodocs']` for the Docs tab) — this runs through the same real story/decorator pipeline every component story already uses, so the existing theme-toggle decorator in `preview.tsx` (built earlier this session) applies normally and the original hook-based `useThemeTick` (MutationObserver on `data-theme` + a `prefers-color-scheme` listener) works without modification. Verified by toggling `?globals=theme:dark` on the Colors page and reading the live computed values via `javascript_exec` — `--content-default` correctly flipped from `rgb(10 10 10)` to `rgb(255 255 255)`.

**Colors** (`src/stories/Foundations/Colors.stories.tsx`): every semantic color token grouped by family (Content, Background/Surface, Background/Interactive, Background/Status, Background/Action, Border, Brand, Chart), each swatch showing the live resolved value plus its usage label straight from the tokens (e.g. `background-surface-raised` → "Cards, panels, modals, menus"). **Typography** (`Typography.stories.tsx`): the real Default-Comfy/Tight and Accent-Tight/Comfy tokens rendered at true size/weight/line-height, plus the Figma Typography guide's heading hierarchy rules (one H1 per page, never skip levels, the "compact ladder" concept) and the real H1–H6 desktop/mobile size table (40/28 → 16/14px) — flagged explicitly as "not yet built as code components/tokens" since no Heading component exists yet. **Spacing** (`Spacing.stories.tsx`): the padding/gap/radius/icon-size scales, plus the Spacing & Layout guide's binding discipline rule, the component-internal-vs-layout-level split (100–300 vs 400–800), the 768px responsive-halving rule, and the full "which token for which layout role" table (8 rows, inline rhythm through content padding). **Breakpoints & Grids** (`Breakpoints.stories.tsx`, new): the 12-column-grid rationale, the 5-breakpoint grid table (columns/gutter/margin/widget-span/notes for Ultra wide through Mobile), the per-breakpoint element-scale table (outer margins/corner radius/H3 size), and the sidebar nav component spec (collapsed/expanded width, docking vs. drawer behavior).

Every table/rule was cross-checked against the raw text pulled directly from the three Figma guide pages (not reconstructed from memory) before writing, per an explicit request to avoid hallucinating details — verbatim where quoted, and only lightly compressed in a couple of connecting sentences with no facts or numbers altered. Verified: 105/105 tests passing, clean typecheck, all four pages confirmed live in both Docs and Canvas view.

User compared our Foundations pages against Vercel's Geist design system docs (screenshots) — Geist uses clean single-line rows (swatch + name on the left, muted description on the right) with generous padding and a simple divider between rows, versus our first pass which stacked name/label/resolved-value in three lines under each swatch, feeling cluttered by comparison.

### Figma Code Connect wired up for Button, as a first test case (2026-09-02)

User asked what the process is for pushing a future Figma change to Button into code — answered that token-level changes propagate via `tokens.css` regeneration automatically, but structural/behavioral changes still need the same manual "pull, diff, edit, verify" pass done all session; offered Code Connect as a way to formalize the Figma↔code mapping so Figma's inspect panel can show real code and flag drift. User asked to set it up for Button as a trial.

Followed the `figma-code-connect` skill's parserless template format (`.figma.ts` + `` figma.code`` ``, not the deprecated `.figma.tsx` + `figma.connect()` parser-based format — v2 of `@figma/code-connect`, installed as a new dev dependency, no longer supports the parser-based format at all). Added `"@figma/code-connect/figma-types"` to `tsconfig.app.json`'s `types` array and a root `figma.config.json` (`{ codeConnect: { include: ["src/**/*.figma.ts"] } }`, deliberately no `parser` key — that's what keeps it parserless rather than falling back to the auto-detected React parser).

**Button/Action's real Figma properties** (`Type`: Primary/Secondary/Tertiary/Ghost/Danger, `Size`: Small/Medium/Large, `State`: Default/Hover/Pressed/Focus/Inactive, plus `Leading icon`/`Trailing icon` booleans) were mapped against `Button.tsx`'s actual props (`type`, `size`, `disabled`, `leadingIcon`, `trailingIcon`, `children`) in `src/components/Button/Button.figma.ts`. Two things had no direct 1:1 prop and were handled deliberately rather than guessed: (1) `State`'s Hover/Pressed/Focus values are real CSS pseudo-classes (`:hover`/`:active`/`:focus-visible` in `Button.module.css`), not settable props — only `Inactive` maps to a real prop (`disabled`); the other three are omitted rather than invented. (2) Both icon slots share the identical layer name "Sparkle" in Figma with no path to tell them apart by name — resolved via `findLayers` + document order (first match = leading, second = trailing) instead of a fragile single `findInstance('Sparkle')` call that would only ever find one of them.

**Validated three ways**, none of them assumed: `tsc -b --noEmit` confirmed the `figma` module import and all `instance.*` API calls type-check cleanly against the installed package's own types (same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors as before, nothing new); `vitest` confirmed the new file doesn't interfere with the existing 105-test suite; and `npx figma connect publish --dry-run` from the project root correctly found the config, parsed `Button.figma.ts`, and resolved it to "Button" pointing at the right Figma URL/node — stopping cleanly at "couldn't find a Figma access token," which is the correct, expected stopping point since providing that token is the user's own action, not something to be entered on their behalf. **Not done**: actually publishing to Figma (`figma connect publish --token <token>` or a `FIGMA_ACCESS_TOKEN` env var/`.env` file, set up by the user) — that's the one remaining manual step to make the mapping live in Figma's inspect panel.

User separately asked whether the Figma library needs publishing first for this to work at all — checked directly rather than assuming: `Button/Action`'s component-set node has a real, non-empty `key` (`remote: false`) in the Plugin API, which is the signature of a component already published to a library at least once (unpublished components have an empty key). So "Gist Foundations" has already been published as a library, likely before this session — the token is genuinely the only remaining blocker.

### Button/Icon focus ring: initially misread as a gap, corrected after checking effects (not just strokes) (2026-09-02)

Re-pulling Button/Icon fresh (150 variants: 5 types × 3 sizes × 2 shapes × 5 states) surfaced one clean structural result and one self-correction. **Clean**: 0 Round/Square mismatches across all matched Type/Size/State pairs — full shape parity. **Also clean, once cross-checked against Button/Action's own already-shipped Focus treatment**: Primary/Danger/Tertiary's Focus-state stroke binds to `background/surface/page` (an off-white background token, not a border token) at `strokeWeight: 2` — my first read flagged this as a wrong-token-family bug, but Button/Action (already coded, audited weeks earlier) uses the exact same token for the exact same purpose, and the user confirmed directly: it's a deliberate Figma workaround for drawing a two-layer focus ring (an inner pale "gap" stroke plus an outer visible ring via a second effect/shadow), not achievable any other way in Figma. `Button.module.css`'s own `:focus-visible` rule already mirrors this exact pattern in code (`box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px var(--background-surface-page), 0 0 0 4px var(--button-focus-ring)`), confirming it's intentional, established convention — not something to "fix."

**Self-correction, caught before it shipped anywhere:** having found Secondary's Focus stroke unchanged from Default (`border/strong`, weight 1) and Ghost/Danger/Tertiary's Focus stroke entirely unbound (`strokeVar: null`), I initially concluded 3 of 5 types had zero visible keyboard-focus affordance — a real accessibility gap. That was wrong because I'd only inspected `strokes`, not `effects`. Checking effects on the same Focus variants showed **all 5 types have a real, visible `DROP_SHADOW` at `spread: 2`** — `border/focus` for Primary/Secondary/Ghost/Tertiary, and `border/status-error` for Danger — exactly matching `Button.module.css`'s existing focus-ring tokens per type. The visible ring comes from that effect layer, not the stroke; the unbound/inert strokes were a red herring, same "leftover inert value" pattern seen elsewhere this session, not a real gap. No fix needed — the design is fully consistent with Button/Action's code convention across all 5 types and both shapes.

### Two real cross-cutting bugs found and fixed while building Button/Icon: missing global font-family, and an incomplete typography scale (2026-09-02)

**Bug 1 — `base.css` never set `font-family` on `body` at all.** User reported the Foundations pages "look not as professional... the fonts you use are serifs" after an earlier layout-only pass (row/divider cleanup) failed to address the actual root cause. Checked `base.css` directly: it sets `background-color` and `color` from tokens but never `font-family` — so any plain text outside a component's own CSS module (every heading/paragraph/list/table cell hand-written in the Foundations pages, plus implicitly anywhere else in the app relying on inherited body font) fell back to the browser's default serif, while only `TypeSample`'s literal inline `fontFamily` style happened to render correctly. This was latent since `base.css` was first written and not specific to Foundations — it would affect any unstyled text anywhere in the app. Fixed with one line: `font-family: var(--font-family-default)` on `body`.

**Bug 2 — `typography.css` was missing real, larger text styles that already exist in Figma.** User separately asked whether the type scale's line-heights included a ~110% step, recalling something not currently in the 12 tokens captured earlier. Pulled every real local text style from Figma via `getLocalTextStylesAsync()` (not just the styles already referenced by built components) and found `Accent/Tight/Desktop` and `Accent/Comfy/Desktop` both continue well past step 400 — up through step **900** (40px), tightening from 140%/160% down to **110%/122%** at the largest sizes — plus a `/100` step (10px) on `Default/Tight` and `Default/Comfy` that was also never captured. Cross-checked against the Breakpoints & Grids page's H1–H6 desktop sizes (40/36/28/24/18/16) logged earlier this session: they match `Accent/Tight/Desktop` steps 900/800/700/600/500/400 exactly — meaning the "heading hierarchy" referenced in the Typography guide isn't a separate, unbuilt scale at all, it's this same family carried up to display sizes, just never fully captured. Added all 11 missing tokens to `typography.css` (rounding Figma's floating-point percentages — e.g. `139.9999976158142%` — to their clean intended values: 140%, 128%, 120%, 113%, 110%, 142%, 133%, 125%, 122%) and rewrote the Foundations Typography page's "Heading hierarchy" section to render real, live `H1–H6` samples off the new tokens instead of a static placeholder table marked "not yet built as code tokens."

**Foundations layout polish, addressing the "treat the entire layout well" feedback directly**: made `ColorRow`'s left/right groups properly shrink and truncate (`min-width: 0`, `text-overflow: ellipsis`, a `max-width: 35%` cap on the description) instead of wrapping awkwardly at moderate content widths — caught by deliberately testing at a narrower viewport rather than only the wide one used for the original screenshots. Re-verified after every change in this pass: 105/105 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live via `javascript_exec` reading computed CSS variable values directly (not just eyeballing screenshots, which turned out to show a stale pre-HMR render at one point during verification).

### Full-system accuracy re-audit, triggered by finding my own "0 mismatches" script had a real bug (2026-09-02)

While auditing Button/Icon, a Round-vs-Square parity check I'd written earlier claimed "0 mismatches" across all matched pairs — but a fresh, independent spot-check found Round's `cornerRadius` is genuinely `32` and Square's is `4`, a real difference the script should have caught. The bug: the script only compared pairs where `bySig[sig].Round && bySig[sig].Square` both existed, but never logged *how many* pairs actually satisfied that condition — so "0 mismatches" could mean "0 real comparisons ran" just as easily as "compared and found equal." Given this, and given the user's explicit request to check for hallucination/inaccuracy everywhere (not just this one spot) after the earlier missed typography steps, ran a full, independent re-audit of every shipped component plus `tokens.css`/`typography.css`, this time requiring every comparison script to report its actual comparison count, not just a boolean.

**Method:** dispatched 6 parallel agents (each re-pulling fresh Figma data — never trusting prior session notes — and diffing against the actual current code), covering all 14 built components: Button+Tag, Radio+Checkbox(+Indicators), Input+InputHeading, the 3 Pagination components, Alert+TemplateCard, and Menu+MenuItem+Divider. Personally handled `tokens.css` (100 semantic variables, resolved fresh in both Light/Dark mode, diffed programmatically against the CSS file — 75 color vars × 2 modes + 25 floats = 175 individual value checks, 0 missing, 0 mismatches) and `typography.css` (all 24 real Desktop text styles pulled fresh via `getLocalTextStylesAsync()`, diffed programmatically — found one more real gap even after the earlier fix pass: `Default/Tight/Desktop/500` (18px) existed in Figma but had never been captured; added it, re-diffed, 24/24 clean) plus a fresh re-verification of the Spacing/Typography/Breakpoints Foundations guide content against newly-pulled raw text (all confirmed accurate, no drift).

**Confirmed real bugs found and fixed (10 total):**
- **Checkbox**: `align-items: flex-start` didn't match Figma's current `counterAxisAlignItems: CENTER` (consistent across all 6 states) — the Figma design apparently changed again since an earlier session fix set it to flex-start; fixed to `center` per the live file, not the old note.
- **Input**: (1) `.tertiary` zeroed `padding-left/right` but Figma keeps 12px on all 24 Tertiary variants — removed the override. (2) The shared disabled-border rule applied to all types, but Figma's Secondary/Inactive has `strokes.length === 0` (no border at all) — scoped the rule with `:not(.secondary)`.
- **Button**: Ghost's Figma Focus state has no inner stroke (only Primary/Secondary/Tertiary/Danger get the white "gap" stroke) — added a `.ghost:focus-visible` override with a single ring, no gap layer. (A separately-flagged "4px spread vs Figma's 2px" claim was investigated and found to be a **false positive** — CSS `box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px A, 0 0 0 4px B` is the standard two-ring offset technique, where each layer is independently 2px thick; the code was already correct and was left unchanged. Also flagged but left as-is: a Figma-side text-fill token-naming inconsistency between Small vs Medium/Large Button variants that resolves to the identical rendered color either way — nothing for code to fix.)
- **TemplateCard**: (1) Title used `--text-accent-tight-400` (16px/0.02em) but Figma's real text style is `Accent/Tight/Desktop/500` (18px/0 letter-spacing) — fixed, including adding the missing `letter-spacing` property. (2) Thumbnail background was a flat `--background-surface-sunken` fill, but Figma's real fill is a `GRADIENT_LINEAR` from `brand/inactive` to `brand/quinary` — derived the exact direction from Figma's gradient transform matrix (a pure vertical vector, top→bottom) rather than guessing, and replaced the flat fill with `linear-gradient(180deg, var(--brand-inactive) 0%, var(--brand-quinary) 100%)`.
- **PaginationDropdown**: (1) Active state's border stayed 1px in code, but Figma's Active is `strokeWeight: 2` on all 3 sizes — fixed, and added `box-sizing: border-box` alongside it so the thicker border doesn't grow the box (same class of bug as the earlier CheckboxIndicator content-box fix). (2) The chevron never flipped direction — Figma's Active variants use an upward caret; code always rendered `CaretDown` regardless of state — now renders `CaretUp` when active.
- **PaginationPage**: the "More" ellipsis button shared its numbered-page sibling's width (24/36/48 per size), but Figma gives it distinct, narrower fixed widths (22/32/44) at every size — added `.more.small/.medium/.large` overrides.
- **Alert and Menu/MenuItem/Divider**: fully clean — 26 and 93 property comparisons respectively, 0 discrepancies in either, including an independently recomputed Dark-mode contrast check on all 4 Alert border colors (6.10–11.04:1, comfortably above 3:1).

### PaginationDropdown was a static shell with no real dropdown behavior — user caught it, not the audit (2026-09-02)

None of the 6 audit agents were asked to check *interaction behavior*, only Figma-vs-CSS property fidelity — so the audit above passed PaginationDropdown as visually correct while missing that clicking it did nothing. The user caught this directly: "when we click on it it doesn't seem to actually open into the drawer dropdown." This traced back to an explicit, but not clearly communicated, scope decision made earlier in the session ("I'll skip Pagination Menu Item and Pagination Dropdown Results... since that would essentially require a full custom Select/Menu overlay component") — the trigger button was built and styled, but the actual list it should open was never built at all, and the trigger had no open/close state or click handler.

Built the missing pieces properly: **`PaginationMenuItem`** (new — a single selectable row with a checkmark that shows only when `selected`; pulled Figma's real "Pagination Menu Item" set fresh, including finding the checkmark is a `VECTOR` named "Check" with `opacity: 0/1` toggling visibility rather than a swapped icon instance — an easy thing to miss with an instance-only search, which is exactly how the very first pass at this same pattern missed it too) and **`PaginationDropdownResults`** (new — the raised list container, sized 104/116/116px per size tier, matching Figma exactly). Rewrote `PaginationDropdown` itself from a stateless visual trigger into a real, self-contained combobox: manages its own `open` state, closes on outside-click or Escape (`pointerdown`/`keydown` listeners scoped to when open), and takes `options`/`value`/`onChange` instead of a static `children` label. This is a breaking API change from the version built minutes earlier in this same session — justified because the old API was fundamentally non-functional, not because of a design change.

Verified live in the browser (not just via the play-function test, which passed but is worth confirming isn't the only evidence): clicked the trigger → list opened with a checkmark on "10" and the border/chevron both switching to their Active treatment; clicked "50" → list closed, trigger updated to "50", border and chevron reverted to Default. 104/104 tests passing (one net test-count change from replacing the old static `ActiveOpen`/`ClickInteraction` stories with a real `OpenAndSelect` interaction test), clean typecheck.

### IconButton — split from Button.module.css's shared type/color tokens (2026-09-02)

User asked whether "Button/Icon" should really be a separate component from Button, given it's visually just an icon-only, differently-shaped variant. Answered directly: structurally it's not "Button minus text" — it's aspect-ratio-locked (width=height) rather than label-hugging, has a `Shape` axis (round/square) Button doesn't have, and needs a mandatory `aria-label` since there's no visible text. Recommended keeping them as two components (matching Radix/Chakra/Material precedent, and Figma's own separate component sets) but sharing the per-type color-token mapping so it isn't duplicated — user agreed, and separately confirmed the code name should be `IconButton` (Figma's `Button/Icon` slash-naming is Figma's own organizational convention, same reason `Button/Action` became just `Button` earlier).

Extracted the 5 type blocks (`.primary/.secondary/.tertiary/.ghost/.danger`, each just a bundle of `--button-bg/-fg/-border/-focus-ring` custom properties) out of `Button.module.css` into a new shared `src/styles/button-types.module.css`; both `Button.module.css` and the new `IconButton.module.css` now `composes: <type> from '../../styles/button-types.module.css'` for each type class, rather than duplicating the color mapping. Verified Button still renders identically after the refactor (screenshotted `AllTypes` — pixel-identical to before).

Built `IconButton` (`type`/`size`/`shape`/`icon`/required `aria-label`) reusing the same size scale as Button (32/40/48) but square instead of label-hugging, `Round` bound to `--border-radius-xl` (32px, not a magic "make it circular" value — confirmed this literally is the real token Figma uses) and `Square` to `--border-radius-sm` (4px). Reused Button's exact focus-ring CSS including the Ghost single-outer-ring special case verified earlier this session. Confirmed live via keyboard focus that Ghost still gets exactly one ring (`boxShadow: rgb(8, 8, 9) 0px 0px 0px 2px`, no second layer). 116/116 tests passing, clean typecheck.

### Avatar + AvatarGroup (2026-09-02)

Fresh Figma pull surfaced a real, non-obvious structural question worth resolving before coding rather than guessing: Avatar's two variants ("placeholder"/"avatar") looked structurally identical at the variant-properties level — same children, same visibility — until checking each child's actual `fills` showed the only real difference is whether the "image 1" rectangle has an `IMAGE` fill or none at all. Also found, by checking `componentPropertyDefinitions` (not just `variantProperties`, which had missed this pattern once before) — two real boolean properties, `gitAvatar` and `withIcon`, both defaulting to `true`, controlling a status-icon badge (top-right) and a git-provider badge (bottom-left). The badge slots are genuinely swappable (`_Icon` has a real `INSTANCE_SWAP` property), just defaulting to the same GitHub icon as the git badge in Figma's own example instance — not a duplication bug, just a coincidental shared default.

**Avatar Group's "+N" overflow circle and every Avatar instance inside a Group are uniformly resized to 30px** in Figma (vs. Avatar's own standalone 24px default) — no formal `Size` variant exists on Avatar itself, this is just a per-instance override applied consistently across the Group's example. Rather than inventing a named size scale Figma doesn't have, gave `Avatar` a plain numeric `size` prop (default 24) and had `AvatarGroup` pass `size={30}` to match Figma's real demonstrated usage.

**One invented detail caught and removed before it shipped**: initially added a page-color separator ring around each avatar in the group (`border: 1px solid var(--background-surface-page)`) as a common "avatar stack" convention borrowed from other design systems — then explicitly checked Figma's actual group-context avatar instances and found no such ring; they use the exact same plain `border/default` 0.5px stroke as standalone. Removed the invented rule rather than leaving an unverified assumption in the code.

**A real bug found and fixed during live verification, in the test fixture rather than the component**: the `Large` Avatar story initially rendered as a plain solid black circle instead of a photo. Traced it methodically — confirmed via DOM inspection that the `<img>`, its `src`, and both badge `<span>`s were all structurally correct and `img.complete === true`, ruling out the component itself. The actual bug was in the story's own inline SVG data URI: it manually wrote `%23` (a pre-encoded `#`) inside the SVG string *before* passing the whole thing through `encodeURIComponent`, which re-encoded the `%` itself into `%25`, producing a double-encoded `%2523` that the browser only decodes once — leaving the SVG's `fill` attributes literally reading `fill="%23c8dcf9"` (invalid), so every shape fell back to SVG's default black fill. Fixed by using a literal `#` in the source string and letting `encodeURIComponent` encode it exactly once.

Verified: 124/124 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live — photo renders correctly, both corner badges positioned and sized correctly via `getBoundingClientRect()` (not just eyeballed), `AvatarGroup`'s overlap and "+N" overflow circle both render correctly for both the 6-avatar and 4-placeholder cases.

### Avatar simplified — `icon`/`gitBadge` removed, product doesn't need them (2026-09-02)

User clarified the actual product use for Avatar is closer to a plain circular favicon — not a user-profile-photo-with-git-provider-badge pattern, which is what Figma's own example instance happened to demonstrate. Rather than keep unused capability around "just in case" (the exact kind of speculative complexity this session has otherwise avoided), removed `icon`/`gitBadge` props, their corner-badge CSS, and the two stories demonstrating them from `Avatar`. `AvatarGroup` needed no changes — its `avatars` prop type derives from `Omit<AvatarProps, 'size'>`, so it automatically narrowed to just `{src, alt, className}` once `Avatar`'s own props shrank. `Avatar` is now `src`/`alt`/`size` only. Verified: 122/122 tests passing (down 2, matching the two removed stories), clean typecheck, confirmed live — plain photo circle renders with no badges.

### TabToggle, TabToggleGroup, ProgressTab, ProgressTabGroup — new page, thorough audit before any code (2026-09-02)

New `-> Tabs` page (added since the last full-file component survey), containing `TabToggle` (3 states: Default/Hover/Selected), `TabToggleGroup` (3 sizes), and `.ProgressTab` (6 variants: 3 Progress steps × 2 States) + a `ProgressTabGroup` example. Followed the full standing process end to end (token guide, spacing guide, typography guide, corner radius, icons) before writing any code, per explicit request — real, substantive findings surfaced at every layer.

**A genuinely invisible data-integrity bug**: two different variables in this file are both literally named `background/interactive/selected` — one live (`4183:367`, the one used correctly everywhere else, e.g. PaginationPage), one orphaned (`39:16`, absent from `getLocalVariablesAsync()`'s live list but still resolvable by ID). `TabToggle`'s Hover fill and `TabToggleGroup`'s own track background were both bound to the **stale** one. Since Figma's own properties panel shows both by the same name, this is only catchable by comparing underlying variable IDs, not by eyeballing bindings — the same category of subtlety as the earlier "0 mismatches" script bug, just at the data layer instead of the tooling layer.

**Remote/foreign-library bindings**, the same "wrong source" pattern flagged repeatedly this session: `TabToggle` Default/Hover text → remote `button/content/secondary` (real local equivalent: `content/secondary`); `TabToggle` Selected fill → remote `background/info/primary`, and semantically wrong on top of being remote (an "info" status color for a plain selected-tab background); `.ProgressTab`'s "Current" step → remote `button/background/primary` + `button/content/primary`. The *design intent* behind that last one was actually sound — current step gets the same bold treatment as a Primary button — just wrongly sourced; mapped it to the real local `background/action/primary-default` + `background/action/primary-content` pairing already used for Button's own Primary type.

**A real functional gap, not just a token issue**: `TabToggleGroup`'s Small/Medium/Large variants resize the outer box (24/32/40px) but the nested tab content stays pinned to the remote `.TabItem`'s "Size=Small" variant every time — Medium/Large just add empty padding around identical 14px text rather than genuinely scaling. Confirmed by checking the actual nested instance's resolved main-component name, not assumed from the outer box size alone.

**Judgment calls, delegated back and decided with reasoning, not by default:**
- *Border opacity* — `border/interactive-strong` was applied at a manual 10% paint opacity on both components, unlike every other use of that token elsewhere (always full opacity). This system bakes translucency into the token itself when intended (e.g. `background/interactive/hover` already has 5% baked in), never as an ad-hoc paint-level override on an otherwise-opaque token — so rebound to `border/default` at 100%, the same plain-boundary token every other card-like container (Menu, TemplateCard, PaginationDropdownResults) already uses.
- *Missing states* — the remote `.TabItem` has real Disabled and Focused variants, plus Icon/Badge slots, none of which `TabToggle` exposed. Added real keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`, matching the same double-ring pattern used by Button/IconButton/Input) and Disabled (matching the plain `content-disabled` + `not-allowed` convention used everywhere else) to the code. Deliberately skipped Icon/Badge — speculative capability mirroring the remote library's demo with no stated product need, the same call just made on Avatar's git badge.
- *Corner radius* — `TabToggle`'s own pill was 4px (sm) nested inside an 8px (md) group track; `.ProgressTab`'s individual item was 8px instead. Checked for existing precedent rather than picking arbitrarily: `PaginationDropdownResults` already nests 4px rows inside an 8px container, confirming TabToggle's own convention is the one to keep — unified `.ProgressTab`'s item to 4px (sm) to match, not the other way around.
- *Selected tab's fill* — user pushed back live after seeing it rendered ("i dont like the beige"). Initially bound to `background/interactive/selected` (a pale cream tint) matching PaginationPage's own Active-state precedent; reconsidered given `TabToggleGroup`'s track is `background/surface/sunken` — a genuine sunken-track-plus-raised-selected-chip segmented control (iOS/GitHub-style) reads better with a true `background/surface/raised` chip than a tinted one, which doesn't contrast as clearly against a sunken well. Switched both the Figma binding and the code to `background/surface/raised`.

**Sizing built to match the system, not the remote component's own native scale**: the remote `.TabItem`'s real Size variants use 36/40/56px heights and a 14/16/18px type scale — but 36 and 56 don't match this system's own established 32/40/48 height convention (Button, Input, IconButton, PaginationDropdown all use exactly this scale). Per explicit instruction that everything in the file should read as one cohesive system, snapped `TabToggle`'s three sizes to 32/40/48 instead of preserving the remote component's own slightly-off native heights, while keeping the real local typography steps that do line up cleanly (`Accent/Tight/Desktop/300` at 14px, `/400` at 16px, `/500` at 18px, one real token per size tier).

Coded all four: `TabToggle` (`size`, `selected`, native `disabled`, real `role="tab"`/`aria-selected`), `TabToggleGroup` (auto-propagates its own `size` to every `TabToggle` child via `cloneElement`, `role="tablist"`), `ProgressTab` (`progress`: default/current/complete), `ProgressTabGroup` (plain flex row). Verified: 135/135 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live — clicking between tabs in `TabToggleGroup` correctly moves the raised-white selected state, `ProgressTabGroup`'s three-tier emphasis ladder (muted default → full-emphasis complete → bold primary current) renders exactly as intended.

**Recurring environment issue, not a code bug**: the Storybook dev server itself was found running with `cwd` resolved to `~/gist-geo-demo` instead of `~/design-system`, throwing an "Unable to index files" error on reload. `.claude/launch.json`'s `storybook` config already uses `npm --prefix /Users/shardikar/design-system`, which is cwd-independent — restarting the server through that config resolved it immediately. Same root cause as an earlier session hiccup where bare shell commands (not using `--prefix`) picked up the wrong directory after an environment reset.

### Two real misses caught by the user after the fact, one genuine token-design gap confirmed (2026-09-02)

User caught two things immediately after the components above shipped, plus asked for a proper Figma-vs-Storybook consistency re-check.

**Miss 1 — `.ProgressTab`'s Complete-state checkmark was never coded.** I'd checked whether `TabToggle`'s icon slot was ever toggled on (it wasn't) and concluded icon support was speculative for the whole Tab family — but never separately checked `.ProgressTab`'s own icon boolean. It was: `↳ Icon#5082:230` is `true` on both Complete variants, pointing at a real `light/check` icon (the old, pre-Phosphor asset library, confirmed by name pattern). Per the user's standing instruction — "phosphor icons! if figma doesn't use that, update figma too" — fixed this in **both** places rather than only in code: swapped Figma's icon instance from `light/check` to the real Phosphor `Check` component (`Format=Stroke, Weight=Regular`, matching Button's own Sparkle icon convention), imported via `search_design_system` + `importComponentSetByKeyAsync`; and added a real `icon` prop to `ProgressTab` (defaulting to Phosphor's `Check` for `progress="complete"`, overridable), sized at the real Figma-measured 20×20 (`--size-icon-md`). Verified both the Figma swap (`remote: true`, correct main component name) and the code (checkmarks visible on both Complete steps in Storybook).

**Miss 2 — the Selected tab's dark-mode background doesn't read as selected.** User's screenshot showed almost no visible contrast between the selected pill and its track in dark mode. Investigated with the same independent-verification technique from earlier this session (bind to a temp node, force Dark mode via `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection`, read Figma's native resolution) — first pass had a bug in my own check (read `color` but not the paint's separate `opacity`, misreporting `background/surface/sunken` dark as solid white); corrected, it resolves to a 5% **white** wash (unlike light mode's 5% *black* wash — the token deliberately flips wash direction per mode). Composited over the dark page (`rgb(20,20,20)`), that lands at roughly `rgb(31,31,31)` — which is nearly identical to `background/surface/raised`'s own dark value, also `rgb(31,31,31)`. **This is a genuine token-pairing gap, not a rendering bug**: confirmed by reading actual computed styles in the browser for both light and dark mode and finding every single value (tab background/border/text, group background/border) matches `tokens.css` exactly in both modes — the code renders the tokens faithfully; the tokens themselves just don't provide enough separation between "sunken" and "raised" specifically in dark mode. No Figma dark-mode preview exists for this page to compare against (confirmed — checked for one), so this was never actually designed or caught before. Flagged to the user as an open item rather than silently picking a different token — the real fix is a design decision (a more distinct dark-mode value for one of the two tokens), not a code change.

**Consistency re-check requested and performed**: cross-checked `TabToggle`'s Selected state and `ProgressTab`'s Current state against `tokens.css` in both Light and Dark mode by reading real computed styles via `javascript_exec` (not just visual comparison) — 7 distinct values checked (tab background/border/text color, group background/border in both modes for TabToggle; background/text color in both modes for ProgressTab), all matching exactly. Combined with the dark-mode finding above, this confirms Storybook is rendering the token system with full fidelity — every discrepancy traced back to either a real miss in scope (the checkmark) or a genuine gap in the tokens themselves (the dark contrast), never a code-vs-token mismatch.

**Follow-up on the dark-mode finding — user pushed back with their own Figma screenshot showing clearly visible contrast**, seemingly contradicting the "genuine token gap, nothing to fix" conclusion above. Re-verified properly instead of re-asserting the same conclusion: duplicated the real `TabToggleGroup` (Small) component onto a temp instance, forced Dark mode on it and every nested child via `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection`, and rendered a real `get_screenshot` of it — which matched the user's own Figma reference almost exactly, confirming Figma itself really does show that contrast. Reading the actual resolved paint values off that same instance showed the fill, opacity, border color/weight, and corner radius are all byte-identical to what's already in `tokens.css` and `TabToggle.module.css`. The visible "box" outline in both images turns out to come mostly from the **border**, not the fill — `rgba(255,255,255,0.1)` composited over the `rgb(31,31,31)` chip works out to roughly `rgb(53,53,53)`, clearly lighter than either the chip or the track, so it reads as an outline even though the two fills really are nearly identical (`rgb(31,31,31)` vs. a composited ~`rgb(32,32,32)`). Conclusion stands, now confirmed by an actual rendered comparison rather than computed-style values alone: no Figma-vs-code discrepancy exists; the earlier report of a flatter-looking Storybook screenshot is most likely a PNG-export-vs-live-browser rendering difference, not a value mismatch.

### TabToggle: icon-only tabs, for cases with no label text at all (2026-09-02)

User showed a mockup of `TabToggleGroup` variants that are icon-only (a people icon, a hamburger-menu icon) — no label text — a real use case `TabToggle` didn't support (it always required `children` as the tab's label). Made `children` optional and added an `icon` prop; icon-only tabs (no `children`) get a square shape (`aspect-ratio: 1`, no horizontal padding) instead of stretching to fill the group, and their icon is sized per tier via the existing `size-icon-sm/md/lg` scale (matching how Button/IconButton already size their own icons per size tier).

**Accessibility**: an icon-only tab has no visible text for a screen reader to announce, so it needs `aria-label`. Tried enforcing this at the type level with a discriminated union (`children` required XOR `icon` + `aria-label` required) — this fought Storybook's CSF3 `Meta`/`StoryObj` typing badly (partial per-story `args` couldn't satisfy either union branch cleanly, a known limitation of Storybook's typing with union component props). Dropped the union in favor of a plain optional-props interface plus a `console.warn` in dev mode when neither `children` nor `aria-label` is present — less compile-time-strict, but avoids fighting the tooling, and still surfaces the mistake immediately during development.

**Also answered directly: how to test a `ReactNode` prop like `icon` live in Storybook.** Storybook's auto-generated Controls can only render pickers for serializable primitives (strings, numbers, booleans, a few known shapes) — a raw `ReactNode` prop has no Controls representation on its own. Built a `PickAnIcon` story demonstrating the standard workaround: a story-only `iconName` arg with a `select` control (`argTypes: { iconName: { control: 'select', options: [...] } }`), whose `render` function maps the selected name to a real icon element and passes it as the actual `icon` prop. Verified live: switching the "iconName" dropdown from "Users" to "Settings" swaps the rendered icon immediately, with no code change needed to test different options.

Verified: 138/138 tests passing, clean typecheck, confirmed live in Storybook (icon-only tab renders as a compact square with just the icon; the icon-picker dropdown swaps the icon live).

### Colleague additions vetted: Haffer Mono text style + Drop Shadow (sm) effect (2026-09-03)

User flagged two additions a colleague made directly in Figma — asked to vet and log, not build into code yet.

**`Label/Label` — a new Haffer Mono text style.** Real, licensed font: confirmed via `listAvailableFontsAsync()` that Figma has both "Haffer Mono" Regular and Medium installed (not a placeholder/missing font). The actual web font files are also already sitting in the repo — `src/assets/fonts/Haffer/HafferMono-{Regular,Medium}.{woff,woff2,ttf,otf}` — but **`fonts.css` has no `@font-face` for them yet**, and no token exists in `typography.css` either, so nothing renders in Haffer Mono in code today; anything referencing it would silently fall back to a generic system monospace. That's a real, known gap, left unaddressed per the "vet and log, don't build" scope of this pass.

Spec: 12px, Regular weight, 0% letter-spacing, `line-height: AUTO` (the font's own natural leading — every other text style in this file specifies an explicit percentage instead, so this is a deliberate one-off, not an oversight to "fix"). The style's own name doesn't follow the file's established `Category/SubCategory/Platform/Step` convention (e.g. `Accent/Tight/Desktop/300`) — reasonable, since it's a different font family serving a narrow, separate purpose, not another step in the main scale. **Its own description is the important part and should carry forward wherever this gets used**: *"Reserved for tiny eyebrow caps inside spec cards. In product UI, mono is rare — almost an Easter egg."* — i.e. deliberately rare/narrow-scoped, not a general-purpose label or body option.

**`Drop Shadow (sm)`.** DROP_SHADOW, radius 8, spread 0, offset (0, 2), color `rgba(0,0,0,0.10)`. Auditing it surfaced that a whole small effects-style library already existed in this file that had never been captured or cross-checked before: `Drop Shadow (lg)` (radius 22, offset y:8, opacity 0.15), `Shadow and Glass` (a GLASS+DROP_SHADOW combo, radius 16, offset y:4, opacity 0.15), and `Focus`/`Focus-danger`. The sm/lg pairing's non-linear progression (radius 8→22, offset 2→8, opacity 0.10→0.15) matches standard shadow-scale conventions (Tailwind, Material elevation) — no red flags. None of the three shadow styles bind their color to a variable (all hardcoded black-with-alpha) — true of `sm` too, but that's consistent with the two pre-existing styles, not a new inconsistency the colleague introduced.

**One useful cross-check this surfaced**: `Focus` (spread 2, radius 0, color `rgb(8,8,9)`) and `Focus-danger` (spread 2, color `rgb(197,2,2)`) are the SAME named effect styles that Button/IconButton's own `:focus-visible` box-shadow rings already reproduce in code — confirmed by comparing values directly, not just by name. That code was built earlier this session by reading the raw `DROP_SHADOW` effect straight off individual component instances, never by referencing these named styles — so this is independent confirmation the two approaches agree, not a discrepancy.

`Drop Shadow (sm)` itself isn't referenced by any component built so far (nothing in this system currently has an elevation shadow — Menu, PaginationDropdownResults, and TabToggleGroup's dropdown are all flat, border-only surfaces today). Logged as available and ready for a future component that needs one; not wired into `tokens.css` or any component in this pass.

### Both colleague additions wired in for real, plus a real bug caught while doing it (2026-09-03)

User asked to actually make both available rather than just logged — "feel the tokens should be available there... dropshadows in general should go into effects in documentation."

**Haffer Mono**: added the two `@font-face` blocks to `fonts.css` (pointing at the already-present `HafferMono-{Regular,Medium}.woff2/.woff` files) and a new `--font-family-mono` alongside the existing `--font-family-default`. Added `--text-mono-label-*` to `typography.css` (12px/400/`normal` line-height/0 letter-spacing, matching Figma's `Label/Label` exactly — `line-height: normal` is CSS's real equivalent of Figma's "Auto", not a guessed percentage), carrying its "rare, spec-card eyebrow caps only" description forward as a comment. Added a new "Mono" section to the Foundations Typography page.

**Effects**: new hand-maintained `src/styles/effects.css` (same non-auto-generated pattern as `base.css`, since effect styles aren't part of the "semantic" variable collection `tokens.css` pulls from) with `--shadow-sm`/`--shadow-lg` as real CSS `box-shadow` values converted directly from Figma's offset/radius/spread/color, and `--shadow-focus`/`--shadow-focus-danger`. The focus pair is deliberately **not** a literal copy of the effect style's own hardcoded RGBA — that raw value is mode-blind (only ever the Light-mode color), so instead they reference `var(--border-focus)`/`var(--border-status-error)` directly, the same real, theme-aware variables Button/IconButton's own focus rings already use, so they correctly flip color in Dark mode instead of silently staying wrong. Imported `effects.css` in both entry points (`main.tsx`, `.storybook/preview.tsx`), per the standing two-entry-point rule. New `Foundations/Effects` page (`Effects.stories.tsx`) with a new `ShadowRow` display component, showing both shadow tokens as rendered boxes plus the two focus rings as the shared reference other components already implement inline.
