**Real bug caught in the process, unrelated to either addition**: `TypeSample` (the shared component every Foundations Typography row uses) hardcoded `fontFamily: 'var(--font-family-default)'` in its inline style instead of reading each token's own `--{name}-font-family` value — meaning the Mono sample would have silently rendered in Haffer (the default sans), not Haffer Mono, defeating the entire point of adding it. Fixed to read the real per-token font-family. This was a latent bug in every *previous* Typography row too, just invisible until now because every earlier token happened to already point at `--font-family-default` — the mono token was the first one that didn't.

Verified: 139/139 tests passing, clean typecheck. Confirmed live: `document.fonts` shows Haffer Mono (weight 400) with `status: "loaded"` — the real font file, not a system fallback — and the rendered sample's computed `font-family` matches exactly. Both shadow tokens and both focus-ring tokens render correctly on the new Effects page with live values shown.

### Report Card family — new page, "Figma first" (2026-09-03)

User asked for a family of clean dashboard/report cards ("Report frames" page, later renamed by a colleague to "Report frames (WIP)"), benchmarked against 5 reference screenshots of a competitor product (Visibility Trend line chart, Visibility Score donut, Competitor Ranking numbered list, Query Coverage Gaps data table, plus a richer dashboard-widget panel — the last one deliberately not built, out of scope for this pass). Explicit scope: Figma only this round, chart bodies are placeholders/slots, structure and token discipline are what matters, "should be nicely in autolayout too — easy to work with."

**Shared atoms built first, reused across every card**: `Card Header` (Title + Description, both slots forced `visible: true` on the master — see the visible:false quirk below), `Stat Display` (label + big-number + delta, baseline-aligned), `Legend Item` (color dot + label), `Chart Slot` (a flat placeholder frame, 5%-opacity `background/surface/sunken` fill, `border/radius/md`, literally says "Chart slot" — swap-in point for a real chart later).

**Figma Plugin API quirk newly discovered and worth remembering**: a master component's child with `visible: false` is silently **omitted from every fresh instance's own `.children` array** — confirmed by an isolated A/B test (identical setup at `visible: true` correctly includes the child). This broke `Card Header`'s Description and `Ranked List Row`'s Badge (Tag instance), both designed "hidden by default, toggle on per instance." Fixed by inverting the pattern: optional slots default `visible: true` on the master, individual instances that don't want them get `.visible = false` set per-instance instead.

**Second quirk**: `figma.appendChild()` refuses to add new children into a component INSTANCE (`"Cannot move node. New parent is an instance or is inside of an instance"`) — instances only accept overriding existing children, never adding new ones. So every composed card (`Ranking Card`, `Data Table Card`, `Score Card`) is built as a **plain auto-layout frame styled identically to `Trend Card`**, not an instance of a shared "container" component, with the smaller reusable atoms (`Card Header`, row components, `Donut Chart Slot`) instanced *into* that plain frame. Confirmed this is the intentional pattern going forward for any card whose body content varies per use.

**`Ranked List Row`** (new component) — Rank/Icon(Avatar-as-favicon)/Name/Badge(Tag, "You")/Spacer/Value/Trend, `HORIZONTAL`, `spacing/padding/300`+`spacing/gap/300`. Assembled into **Ranking Card**: `Card Header` + 4 rows, alternating `background/surface/sunken` zebra fill, "You" tag shown only on the matching row. One real bug caught during assembly: the `Trend` field's text node had a stale `FIXED` 16px width (sized for its original single-character `"-"` placeholder) — real delta values like `"+2.1%"` wrapped across 3 lines inside that box. Fixed by switching it to `layoutSizingHorizontal: 'HUG'` on both the master and all instances.

**`Data Table Row`** (new component) — Query (text, `FILL` width, single-line ellipsis via `maxLines: 1` + `textTruncation: 'ENDING'`, a real Figma text-truncation feature rather than a manual character-count hack) / Models (a mini row of small `Avatar` instances standing in for AI-model icons — no real brand icon assets for this exist in the file yet, deliberately generic) / Avatars (reused `Avatar Group`'s `limited=true` variant directly for the "+N" overflow stack) / Coverage (right-aligned %). Assembled into **Data Table Card** the same way as Ranking Card, same zebra pattern.

**`Donut Chart Slot`** (new component) — a circular (`cornerRadius: 90` on a 180×180 frame) variant of the same `Chart Slot` placeholder fill, with the big-stat/label pair (reusing `Stat Display`'s exact type styles) centered inside it rather than beside it, so it doubles as a real donut-chart center-label placeholder. Assembled into **Score Card**: `Card Header` + centered `Donut Chart Slot` + a `Legend Item` list, one per row's own `chart/categorical/1-4` color bound directly to each legend dot.

**Token audit caught real gaps before they shipped** (user asked directly whether typography/spacing were "as per system" — right to ask, this wasn't yet true): the three new card containers (`Ranking Card`, `Data Table Card`, `Score Card`) had `cornerRadius`/padding/`itemSpacing` set as raw numbers copied from `Trend Card`'s visual values, but never actually bound to the variables `Trend Card` itself uses (`border/radius/lg`, `spacing/padding/600`, `spacing/gap/500`) — fixed, all three now bound the same way. Both row masters' own container fill was raw white with empty `boundVariables` (true of the pre-existing `Ranked List Row` too, not just the new `Data Table Row`) — bound both to `background/surface/raised`, matching the card they sit on. `Data Table Row`'s own padding/gap were also raw — bound to the same `spacing/padding/300`/`spacing/gap/300` tokens `Ranked List Row` already used. The `Models` mini-row's 4px gap was raw — confirmed `spacing/gap/100` resolves to exactly 4px via the alias chain, then bound. Lesson: copying a visual value from a sibling component is not the same as copying its token binding — always check `boundVariables`, not just the resolved number, especially for anything assembled as a plain frame rather than derived from an existing bound component.

Verified each card via `get_screenshot` against its own reference screenshot (Ranking Card, Data Table Card, Score Card all confirmed visually clean and structurally matching). Not yet done: the code-side React/CSS translation (explicitly Figma-only this pass), the Dashboard Widget Card variant (deprioritized, not committed to), and a real brand-icon asset set for `Data Table Row`'s Models slot.

### Table component audit (`Cell`, `.header-more`, `Row`, `Table`) — a colleague addition, vetted before coding (2026-09-04)

User added a Table component (`Cell` — 20 variants across `Type` × `Device`; `.header-more` — the row-actions overflow trigger; plain `Row`/`Table` frames) and asked for the same token/spacing/typography audit rigor before touching code. Full pass, in two rounds: an initial token audit, then — after fixing those — a second "everything" pass (naming, properties, radius, icons) per an explicit follow-up ask.

**Round 1 — token/spacing findings, all fixed:**
- Every one of the 20 `Cell` variants (plus both `.header-more` states and the standalone hover-state `Cell` instance) had their own background fill bound to a **remote** variable from the deprecated `component` collection (`button/background/tertiary`, `remote: true`) — not a local token at all. Resolved its actual color+opacity and confirmed it's byte-identical to local `background/surface/sunken` (`color/darken/100`, 5% opacity); rebound all 21 instances with zero visual change.
- `.header-more`'s `Active=True` fill was bound to `border/interactive-strong` — a border-family token repurposed as a background fill. Found the correct match: `background/interactive/pressed` resolves to the exact same 10% wash. Rebound.
- `Table`'s outer corner radius (4px) was a raw unbound number; matches `border/radius/sm` exactly. Bound it.
- The status dot in `Type=Dot + text` had **two stacked opaque fills** on its inner vector — `brand/tertiary` (light blue) sitting underneath `brand/primary` (dark navy), the top one fully hiding the bottom. Dead paint layer, almost certainly left behind by an in-place recolor that added a new fill instead of replacing the old one. Deleted the dead layer on both Desktop and Mobile variants.
- Three items needed the user's own call rather than a silent fix, since they'd change actual pixel values: **(a)** Desktop `Cell`'s horizontal padding was a raw, off-scale 6px (no step in the 4/8/12 scale matches it) — user chose to snap to `spacing/padding/200` (8px) over `padding/100` (4px), applied to all 10 Desktop variants. **(b)** `Type=Title Active, Device=Mobile` deviated from all 9 sibling Mobile variants (8px vertical padding + a global-primitive-bound 2px gap, vs. everyone else's 12px/`spacing/padding/300`) — user confirmed this was a bug, not intentional (an active sort-header cell rendering at a different height than its inactive sibling would break header-row alignment), fixed to match. **(c)** That same investigation surfaced a genuine gap in the spacing scale: `Type=Dot + text`'s 2px `itemSpacing` had nowhere to bind since the semantic `spacing/gap/*` scale's smallest step is `gap/100` (4px) — both Dot+text and Title Active Mobile were bypassing the semantic scale entirely, binding straight to the global primitive `size/0`. User asked to close the gap properly: added a new `spacing/gap/050` semantic token (same collection/scopes as the rest of the `gap/*` family, aliased to `size/0` = 2px) and rebound both spots to it instead of the global bypass.

**Round 2 — full audit (naming, properties, icons), asked for explicitly after Round 1 fixes landed:**
- **All icons in this component were legacy pre-Phosphor assets** (`light/edit`, `light/trash`, `light/archive`, `light/more-horizontal` ×2 — used twice, once in `Type=Icon`'s action row and once in `.header-more`) — 10 instances total across Desktop/Mobile. Per the standing "Phosphor icons only" rule, imported the real equivalents from the Phosphor Icons (Community) library (`Pencil`, `Trash`, `Archive`, `DotsThree`, all `Format=Stroke, Weight=Regular`) and swapped every instance, matching the legacy icons' exact 16×16 size. The icons' own stroke color was ALSO bound to a remote deprecated token (`button/content/secondary`, 50% opacity) — resolved the real semantic equivalent by opacity-matching across every `content/*` token and found `content/placeholder` (0.5 opacity, same base dark color) is the exact match, already used elsewhere in this file for the same "muted content" role (Report Card's `Trend` delta text uses it too) — bound the new icons' strokes to it.
- **The `Tag` instance inside `Type=Tag` cells was bound to a fully orphaned duplicate `Tag` component set** — 12 variants, structurally identical to the real canonical `Tag` (`Size × Type`), but internally typo'd (`"Size= Medium, Type=Success"` with a stray space, inconsistently — the `Size=Large` variants in the SAME duplicate set have no space). Confirmed via direct component-id comparison this is a different node from the real Tag, not a misread. `swapComponent()`'d both the Desktop and Mobile `Tag` instances onto the real, canonical Tag (`Size=Medium, Type=Success`, on the `-> Tags` page) — confirmed zero remaining references to the orphan afterward. **Could not delete the orphaned set itself** — the Plugin API refuses (`"Removing this node is not allowed"`, likely because it's fully detached from any page, `parent: null`) — needs manual deletion in the Figma UI (search layers for a parentless "Tag" component set, or check Figma's own "unused components" audit if the file has one).
- **`Cell`'s own `componentPropertyDefinitions` briefly looked like it exposed 4 unrelated boolean toggles** (`Trailing icon`, `Leading icon`, `Helper text`, `Show info`) that don't belong on a table cell. Investigated properly rather than assuming: `Trailing icon`/`Leading icon` turned out to be **real, legitimate properties of the (now correctly-bound) canonical `Tag` component** bubbling up through Figma's normal variant-property aggregation — not a bug. `Helper text`/`Show info` remain unexplained and are very likely coming from the `Type=Button` cell's nested `Button/Action` instance, whose own component set **Figma itself reports as internally errored** (`"Component set for node has existing errors"` — this specific error blocked every attempt to introspect that instance's own properties directly). This looks like a real corruption in the Button component set (property definitions possibly left over from a different component during construction) that needs manual repair in the Figma UI — the Plugin API can't get closer to it than this.
- **Naming hygiene**: the default `Row` frame was named `"  "` (literally two spaces); the demo `Table`'s 6 row frames were all default Figma names (`Frame 1618872904`, etc.) instead of `Row`. Renamed all of them. Separately confirmed **no reusable `Row` component exists at all** — every row in the demo table is an unnamed, independently-duplicated frame, and the "hover" state the user pointed at is actually just a single bare `Cell` instance with its own hover fill toggled on, not a row-level state. Flagged, not fixed — building a real `Row` component (so hover/selected can be driven at the row level, not per-cell) is a decision for the code-translation pass, not a silent Figma-side fix.

Verified every fix via `get_screenshot` (before/after pairs for the token rebinds, and after the icon swap) — all render identically or, for the two user-approved padding changes, exactly as expected. Not yet done: the actual React/CSS code translation and Storybook page (next task), and the two flagged manual-cleanup items (orphaned Tag deletion, Button component-set repair) which are out of Plugin-API reach.

### Table family shipped to code: `Cell`, `Row`, `Table`, `RowMenuTrigger` (2026-09-04)

Translated the audited Figma Table into 4 new components, following the "Figma-vetted first, code second" order this file has used all along.

**API decisions:**
- `Cell` collapses Figma's `Type` variant into one component with a `type` union (`checkbox` | `text` | `number` | `tag` | `dot-text` | `icon` | `title` | `button` | `number-percentage`) and plain optional per-type props — not a discriminated union. Same call already made for `TabToggle`'s icon-only props earlier this session: a strict union fights Storybook CSF3's `Meta`/`StoryObj` typing on partial per-story `args`. Figma's separate `Title (Inactive)`/`Title Active` variants collapse into `type="title"` + an `active` boolean, since they're the same content shape with a state difference, not two different shapes.
- Figma's cells were all a fixed 160px (canvas sizing for the demo, not a real constraint) — `Cell` takes an optional `width` instead, defaulting to flexible (`flex: 1`), so real tables can size columns as needed. Height is content-driven, not hardcoded to Figma's 40px, so wrapped/varied content doesn't clip.
- `type="checkbox"` doesn't reuse the existing `Checkbox` component — `Checkbox` requires a visible `label` string it always renders next to the box, which doesn't match Figma's layout (indicator + separate sibling "Content" text). Built directly on `CheckboxIndicator` (the presentational piece already extracted for this exact kind of reuse) plus a real visually-hidden `<input type="checkbox">`, matching `Checkbox.tsx`'s own internal pattern minus the forced label.
- `.header-more` shipped as a new `RowMenuTrigger` component, not folded into `IconButton` — at 24×24 it's smaller than IconButton's established 32/40/48 scale, and its faint circular backdrop is visible at rest (Figma's `Active=False` fill is unconditionally visible, not hover-only), a different feel than any of IconButton's 5 types.
- **Real fix for a gap flagged during the Figma audit**: Figma has no row-level hover state (each `Cell`'s hover fill is independent per-cell), so hovering one cell there doesn't highlight its neighbors. `Row` fixes this in code with a CSS custom-property cascade — `Cell` reads its background from `var(--cell-bg, var(--background-surface-raised))`, and `Row:hover` sets `--cell-bg` once, which plain CSS inheritance carries to every `Cell` child without either component reaching into the other's CSS Modules classes. Verified live: hovering anywhere in a row highlights every cell in it together.

**Two colors fixed after checking real Figma bindings instead of guessing** (caught by Storybook's own accessibility addon flagging a contrast violation, which is what prompted double-checking rather than assuming the guess was fine): `title` (inactive) text and `number-percentage`'s parenthetical both initially used `content/placeholder` (0.5 opacity) as a plausible-looking "muted text" choice — neither was actually checked against Figma. Real bindings: `Title (Inactive)` uses `content/disabled` (0.4 opacity, the same token Checkbox/Radio's own disabled states use — a systemic, accepted low-contrast look for de-emphasized/non-interactive text elsewhere in this system too, not something to silently swap for a higher-contrast token), and `Number & Percentage`'s second text is actually full-contrast `content/default` — same color as the primary number, not muted at all. Fixed both to match.

**Accessibility**: `Table`/`Row`/`Cell` use real ARIA `table`/`row`/`cell` roles (`Cell` defaults to `columnheader` when `type="title"`, since that's the type used for sortable header rows). The initial version had `role="row"` and `role="cell"`-less `Cell`s — Storybook's a11y addon caught it immediately as a real "ARIA required children" critical violation (a `role="row"` had `<button>` children, not an allowed role for that context) before it shipped.

**Icon weight, table-only per explicit user instruction**: the `Cell`/`Table` Storybook demos' row-action icons (`PencilSimple`/`Trash`/`Archive`) use `weight="thin"` — user feedback was specific to this context ("at least in this table case"), not a change to the system-wide Phosphor convention (`Format=Stroke, Weight=Regular`) used elsewhere (Button's `Sparkle`, etc.).

**New token added to code, mirroring the Figma addition from the audit pass**: `--spacing-gap-050: 2px` added to `scripts/figma-tokens.json` (one new entry, not a full re-sync of the other 100 already-synced tokens) and regenerated via `node scripts/build-tokens.mjs`.

Verified: 159/159 tests passing (up from 139), clean typecheck. Confirmed live in Storybook: all 9 `Cell` types render correctly, the assembled `Table` story matches the reference screenshot's clean look, row-hover highlights the whole row, dark mode renders correctly (tags keep their color-coded meaning, borders/backgrounds flip), and the 8px→4px mobile padding switch fires at the real breakpoint (confirmed via computed styles at a 375px viewport, not just visually). Not done: the two Figma-side manual-cleanup items flagged in the audit (orphaned Tag component deletion, Button component-set repair) remain outside Plugin API reach.

**Follow-up — a "full capacity" story, after the user noticed the first demo only showed 3 columns**: the original `Default` story deliberately used a narrow 3-column shape (Company/Status/Actions) as one realistic example, not a demonstration of everything `Cell` can do — the fuller range lived in `Cell.stories.tsx`'s own `AllTypes` story instead. Added `Table.stories.tsx`'s `FullCapabilities` story: a denser project-tracker table exercising 8 of `Cell`'s 9 types together in one row (checkbox, dot-text, tag, number, number-percentage, button, icon — every type except `title`, reserved for header rows). Caught two more real accessibility gaps while checking it, the same way the original build caught its ARIA bug: the checkbox header cell defaulted to `role="cell"` (`Cell`'s role default only special-cases `type="title"` as `columnheader`, not `type="checkbox"` used in a header row — a real limitation, since `Cell` has no way to know it's in a header row without the consumer telling it) and two label-less action-column headers had no accessible name at all. Both fixed via the same `role`/`aria-label` override already supported by `Cell`'s prop spread — not a component change, just correct usage — which the story's own comment now calls out explicitly as the pattern to reach for.

**User flagged the `FullCapabilities` story's "View" button as looking off-brand ("like from another library")** — investigated by reading real computed styles rather than assuming either "it's fine" or "something's broken": confirmed it genuinely was the real `Button` component with real tokens applied (hashed CSS Module classes from `Button.module.css`/`button-types.module.css`, real Haffer font, `background-action-primary-default` black background, 8px radius — no foreign library, no style leak). The actual problem was a usage mistake, not a rendering bug: the button used `type="primary"` (the default), and `Button`'s own doc comment says "pick by emphasis — one primary per screen/section" — repeating a solid black primary button on every row of a dense table visibly breaks that rule and reads as heavy/foreign next to the row's other `ghost`-type icon actions. Switched it to `type="tertiary"`, which sits quietly alongside the icon actions the way a real row-level action should. Left `Cell.stories.tsx`'s own standalone `ButtonType`/`AllTypes` demos on `primary`, since that single-instance showcase matches Figma's own real Type=Button reference exactly (`Primary, Small, Default`) — the emphasis rule is about repetition across a list, not the type itself being wrong to demo in isolation.

**Two more real issues caught from a screenshot of the row-hover state, both fixed**:

1. **`RowMenuTrigger` didn't match the system's own existing "options" pattern.** User pointed at `TemplateCard`'s own options button (`DotsThree weight="bold"`, 24×24, `border-radius-md` — a rounded *square*, transparent at rest, single `background-interactive-hover` wash on hover) and asked for the same treatment instead of `RowMenuTrigger`'s own bespoke design (circular, `weight="regular"`, an always-visible 5% wash at rest, a separate darker 10%-wash "active" tier). Rebuilt `RowMenuTrigger` to match `TemplateCard`'s exact values — now the two "more options" affordances in this system read as one shared pattern instead of two independently-invented ones.

2. **The pencil/trash icon buttons' hover looked heavier inside a hovered row than the same hover looks elsewhere — confirmed as a real, always-reproducible bug, not a one-off screenshot artifact.** Root cause, found by reading actual token values rather than guessing: `background-interactive-hover` (IconButton's own ghost hover) and `background-surface-sunken` (`Row`'s hover-cascade tint on `Cell`) resolve to the exact same translucent value (`rgb(2 9 18 / 0.05)`). Since any icon/button cell's *content* is itself interactive, hovering it always triggers both washes stacked on top of each other at once — two 5% translucent overlays compositing in sequence over white don't simply read as "5%," they read visibly darker, and this happens on every real mouse interaction with a row's action icons, not just under a screenshot's magnification. Fixed by opting `type="icon"`/`type="button"` cells out of the ambient row-hover tint entirely (pinned their own background to `background-surface-raised` regardless of `--cell-bg`) — their own nested control already gives its own hover feedback, so the passive row-level tint was redundant there anyway. Verified via computed styles: the icon cell now stays plain white while its row is hovered, while text/tag/number cells in the same row still correctly pick up the sunken tint.

Verified: 160/160 tests still passing, clean typecheck, both fixes confirmed live via real computed-style checks (not just visual comparison) in Storybook.

**A third real bug from that same screenshot, more serious than the first two**: the pencil icon's hover/focus ring wasn't just discolored, it was visibly bleeding past its own row into the row above. Root cause: `Cell` had a hardcoded `height: 40px` — with `spacing-padding-300` (12px) top/bottom padding, that leaves only 16px of actual content height, nowhere near enough for a 32×32 `IconButton`. Text-based cells never surfaced this because a text line's line-height metric quietly overflows a too-small box without any visible artifact (glyphs are shorter than their line-height), but a rigid 32px icon button has a hard geometric edge that overflows obviously, uncontained since `Cell` has no `overflow: hidden`.

Fixed by changing `height: 40px` to `min-height: 40px`. `Row` already had `align-items: stretch` (used for the row-hover cascade, but never actually taking effect before — a hardcoded child height neutralizes flexbox stretch entirely, which is exactly what was suppressing it). With the fixed height gone, every cell in a row now naturally stretches to match its tallest sibling: a header row with only text stays ~46px, while a body row containing 32px icon buttons grows to 56px — self-consistent per-row, the same way a real HTML `<table>` row's height is driven by its tallest cell, with no hardcoded magic number needed. Verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the icon button no longer exceeds its row's own bounds, on top of the usual test/typecheck pass.

**Also, in response to "what size are the table's tags?"**: they were Medium (25px, `Tag`'s own default — never explicitly set). Asked the user directly rather than assuming a fix was wanted; user confirmed switching to Small (22px, `text-accent-tight-100`) fits the row's existing compact density (32px icon buttons, 24px row-menu trigger) better. Applied `size="small"` to every `Tag` used inside a `Cell` across both `Table.stories.tsx` and `Cell.stories.tsx`'s own `TagType` demo — then reverted back to Medium a few minutes later per a follow-up ask, with no explanation given. Left it at Medium (the default, i.e. no explicit `size` prop) rather than re-litigating which was "right."

**A fourth bug, the real root cause behind the whole "hover feels off" thread — the `min-height` fix helped but the icon still visibly overflowed sideways into the next cell.** Checked with real `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements instead of re-guessing at CSS: the icon-actions `Cell` (the one with no explicit `width` prop, since it's the last, flexible column) had computed to a bizarre 8px total width — just its own padding — while its 32px icon-button children sat rendered dozens of pixels away, completely outside that shrunken box. Cause: `.cell` had `min-width: 0` (needed so `.content`'s `text-overflow: ellipsis` can actually engage — a flex item's default `min-width: auto` pins it to its unwrapped content width and silently blocks ellipsis otherwise), but that same `min-width: 0` also let the *whole cell* shrink far below its actual children's combined size whenever sibling cells with fixed pixel widths (`width={200}`, `width={140}`, etc.) consumed more of `Row`'s available flex space than there was room for — flexbox happily shrank the one unconstrained cell toward zero, while its fixed-size icon-button children (which don't shrink) rendered outside the now-tiny box, uncontained since `Cell` has no `overflow: hidden`. This explains every version of the "overflow" symptom reported in this thread — it was never really about hover color, row height, or the dots icon's weight; the cell itself had collapsed to near-nothing and everything inside it was rendering in the wrong place as a result. Fixed by moving `min-width: 0` off `.cell` and onto `.content` specifically (the actual element that needs it for ellipsis) — `.cell` now defaults back to `min-width: auto`, meaning it can never shrink below its own content's real minimum size again. Verified three ways: `getBoundingClientRect()` shows the icon cell now sizing to its real ~152px content width with zero button overflow either edge; a forced-narrow test cell confirms `.content`'s ellipsis truncation (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`) still engages exactly as before; and 160/160 tests plus a clean typecheck.

### `content/secondary` re-pointed to `color/darken/600` (was `/700`) — a live Figma edit, synced to code (2026-09-04)

User made this change directly in Figma and flagged it verbally rather than asking for a build. Verified rather than trusting the stated "600 from 700" at face value: a simple alias-chain walk gave a confusing result (the final primitive's own collection reported a mode named "Desktop", not "Light"/"Dark", which didn't match how every other token in this system has resolved so far) — re-checked with the same native-resolution technique used earlier this session (bind to a temp node, `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection` across both real modes of the *semantic* collection `content/secondary` itself lives in, read the resolved paint back), which confirmed: Light `rgb(2 9 18 / 0.70)` (down from the code's existing `0.80`), Dark unchanged at `rgb(255 255 255 / 0.80)` — only the light-mode opacity actually moved.

Synced to code the same way the earlier `spacing/gap/050` addition was: updated the one affected entry in `scripts/figma-tokens.json` (Light `a`: 0.8 → 0.7), regenerated via `node scripts/build-tokens.mjs` rather than hand-editing the auto-generated `tokens.css`. This token is already consumed by two shipped components (`TabToggle`, `ProgressTab`) plus several Foundations doc pages — all pick it up automatically via `var(--content-secondary)`, no component code changes needed. Verified: 160/160 tests passing, clean typecheck, spot-checked `TabToggle` live in Storybook to confirm the softer opacity renders without any visual regression.

### New "underline tab" component audited and built (`Tab Item` / `Tab Bar`) — Figma only, code translation still to come (2026-09-04)

User added a new pattern to the `-> Tabs` page — a horizontal underline-style tab bar (unlike `TabToggle`'s pill/segmented-control look) — and asked for the same token/spacing/corner audit before setting it up to match a reference screenshot of a real product nav (Visibility / Mentions & Citations / Sentiment / Sources / **Topics** (active) / Query Fanout / Location).

**Found in placeholder-naming hell, cleaned up first**: the component set was named `namethis`, its one variant property was the default unrenamed `Property 1`, and the demo group plus all 7 of its instances were named `namethisgroup`/`namethis` — never finished by whoever built it. Renamed throughout: component set → `Tab Item`, property → `State` (values `Default`/`Hover`/`Selected` — the third was already internally valued `selected` despite the component's own display name reading `Property 1=Variant3`, a real display-name/value mismatch, resolved by the rename), demo group → `Tab Bar`, each instance → `Tab Item — <label>`.

**Real bugs found and fixed**:
- **Underline stroke bound to nothing** — Figma had it as a raw, unbound pure-black `{r:0,g:0,b:0}`, which doesn't exactly match any real token (`content/default`/`border-strong` are both `rgb(10,10,10)`, visually identical but not byte-identical to pure black). Bound it to `content/default` — the same token the Selected tab's own label text already uses, so the label and its underline are now provably the same color by construction, not just by coincidence.
- **Padding inconsistency, resolved only after checking the real fill data rather than assuming**: `Default`'s horizontal padding (8px) didn't match `Hover`/`Selected` (12px). Initially looked like `Hover` might be a stray duplicate of `Default` — checked the user's own hypothesis ("hover state, right, it has a diff bg?") by inspecting `Frame 1`'s actual fill on both variants (not just the root component's, which was empty on all three and had misled the first pass): confirmed `Hover`'s `Frame 1` genuinely has a real, correctly-bound 5%-opacity `background/interactive/hover` rounded chip — a legitimate third state, not a mistake. That reframed the padding gap as a real layout-shift bug instead (a tab would visibly widen by 8px when hovered or selected) — fixed by unifying all three states' `Frame 1` padding to `spacing/padding/300` (12px).
- **The tab bar's own gap was an unbound, off-scale 10px** — no step in the spacing scale matches 10. Asked the user directly rather than guessing a value that changes a real visual proportion; confirmed `spacing/gap/600` (24px), matching the generous breathing room in the reference screenshot.
- **Icon/Badge optional slots hit the same `visible:false`-omitted-from-fresh-instances Plugin API quirk** discovered earlier this session (Report Card work) — flipped both to `visible: true` on all three master variants so future instances actually get the nodes at all, then immediately caught and fixed the direct consequence: doing this made all 7 *existing* demo instances suddenly show icons and a "00" badge chip (confirmed via a real `get_screenshot`, not assumed), since they'd never had a per-instance override on a property that used to not exist for them. Fixed by explicitly setting `visible: false` on `Icon`/`Badge` for each of the 7 real instances — the standard two-part fix this pattern always needs (visible on the master, hidden per-instance where not wanted).

**Populated the demo to match the reference exactly**: renamed the 7 "Label" placeholders to the real nav labels from the screenshot, set "Topics" (5th tab) to the `Selected` state, everything else `Default`.

**A rendering false alarm, resolved by cross-checking with a second method rather than trusting one screenshot**: two consecutive `get_screenshot` calls on the finished `Tab Bar` showed everything from "Topics" onward rendering as an opaque black block obscuring the text — investigated the underlying node tree first (fills, strokes, child list, positions) and found nothing that could produce a black rectangle anywhere. Re-rendered via the node's own inline `screenshot()` method instead (a different rendering path than the separate `get_screenshot` tool) and it came back completely clean, matching the reference exactly — confirming the black block was a `get_screenshot`-specific rendering glitch for this particular node/moment, not a real file problem. Lesson: when a screenshot shows something the node tree's own data can't explain, cross-check with a second rendering method before concluding the file is actually broken.

Not yet done: the React/CSS code translation (same "Figma first" order used for the Report Card and Table work) and a Storybook page.

**Follow-up — switched from gap-based spacing to padding-based spacing**: user asked for a tighter layout where each `Tab Item`'s own padding does the work of separating tabs, instead of a gap between them (the standard reason: this removes the dead, unclickable zone between adjacent tab hit-areas — hovering anywhere between two labels now lands inside one of the two tabs, not nowhere). First pass overcorrected — doubled each tab's padding to `spacing/padding/600` (24px) to try to preserve the old 24px gap's total visual distance, which the user hadn't asked for. Corrected per their actual intent: left each `Tab Item` variant's padding at `spacing/padding/300` (12px), just zeroed `Tab Bar`'s `itemSpacing` — two adjacent tabs' 12px+12px padding already reproduces the same 24px of visual space the old gap provided, with no separate gap token needed at all. Verified via inline `screenshot()`: tighter, correct spacing, bar width 742px (vs. 886px with the old gap, 910px from the overcorrected first pass).

**Completeness check, requested directly ("does this feel complete and per our rules?") — answered honestly rather than rubber-stamping it.** Checked things not yet covered by the first audit pass: the Icon slot (real Phosphor `PhosphorLogo`, not legacy — clean), Hover's corner radius (properly bound to `border-radius-md` — clean), then four real gaps: no keyboard focus state, Icon/Badge not exposed as real toggleable component properties (just raw layer visibility), no Disabled state, and `Tab Bar` being a `COMPONENT` with 7 *fixed* tab instances rather than something that can hold a different set. User fixed the Icon/Badge properties themselves in Figma directly (confirmed via `componentPropertyReferences` — both now real `BOOLEAN` properties correctly bound to `visible`) and asked for the other three: Disabled state, Focus state, and make `Tab Bar` genuinely reusable.

**Checked precedent before building anything**: pulled `TabToggle`'s own Figma component fresh and found it *also* only has Default/Hover/Selected — no separate Disabled/Focus variants exist there either. Confirmed via `TabToggle.module.css` that Disabled/Focus were **code-only** additions (`:disabled` → `content-disabled` + `not-allowed`; `:focus-visible` → the double-ring `box-shadow` every interactive component in this system shares). Followed the same precedent here rather than inventing new Figma variants for states that have never been modeled as variants anywhere in this file.

**`Tab Bar` converted from a fixed component to a plain frame**: recreated it as a `figma.createAutoLayout()` frame, moved all 7 existing `Tab Item` instances over in order, deleted the old locked component. Verified via `screenshot()` — pixel-identical (742×38, same as before) — confirming the conversion was purely structural, no visual change. `Tab Item` is now the only real reusable component; `Tab Bar` is just an example arrangement, matching how the Report Card family and `Table` were built.

**One more real bug found while double-checking before writing code**: `Frame 1`'s own internal `itemSpacing` (the gap between icon/label/badge inside a single tab, separate from the gap between tabs) was *also* an unbound, off-scale 10px — the exact same bug pattern as the group-level gap, just one level deeper, and not caught by the first audit pass because that pass only checked the outer group. Bound it to `spacing/gap/200` (8px) across all three `Tab Item` variants. Also noted, but left as a flagged gap rather than fixing now since it's an unused placeholder slot: `Badge`'s own fill and text color are both raw unbound values in Figma, not tokens.

**Shipped to code**: `TabItem` (`src/components/TabItem`) and `TabBar` (`src/components/TabBar`), both exported from the library. `TabItem` renders as an outer `<button role="tab">` (carrying the underline via `border-bottom`, transparent unless `selected`) wrapping an inner `.chip` span (Figma's `Frame 1` — padding, corner radius, the hover background) — mirroring Figma's own two-layer structure rather than flattening it, so the hover chip's rounded background doesn't extend down into the underline gutter. Props: `children` (label), `icon?`, `badge?`, `selected?`, plus every native button attribute (`disabled` included, no separate prop needed). `TabBar` is a thin `role="tablist"` flex wrapper with **no fixed child count or cloned props** — deliberately simpler than `TabToggleGroup` (which injects a shared `size` into its children) since this component never had a size axis in Figma.

Verified: 171/171 tests passing (up from 160), clean typecheck. Confirmed live in Storybook: the `Default` story reproduces the reference screenshot exactly; clicking a tab moves the selection and underline; the `WithDisabledTab` story shows "Query Fanout" correctly muted and non-interactive; real keyboard Tab navigation (not a programmatic `.focus()`, which doesn't reliably trigger `:focus-visible`) confirmed the double-ring focus indicator renders with the exact same values as `Button`/`TabToggle`; the new `FewerTabs` story (3 tabs instead of 7) directly demonstrates the reusability fix; dark mode renders correctly.

**Follow-up — underline felt too thin at 1px**: bumped `strokeBottomWeight` from 1 to 2 on all three `Tab Item` Figma variants (kept consistent across Default/Hover even though only Selected's is visible, matching the established "reserve the same space regardless of state" convention), and the code's `border-bottom` to match — Figma and code fixed together rather than patching just one side. Verified via `screenshot()` in Figma and a real computed-style check in Storybook (`borderBottomWidth: "2px"`).

### `Light/Dark Toggle` — a pasted-in external component, fully re-themed to this system (2026-09-04)

New component on a new `-> Theme` page. Structural signature made it obvious before even auditing the tokens: extremely non-round pixel values everywhere (widths like `81.76`, `8.12`; corner radii like `26.666667938232422`; stroke weights like `0.5801296830177307`) — the hallmark of a component copy-pasted from an external community Figma kit at some odd scale factor, never integrated into this file's own token system. User confirmed: fix everything, theme it to this system per all guides.

**Real bugs fixed**:
- **A genuine remote/foreign-library variable binding**, not just a value that happened to look like one: the Dark-mode variant's inactive Sun icon-chip had a fill bound to `button/background/primary` from a completely different, remote library (confirmed `remote: true` on the resolved variable) — currently invisible, but every other "inactive, no chip" slot in this component simply has **no fill at all**, so even the pattern itself was wrong, not just the binding. Cleared the fill entirely to match.
- **Root pill's corner radius bound to a global primitive** (`size/15` = 64px) instead of a semantic token — same "don't bind to global directly" class of bug found elsewhere in this file. Rebound to `border/radius/xl` (32px), which renders an identical full-capsule shape since any radius ≥ half the pill's height caps out the same way.
- **Root pill's background fill used `content/inverse`** (a text/icon-color token) instead of a background-family token — wrong token family, though the raw value happened to be the same white either way. Rebound to `background/surface/raised`, which resolves to the exact same white and is the semantically correct family.
- **`Track`'s internal icon-to-icon gap was unbound** — bound to `spacing/gap/100` (4px).
- **Every dimension inside the toggle snapped to clean numbers**: both `Sun Chip`/`Moon Chip` containers set to a real `FIXED` 16×16 (was `~16.003334`, hug-sized off an unbound ~2.67px padding) with padding zeroed and centering handled purely by the existing `CENTER`/`CENTER` alignment; both `Sun`/`Moon` icon instances resized to a clean 12×12 (was `~10.666667`, i.e. exactly 2/3 of 16 — an artifact of whatever scale the paste came in at, not a deliberate size). Root pill (56×24) and its 4px padding were already clean and already correctly bound — no change needed there.

**Confirmed correct, not bugs**: the `Sun`/`Moon` icons are real Phosphor components (`Sun`/`Moon`, `Format=Stroke, Weight=Fill`) — Phosphor rule already satisfied, nothing to swap. The active-segment chip fill (`background/action/primary-default`) is a real, meaningful reuse of the exact token `Button`'s own primary type uses — the active toggle segment is deliberately styled like a mini primary-button chip, not a mistake.

**Naming cleaned up**: component set → `Light/Dark Toggle` (was already named this at the top level, but its variant property was still the default `Property 1` — renamed to `Mode`), the anonymous `Frame 2147225038` track → `Track`, and the two generically-named `Button/Icon` chip frames → `Sun Chip`/`Moon Chip` so the two active/inactive slots are distinguishable by name, not just position.

**Deleted two scaffold/annotation artifacts** that weren't real product UI: a `"Component Name"` marker instance (a devtools-style label reading "Light/Dark toggle" with a colored dot, the kind of annotation that ships baked into community Figma templates) and a stray `"Rectangle 1"` outline — both cleared with the user's confirmation, once identified as leftover template scaffolding rather than anything load-bearing.

**One layout read corrected before acting on it**: initially misread the Theme section's screenshot as showing a missing "dark" demo section (a large plain black area with nothing in it) — checked actual node positions instead of guessing further, and found the two loose demo `Light/Dark Toggle` instances (one per `Mode`) are positioned directly on the `Theme` section's own dark background at `x≈79`, deliberately separate from the `light` section (`x=208`) that holds the master component set against a light backdrop. Nothing was missing — it's a real side-by-side comparison (component swatches on light, usage examples on dark), not an unfinished layout. No structural change made.

Verified via `get_screenshot` before and after every stage (initial fixes, dimension cleanup, and the full section) — both `Mode` variants render correctly (Sun highlighted+white-on-dark-chip in Light mode, Moon highlighted the same way in Dark mode, the other icon dimmed via `content/secondary`), and the two loose demo instances picked up every master-level fix automatically with no separate per-instance work needed, confirming they'd never had conflicting overrides.

**Shipped to code**: `LightDarkToggle` (`src/components/LightDarkToggle`), exported from the library. Built as a single `<button role="switch" aria-checked={mode === 'dark'}>` — matching Figma's real structure exactly once the audit clarified it (outer button owns the border/background/radius/padding; an inner plain `.track` div does `justify-content: space-between` layout only, no styling of its own; two `.chip` spans hold the Sun/Moon icons, one gets the active dark-fill + white-icon treatment via `background-action-primary-default`/`background-action-primary-content`, the other stays `content-secondary`). Props: `mode` (controlled `'light' | 'dark'`), `onChange`. Real Phosphor `Sun`/`Moon` icons at `weight="fill"`, matching Figma's own `Format=Stroke, Weight=Fill` variant choice.

Verified: 176/176 tests passing (up from 171), clean typecheck. Confirmed live in Storybook: `Light`/`Dark` stories match each Figma variant; the `Interactive` story's click toggles the highlighted icon correctly; `OnDarkBackground` reproduces the Figma demo's "usage against a dark surface" example; real keyboard Tab navigation confirmed the focus ring renders with the exact same double-ring values as every other interactive component in this system.

**Follow-up 1 — sized up.** User asked directly whether 56×24 was standard (a fair, compact size for this style of control, but on the smaller side) then said the whole thing felt too small. Scaled up **in Figma and code together**: pill 56×24 → 64×32 (32 = the same "small" height tier `Button`/`IconButton` already use, so the whole control now sits on the system's own established height scale rather than a bespoke number), chips 16×16 → 24×24, icons 12×12 → **16×16 = `size-icon-sm`** — a real icon-size token, correcting a hidden gap from the first pass where the icon size was a raw, unbound 12px that didn't match any `--size-icon-*` step at all.

**Follow-up 2 — micro-interaction, requested to feel more alive**: user asked for the highlight circle to slide between icons instead of one chip abruptly popping visible, plus a subtle rotate-overshoot on the icons. Built via a `.highlight` span that's absolutely positioned and `translateX`'d between the two icon slots (travel distance = track width − circle width = 32px) on a spring-ish `cubic-bezier` easing, and an `.icon-settle` `@keyframes` animation applied only to whichever icon just became active.

**Refined twice more after watching it**: (1) "overshoot should be really smooth/subtle" — eased off both the highlight's cubic-bezier amplitude and the keyframe's own rotation/scale range to gentler values. (2) "icons shift position when rotating, and the final resting position needs to match my design" — two real, distinct issues, not one: **(a)** the rotation was visibly shifting the icon's position because `transform-origin: center` alone doesn't work reliably on SVGs — without `transform-box: fill-box`, percentage-based origins resolve against the SVG viewport, not the icon's own visual bounds; added `transform-box: fill-box` to pin rotation to the icon's true center (confirmed via computed style: `transform-origin` resolves to the icon's exact midpoint, `8px 8px` for a 16px icon). **(b)** the deeper issue: the *resting* inactive icon was permanently tilted (`rotate(-25deg) scale(0.8)`) as a two-state style difference — Figma's real design never rotates or scales the resting icon at all, only its color changes. Redesigned so rotation is purely a **one-shot flourish that plays once and settles back to the real, unrotated resting position** (a `@keyframes` `animation` rather than a persistent `transition` between two rotated states), matching Figma's actual static design exactly once any motion finishes. Caught one more real bug in the process: the resting inactive color had `color: var(--content-secondary)` (which already bakes in 70% opacity) *plus* a separate `opacity: 0.7` on top of it — the same "stacked wash" double-dimming bug pattern found earlier in the Table work. Removed the redundant `opacity`.

**A real verification limitation, not a bug**: tried to confirm the animation's actual motion via computed styles mid-play and found it frozen at the first keyframe — traced this to `document.hidden: true` on the automated browser tab (the Browser pane itself was hidden in this session), and browsers deliberately pause CSS animations on hidden tabs by design. Confirmed everything checkable without live frames instead: `animation-duration`/`animation-timing-function`/keyframe values all parse correctly, clicking the toggle correctly moves the `.iconActive` class to the other icon and flips `aria-checked`, and the resting (non-animating) state matches the corrected design exactly. The actual felt smoothness of the motion still needs a real eyes-on check by the user in a focused browser tab.

Verified: 176/176 tests passing, clean typecheck throughout all three follow-up passes.

**Follow-up 3 — real alignment bugs, caught by measuring actual geometry rather than eyeballing a screenshot.** User flagged "the alignments are off" from a screenshot showing the active highlight circle bulging past the pill's own edge. Measured every element's real `getBoundingClientRect()` instead of guessing, and found two distinct, previously-invisible bugs:
1. **The highlight's `translateX(32px)` was a hardcoded guess that didn't match the track's real width.** `.toggle` uses `box-sizing: border-box`, so its own 1px border — not just its padding — also gets subtracted from the declared 64px width. The travel distance I'd computed (56 − 24 = 32) ignored the border entirely; the real content width is 54px, so the correct distance is 30px, not 32. Rather than patch the magic number, switched `.highlight` from `transform: translateX()` to animating `left`, with the dark-mode position expressed as `left: calc(100% - 24px)` — `%` in `left` resolves against the actual parent width at render time, so this can't drift out of sync with the real box model again regardless of border/padding changes.
2. **The highlight also overflowed the track vertically, asymmetrically** — its hardcoded `height: 24px` exceeds the track's real height (22px, same border-box subtraction), and because it was positioned with `top: 0` rather than being centered the way the icon slots are (which get free centering from the track's own `align-items: center` flex context — `.highlight` is `position: absolute`, so it's outside that flow entirely), the entire 2px mismatch landed at the bottom instead of splitting evenly. This exactly matches what the user described independently before seeing my diagnosis ("closer to the bottom... should be centered"). Fixed with `top: 50%` + `transform: translateY(-50%)`, kept constant across both states so only `left` animates.

Verified precisely, not just visually: `getBoundingClientRect()` on the highlight now returns **exactly** the same `{x, y, width, height, right, bottom}` as its corresponding icon slot in both states, and the small 1px overflow past the track's own edge is now symmetric top/bottom, matching how the icon slots themselves already sit.

**Follow-up 4 — hover state added, requested directly**: "the icon that isn't toggled needs that light grey bg." Added `.iconSlot:hover:not(:has(.iconActive))` using `background-interactive-hover` — the same 5% wash token used for hover states everywhere else in this system (`MenuItem`, `IconButton`'s ghost type, `RowMenuTrigger`, etc.). Scoped with `:has()` so only the *inactive* side gets the affordance — the active side already reads as selected via the highlight circle and doesn't need one. Verified via dispatched hover events and `getComputedStyle`: the inactive slot resolves to `rgba(2, 9, 18, 0.05)` exactly, the active slot stays transparent.

Verified: 176/176 tests passing, clean typecheck.

### `Toggle` — a switch component, structurally complete but 100% un-integrated on tokens (2026-09-04)

New component set on a new `-> Toggle` page: `size` (small/medium/large) × `checked` (false/true) × `disabled` (false/true) — all 12 combinations genuinely present, nothing missing. Unlike the naming mess found in the last two imports, this one was already sensibly named (`Toggle`, `Thumb`, lowercase `size`/`checked`/`disabled` properties reading naturally as real boolean/enum props) — no renaming needed at all. The problem here was purely tokens: **every single fill and stroke across all 12 variants was bound to a remote/foreign variable** (`action/disabled-bg`, `ds/gray/alpha/400`, `surface/surface-primary-default`, `surface/page`, `brand-green/default`, `content/on-brand` — all confirmed `remote: true`, all from a completely different design system's own naming scheme).

**Checked for exact local matches before assuming anything needed a new token** — resolved every semantic-collection color to its final value and diffed against each of the 7 distinct remote colors. Only one real match turned up: the thumb's near-white color happened to numerically match `chart/grid/tooltip` exactly — but reusing a chart-tooltip token for a toggle thumb would be a confusing cross-reference, not a real semantic fit, so it wasn't used as-is. Every track color (unchecked, checked, and both disabled variants) and the border stroke had **no local equivalent at all** — a real, first-of-its-kind gap, not a simple mis-binding like the previous two components' issues.

**Asked the user directly rather than picking values myself**, since this determines real, visible color choices, not just architecture:
- **ON/checked track color** (a muted olive-green with no relationship to anything in this palette, including the existing `content-status-success` green) — user chose to reuse `background/action/primary-default` (the same near-black as `Button`'s primary type), not the closer-but-still-foreign olive or the existing success green.
- **OFF/unchecked track background and the border stroke** (a warm light gray + a separate 14%-opacity white stroke, neither with a local match) — first pass mapped both to `border/default`. **User caught a real mistake in that choice immediately**: binding a *background* fill to a *border*-family token, the exact "wrong token family" bug this file's own audits keep flagging in other people's work — I'd picked it by value-proximity, not by checking whether the token family made sense, exactly the shortcut this whole session's process exists to catch. Corrected properly after the user pointed at `LightDarkToggle` (built earlier this session) as the right precedent to reuse: track-off → `background/surface/raised` (a real background token, matching that component's own pill fill), stroke → `border/interactive-strong` (matching its border exactly). Since this makes the off-track and the thumb the same white, also added a matching `border/interactive-strong` stroke to the `Thumb` itself (which had no stroke before) so it stays visually defined against a same-toned track — confirmed via `screenshot()` that the thumb now reads as a clearly separate shape in every state, not just the on/black one.
- **Thumb and disabled-state colors**: extended the same "closest existing token" spirit without a separate round of questions — thumb fill uses `background/surface/raised` in every state rather than the remote's own slightly-different disabled-thumb gray (which also had no local match), and disabled states get a single blanket `opacity: 0.4` on the whole variant instead of bespoke disabled-specific track/thumb colors — simpler, and consistent with `content-disabled`'s own established 0.4 alpha convention for "disabled" elsewhere in this system.
- **Corner radius** (raw `14` on both track and thumb) bound to `border/radius/xl` (32px) on all 12 variants — still renders fully round given radius ≥ half of any dimension here.

Verified via `screenshot()` at each stage: the full 12-variant grid renders as a clean, cohesive set consistent with `LightDarkToggle`'s own token language — white off-track with a defined thumb, black on-track matching `Button`'s primary color, disabled row uniformly and correctly dimmed.

**Follow-up — user directly edited the `large` variant as a reference and asked for the other 11 to follow it, plus whether the sizing was standard.** Diffed the edited variant against its prior state to extract the actual pattern: track grew 40×24 → 48×30, padding 1px → 4px (a real token, `spacing/padding/100`) on all sides, thumb size held constant per tier (still 22px for large), and — the more interesting part — track-off fill changed from `background/surface/raised` (flat white, matching `LightDarkToggle`) to `background/surface/sunken` (the same 5%-opacity recessed wash used for zebra striping elsewhere), which made the thumb's own border (added in the previous pass specifically to fix a white-on-white contrast problem) unnecessary — a sunken track already contrasts against a plain white thumb without it. Removed the border rather than leaving it as dead styling.

Propagated the pattern to all three sizes using the same formula the edit implied (padding 4px flat, height = thumb + 2×4, width = thumb + 2×4 + a travel allowance kept proportional to the large tier's own 18px-travel : 22px-thumb ratio): small 30×20 (12px thumb), medium 40×26 (18px thumb), large 48×30 (22px thumb, matching the user's edit exactly). Applied the sunken-fill and border-removal changes to every `checked=false` variant across all three sizes and both disabled states (12 variants total).

**On whether the sizing is standard**: yes — `large` (48×30, 22px thumb) now sits very close to iOS's native switch (51×31) and Material Design's (52×32), squarely in the normal range for a default/large mobile-style toggle. `Medium` (40×26) and `small` (30×20) land near common web-toggle conventions (shadcn/Radix ≈44×24, Chakra ≈40×20). The original 40×24 single-size attempt was on the tight/compact side across the board by comparison.

Verified via `screenshot()`: the full grid renders cleanly at the new size, thumb clearly visible against the now-recessed off-track in every variant.

**Shipped to code**: `Toggle` (`src/components/Toggle`), exported from the library. Built on the same visually-hidden-real-`<input>` pattern `Checkbox` already established — a real `<input type="checkbox" role="switch">` (so `checked`/`disabled`/`onChange` are just native props, no bespoke state-management API) sits invisibly over a styled `.track`/`.thumb` sibling pair, matched via `~`/descendant selectors off `.input:checked`/`:disabled`/`:focus-visible`. Applied the `LightDarkToggle` lesson proactively this time instead of re-learning it: the thumb is vertically centered with `top: 50%` + `translateY(-50%)` (not `top: 0`), and its horizontal position is expressed as `left: calc(100% - var(--thumb-size) - var(--toggle-inset))` rather than a hardcoded travel distance. One new wrinkle specific to this component, documented inline in the CSS: absolutely-positioned children measure `top`/`left` against the parent's *padding box*, which means `.track`'s own declared `padding` has zero effect on the thumb's position — so the 4px inset is tracked as its own `--toggle-inset` custom property (still sourced from the real `spacing/padding/100` token) instead of relying on padding to do it.

Caught one real test bug while verifying: `StructureCheck`'s `play` function initially asserted `toBeVisible()` on the switch itself — which correctly *fails*, since the real `<input>` is intentionally `opacity: 0` by design (the same pattern `Checkbox` uses); the assertion was checking the wrong thing, not surfacing a real component bug. Fixed to check `toBeInTheDocument()` + checked-state instead.

Verified precisely, not just visually: 186/186 tests passing, clean typecheck; `getBoundingClientRect()` on the thumb in its checked position confirms vertical centering is symmetric (4px top and bottom) and the horizontal gap from the track's outer edge is exactly 5px — 4px inset plus the 1px border, which the raw box-model math predicts exactly; real keyboard Tab navigation confirmed the focus ring matches every other interactive component's double-ring exactly.

**Follow-up — `small` gets a tighter 2px inset, a new token**: user asked for the small size specifically to use 2px instead of the shared 4px inset — no existing token matches, same gap in the padding scale as the `spacing/gap/050` case from the Table work. Added `spacing/padding/050` in Figma (same collection/scopes as the rest of the `padding/*` family, aliased to the same `size/0` global primitive `spacing/gap/050` already uses, so both the smallest gap and smallest padding step now trace back to one shared 2px primitive) and applied it to all four `size=small` Toggle variants there. Synced to code the same way as before: one new entry in `scripts/figma-tokens.json`, regenerated via `build-tokens.mjs`, then overrode `--toggle-inset` inside `.small` specifically (medium/large keep the shared `spacing/padding/100`). Verified via computed styles: the checked-position thumb resolves to `left: 14px`, matching `28 (padding-box width) − 12 (thumb) − 2 (new inset)` exactly.

### `Tooltip` — imported from Geist Design System, re-themed and drastically reduced (2026-09-05)

User brought a real Vercel Geist tooltip component into the file (`-> Tooltips` page) and flagged it as "not on our brand yet." Audited before touching anything: `Geist` font family (not our `Haffer`), 13px text with an absolute 16px line-height (not our percentage convention), every color bound to remote Geist library variables, and a `COMPONENT_SET` with **240 variants** — `type` (default/success/error/warning/purple) × `position` (top/right/bottom/left) × `boxAlign` (left/center/right) × `center` (true/false) × `tip` (true/false).

**Reduced 240 → 2, per explicit user sign-off after a direct back-and-forth about what was actually excessive:**
- `position`/`boxAlign`/`center` dropped entirely — these are purely runtime placement concerns (where the tooltip sits relative to its trigger), which the already-installed `@radix-ui/react-tooltip` handles at runtime via its own `side`/`align`/`sideOffset` props. Baking 24 positional permutations into static Figma variants for something Radix computes for free would just be dead weight.
- `type` reduced from 5 down to just `default`. `purple` had no equivalent anywhere in our token system — inventing one would mean creating a brand-new semantic color with no established meaning, purely to preserve an import. `success`/`warning`/`error` DO map to real existing tokens (the same ones `Tag` uses), but a closer look showed those (`background/status/*`) are pale, barely-tinted pastels built for a small tag pill on a plain page — not the strong, saturated contrast a tooltip needs when floating over arbitrary content. Combined with colored tooltips being a genuinely rare pattern in practice (the default/neutral tooltip covers the overwhelming majority of real usage), the call was to ship one correct default tooltip now rather than four speculative color variants with no real token backing — matching this session's long-standing "no speculative capability" precedent (the same call made on `Avatar`'s git badge, `TabToggle`'s icon/badge slots, etc.).
- Left `tip` (true/false) as the only real remaining variant axis, since it's a genuine visual difference (the pointing triangle), not a runtime concern.
- One structural limitation hit: the four now-single-valued properties (`type`, `position`, `boxAlign`, `center`) couldn't be removed via `deleteComponentProperty` — the Plugin API rejected it (`"Invalid component property name"`) for a `VARIANT`-type property collapsed to one option. Left as a minor, purely cosmetic manual cleanup for the Figma UI (Layers panel → variant properties) — doesn't affect the component's real behavior.

**Re-themed both remaining variants to real local tokens, changing the whole color direction from Geist's own default (light gray background, dark text)**: bound the tooltip background + triangle fill to `background/surface/inverse`, and the label text to `content/inverse` — this pairing is a real, already-established semantic pair in this system (used correctly elsewhere as the "content readable on an inverse surface" combination) and, being a real semantic pairing rather than a hardcoded dark color, correctly flips per Figma mode (dark tooltip in light mode, light tooltip in dark mode) rather than baking in one fixed appearance — consistent with this system's core "one token, two mode values" rule. Corner radius (was a raw, unbound `6`, matching neither `border/radius/sm` (4) nor `/md` (8) exactly) bound to `border/radius/sm`. Padding (raw `12`/`8`, which happened to already match real token values) bound to `spacing/padding/300`/`spacing/padding/200`. Typography switched from Geist 13px/16px-absolute to the real local `Default/Tight/Desktop/200` style (Haffer Regular, 12px, 139.9% line-height, 0 letter-spacing) — a deliberate, acknowledged 1px size change rather than inventing an off-scale token to preserve Geist's own arbitrary 13px choice.

**Cleanup**: renamed the two surviving variants (`Tip=true`/`Tip=false`) now that their other properties are fixed to one value each, and resized the component set's own outer frame from a leftover `4006×562` (sized for the original 240-variant grid) down to a compact `102×144` fitted to just the two remaining variants.

Verified via `screenshot()` at 3x and 6x scale: both tooltips render as a proper dark chip with white text and correctly rounded corners; the `tip=true` variant's triangle matches the body fill exactly with no seam.

### `Tooltip` shipped to code — the first component built on Radix (2026-09-05)

Wraps `@radix-ui/react-tooltip` (`Provider`/`Root`/`Trigger`/`Portal`/`Content`/`Arrow`) with our own CSS Module styling matching the re-themed Figma component exactly: `background/surface/inverse` + `content/inverse` for color, `border/radius/sm`, `spacing/padding/300`/`200`, and the real `Default/Tight/Desktop/200` type style. `showArrow` maps directly to Figma's `tip` variant. `side`/`sideOffset` are exposed as props — this is the concrete payoff of dropping the `position`/`boxAlign`/`center` axes from Figma: Radix computes placement and collision-avoidance at runtime instead of needing a static variant per combination. A short fade+scale animation drives off Radix's own `data-state` attribute (`delayed-open`/`instant-open`/`closed`), respecting `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**A real bug caught via the test suite, not the component itself**: `StructureCheck`'s `play` function used `canvas.getByText(...)` to find the open tooltip's content and failed — Radix renders `Tooltip.Content` into a portal attached to `document.body`, entirely outside the story's own canvas root that `canvas`-scoped queries search within. Confirmed via the failure's own DOM dump: `aria-describedby` and `data-state="instant-open"` were both correctly present on the trigger button, proving the tooltip really had opened — the test just couldn't see it because it was querying the wrong subtree. Fixed by wrapping `canvasElement.ownerDocument.body` in Testing Library's `within()` instead of using the canvas-scoped helper — a real, generalizable lesson for testing **any** future Radix-portaled component (Dialog, Popover, DropdownMenu will all have the exact same portal-scoping issue).

**Also hit while manually verifying in the browser**: repeated coordinate-based hover checks on the same resting mouse position intermittently failed to re-trigger Radix's hover-open behavior — resolved by moving the pointer away first, then back onto the trigger, matching a real mouse's actual travel path rather than jumping directly onto an already-rested position. Not a component bug — a browser-automation quirk in how synthetic hover reads register with Radix's real pointer-event-based open logic.

Verified: 39/39 test files, 196/196 tests passing, clean typecheck. Confirmed live in Storybook: correct dark background/white text via real computed styles (not just a screenshot, which under-represented the actual dark contrast at small scale), `data-side` correctly reflects each of the four `Sides` story's triggers independently, and `aria-describedby` on the trigger correctly points at the rendered tooltip's own id while open.

### `Combobox` — a pasted "Dropdown" turned out to be a searchable combobox, scoped and rebuilt for real cross-product use (2026-09-05)

User pasted a "Dropdown" component and flagged three concerns up front: they didn't know if it was a standard pattern, and it needed to scale across genuinely different use cases in the product. Audited before building anything.

**What the paste actually was**: not a generic dropdown at all — a searchable combobox (input-like trigger + search box + a scrollable list of favicon+label rows), built for one specific use case. Its internal "Menu item" rows were an **orphaned duplicate component** (`mainComponent.parent: null`), the same class of bug as the Table's duplicate Tag from an earlier session. Recommended treating "dropdown" as a family of genuinely different patterns (Menu for actions, Select for a static list, Combobox for searchable) rather than one component doing all three — conflating them is exactly what makes something hard to scale.

**Decision, made explicitly with the user**: rebuild fresh rather than preserve the pasted layout, backed by `@radix-ui/react-tooltip`'s sibling primitive family — `@radix-ui/react-popover` for positioning and **`cmdk`** for the type-ahead filtering logic (the actual industry-standard pairing behind shadcn/ui's own Combobox, and the standard building block behind most modern AI-product mention-pickers/command palettes). Both ship unstyled, so this is a code-side interaction upgrade only — the entire visual design below is still built from our own tokens.

**Real, cross-product requirements gathered before building**: must clearly support both typing (free-text, live-filtering — needed for a font picker elsewhere in the product) and click-to-select (a static list, no typing); needs a leading **color swatch** slot for color-selection dropdowns, including a custom-color row with a **trailing edit icon** that opens a (separately-scoped, not-yet-built) color picker; needs to support plain text, icon+label, image/favicon+label, and async/remote-loaded lists. A nested/hover-to-open submenu pattern was flagged as a possible future need, not built now — worth knowing `@radix-ui/react-dropdown-menu` (already installed) already ships native `Sub`/`SubTrigger`/`SubContent` primitives for exactly that, so it likely won't need new component engineering when the need actually arrives.

**Key finding: `Menu Item` (existing, already-shipped) can serve as the single universal option row for every required content type** — no new row component needed. Verified directly rather than assumed:
- Plain text / icon+label: already supported via its existing `Leading Icon`/`Trailing Icon` `INSTANCE_SWAP` properties.
- **Color swatch**: built a new `Color Swatch` atom (a plain circle, `border/radius/xl`, `border/default` stroke, fill overridden per instance) and confirmed it instance-swaps cleanly into `Menu Item`'s existing Leading Icon slot at the correct 20×20 size.
- **Image/favicon**: swapped the real `Avatar` component into the same slot — this surfaced a **genuine standalone bug in `Avatar` itself**, unrelated to the swap: its root frame had `clipsContent: false`, so at the smaller 20×20 size its internal 30×30 image rectangle spilled visibly past its own bounds. Confirmed via node inspection (reported instance size 20×20, but `clipsContent: false` on that same instance), not just eyeballing the screenshot. Fixed by setting `clipsContent: true` on `Avatar`'s root — benefits every existing use of `Avatar`, not just this one.

**A second real, standalone bug found in `Input`, unrelated to the combobox work itself**: its Leading/Trailing Icon vectors had `visible: false` baked into the master (the same "omitted from fresh instances" Plugin API quirk fixed multiple times earlier this session — Card Header, Ranked List Row, Light/Dark Toggle), which threw `"The node with id ... does not exist"` errors whenever a fresh instance's icon-visibility boolean was toggled. Fixed the visibility across all 72 `Input` variants. Separately, the specific instance built for this task kept hitting Figma's implicit-instance-detach behavior on any further child mutation (a deeper instability, possibly tied to a discovered duplicate-naming bug where `Input`'s trailing icon vector was actually a second copy of the leading "info" icon, not a caret) — rather than keep fighting an increasingly fragile instance, followed the same precedent as Report Card/Table: built the trigger as a **plain frame styled identically to `Input`** (matching its real bound tokens — `border/interactive`, `border/radius/md`, `spacing/padding/300`/`200`, `spacing/gap/100`, `content/placeholder` text) with a real Phosphor `CaretDown` instance appended directly, sidestepping the fragile instance entirely.

**Final structure — `Combobox` component set, 3 states**: `Collapsed` (trigger only, `CaretDown`), `Expanded` (trigger + `Menu`-styled panel below it — reusing the real `Menu` container's exact tokens: `spacing/padding/100`, `spacing/gap/100`, `border/radius/md`, `background/surface/raised`, `border/default` — trigger's caret becomes `CaretUp`, confirmed via the user's own manual fix), and `Expanded With Search` (adds a dedicated search field row, using the real Phosphor `MagnifyingGlass`, above the option list — the pattern for a non-editable trigger that still needs search). A separate, non-variant reference frame documents the empty-state look (`content/secondary` "No results found", centered) since "empty" is a runtime content state in code, not a structural Figma variant. User also manually removed an unwanted background fill that had ended up on the outer `State=Collapsed`/`State=Expanded` component frames themselves — confirmed via fresh inspection rather than assumed.

Verified via `screenshot()` after every stage.

### `Combobox` shipped to code — Radix Popover + cmdk, with a real click-to-open bug caught only by testing with real mouse events (2026-09-07)

Built per the Figma structure above: `editable` prop switches between the type-ahead trigger (a real `<input>` via `cmdk`'s `Command.Input`, anchored with `Popover.Anchor`, opening `onFocus`/`onValueChange`) and the static button trigger (`Popover.Trigger`, its own dedicated search field inside the panel). `Command.Item`'s `leadingIcon`/`trailingIcon` slots accept any `ReactNode` — a Phosphor icon, the `swatch()` helper (a plain styled `<span>`), or a real `Avatar`/image — matching the "any content type via one universal row" decision made in Figma. `onTrailingIconClick` (used by the `ColorSwatches` story's "Custom color" row) calls `event.stopPropagation()` so clicking the trailing edit icon never also fires the row's own `onSelect`.

**Real bug #1 — dropdown panel truncated its own option labels**: found live in the `ColorSwatches` story, not by inspection. `Popover.Content` was pinned to `width: var(--radix-popover-trigger-width)`, and the non-editable trigger button has no minimum width of its own — so a short selected value ("Blue") produced a narrow trigger, and the panel (bound to that exact width) truncated every longer label ("Green", "Red", "Custom color") and even the panel's own "Search..." placeholder down to a few characters. Confirmed via `zoom()` before touching anything. Fixed three ways: `width` → `minWidth: 'var(--radix-popover-trigger-width)'` on `Popover.Content` (panel is now never *narrower* than the trigger, but can grow to fit its own content), a `min-width: 200px` added to the non-editable `.button` trigger so the closed state isn't itself cramped, and a `max-width: 400px` cap on `.content` so a pathologically long label can't grow the panel unbounded. Re-verified: full "Blue"/"Green"/"Red"/"Custom color" labels and the complete "Search..." placeholder all render.

**Real bug #2 — the editable trigger didn't open on a real mouse click, only on typing (found only because a genuine click was tested, not just a focus-state screenshot)**: `EditableTypeAhead`/`Loading`/`EmptyState` all opened correctly when verified via a synthetic `.focus()` call or by typing, but a real automated mouse click on the trigger left the panel visibly closed every time — confirmed via a `data-debug-open` attribute plus temporary `document.title` instrumentation on `onFocus`/`onOpenChange` that the open-state flow really was: `onFocus` → `setOpen(true)` → immediately followed by Radix calling `onOpenChange(false)` again, all within the same click. Root-caused by reading Radix's own installed source (`@radix-ui/react-popover/dist/index.js`): `PopoverContentNonModal`'s built-in "don't treat the trigger's own click as an outside interaction" exemption checks `context.triggerRef.current?.contains(target)` — and `triggerRef` is a ref that **only `Popover.Trigger` itself ever registers**. Since the editable variant intentionally uses `Popover.Anchor` (not `Trigger`, because open/close is driven by focus/typing rather than a click-toggle), `triggerRef` stays `null` forever, so Radix's own exemption never applies and the anchor's own opening click is always misclassified as "outside," closing what it just opened. This is a documented, generalizable gotcha for **any** future Radix Popover/Dialog built on `Anchor` instead of `Trigger`. Fixed with a local `anchorRef` plus an `onInteractOutside` guard on `Popover.Content` (`onInteractOutside` fires for both the pointerdown-outside and focus-outside cases per Radix's own `DismissableLayer` source, so one check covers both channels): if the interaction's target is inside the anchor, `event.preventDefault()` suppresses the dismiss; every other outside interaction still closes it normally. Re-verified with real clicks (not synthetic focus): the panel now opens and stays open on click for all three editable stories, still closes correctly on a genuine outside click, and type-ahead filtering still narrows results exactly as before.
