Verified across every story with real mouse interactions (not just screenshots): `PlainText`, `WithIcons`, `NonEditableWithSearch`, `EditableTypeAhead` (click-to-open, type-ahead filter, select-and-close all independently confirmed), `ColorSwatches` (swatches + full labels post-fix, custom-color row's trailing edit icon fires independently of row selection without closing the panel), `Loading`, `EmptyState`, `Disabled`. Clean typecheck; 205/205 tests passing across all 40 test files, including the Storybook-integrated `StructureCheck` play-function test (`vite.config.ts`'s `storybookTest` project).

### `Combobox` — four rounds of live usability feedback (2026-09-07)

**1. Focus ring appeared on every open, not just real keyboard focus.** Root cause was two-fold: (a) `.trigger:has(:focus-visible)` used a heavy double box-shadow ring — inconsistent with `Input`, which the trigger is otherwise styled to match exactly and which only ever does a subtle `border-color` swap (`--input-border-focus` → `var(--border-interactive-pressed)`) on focus, no ring at all; (b) the non-editable variant's panel search field used plain `.searchInput:focus` (not `:focus-visible`), so Radix's standard "auto-focus the first tabbable element in the panel on open" behavior painted the ring on *every* open regardless of input modality. Fixed both: `.trigger:has(:focus-visible)` and `.searchInput:focus-visible` now do the same subtle `border-color: var(--border-interactive-pressed)` swap Input uses, and the search field's rule only ever matches on real `:focus-visible` (keyboard-driven) focus. The editable/font-picker trigger's own click-to-focus ring is unavoidable and correct — real `<input>` elements are exempted from the browser's no-ring-on-click heuristic since they're expected to accept typing next — but it's now the same subtle border treatment as everywhere else in the system rather than a jarring ring.

**2. Reopening the editable (font-picker) trigger after a selection only showed the selected option, not the full list.** Cause: `handleSelect` sets `inputValue` to the selected label, and since `Command.Input`'s `value` prop is bound directly to cmdk's own filter state, cmdk kept filtering against that leftover text on every reopen. Fixed in `onFocus`: if the field currently displays exactly the selected option's label (i.e., the user hasn't started typing something new), clear `inputValue` before opening — the full list renders, and the placeholder shows until the user types again.

**3. The color-swatch combobox didn't show the selected swatch in its closed/collapsed state**, only the text label. Fixed: the non-editable trigger button now renders `selected?.leadingIcon` (reusing the same `.leadingIcon` class as the option rows) before the label, so a selected swatch/icon/avatar carries through into the collapsed trigger exactly as it appears in the list.

**4. No way to tell which option was currently selected while browsing the full (unfiltered) list.** This was really the general fix underlying #2 and #3 as well — added a small `content-secondary`-toned checkmark (Phosphor `Check`, `--size-icon-sm`) rendered after the label whenever `option.value === value`, in the single shared `listContent` renderer used by both editable and non-editable modes. Deliberately subtle (secondary-toned, not accent-colored) and positioned after the label but before any custom `trailingIcon` (e.g. the color picker's edit-pencil), so a row can be both "currently selected" and "has its own trailing action" without the two icons colliding.

Also answered two standing questions from the user rather than building anything speculative: sizing — holding off on a size prop until a real screen needs a denser trigger, since this system's own `Input` only ships one size and most references (Radix/shadcn/MUI) don't add a second size without a concrete driver; naming — "Combobox" is the correct WAI-ARIA/industry term specifically for the editable/type-to-filter variant, though it slightly overclaims for the non-editable, click-only use cases (more commonly called "Select"/"Dropdown" elsewhere) — one component intentionally covers both here.

Re-verified all fixes live with real clicks: `ColorSwatches` shows the blue swatch in the closed trigger and a checkmark next to "Blue" in the open panel with no ring anywhere; `PlainText` shows a checkmark on "Draft" after selecting it, no ring on open; `EditableTypeAhead` shows all 6 fonts (not just "Roboto") with a checkmark on "Roboto" after reopening post-selection. Clean typecheck; 205/205 tests still passing.

### `Modal` — first component built against the "close the component gap" roadmap (2026-09-08)

First real gap-closing pass after the earlier design-system completeness assessment (chart/app-shell explicitly deferred until real screen references arrive; Modal picked first since `@radix-ui/react-dialog` was already installed and unused).

**Figma-first, explicitly not from either Gist library.** Investigated three possible starting references before building: (1) `Dialog`/`Dialog Body` from the "Gist Design System" library — real structure (Card/Sheet variants, dismissible boolean, max-width-600 rule) but 100% generic `--sds-*` tokens, a beige `#f8f4eb` background, and a backwards white-on-white scrim (`rgba(255,255,255,0.8)` — the opposite of what a scrim should do); (2) `Modal/Large/Medium/Small` from the "Gist Answers Component Library" — genuinely used our real tokens (`button/background/primary`, `border/radius/md`) but carried Gist-Answers-specific branding (a logo header) and the user explicitly ruled out using anything from either Gist library for this build, preferring well-known industry patterns instead. Ended up building fresh, informed by the shadcn/ui-style Dialog structure (header/title/description/close, body, footer) that already maps directly onto the installed Radix primitive.

**Real per-variant limitation found in `Button/Icon`**: unlike `MenuItem`, it exposes no instance-swap property for its icon at all (only `Type`/`Size`/`State`/`Shape` — the icon is baked into each of its ~150 variant symbols). Confirmed by hitting `Cannot move node. New parent is an instance or is inside of an instance` when trying to swap it in place. Fixing the master properly would mean wiring an icon slot across every one of those variants — out of scope for building one modal — so the modal's own close button instance was detached and re-iconed directly (the same fallback used for `Input`'s fragile trigger earlier this session). Worth a dedicated pass on `Button/Icon` itself at some point.

**Built as a `Modal` component set with a `Size` variant** (Small 400px / Medium 560px / Large 720px), each: header (title + `Dialog.Close`-wrapped `IconButton`), optional description, body, and a `footer` slot — deliberately NOT owned Cancel/Confirm copy or callbacks, since not every use case needs exactly two actions (a `Danger` button for destructive confirms, a single "Got it," three actions — all compose the same way through the slot). Surface uses `background/surface/raised`, `border/radius/lg` (20px — a deliberate step up from every compact control's 8px, appropriately more prominent for a modal), and `--shadow-lg` (defined in `effects.css` since early in the session, unused anywhere until now).

**Title typography caught and fixed mid-build**: initially hardcoded `Haffer:Bold` at 24px instead of the real `Accent/Comfy/600` token (`font/family/accent` + `font/weight/Medium` + `font/size/lg`, weight 500 not 700) — user caught it directly ("i dont think we used bold for the header... use the typography tokens"). Fixed across all 3 size variants plus the in-context reference by binding the real local variables rather than hardcoding, matching how every other text node in this file is built.

**One genuine new token gap, resolved deliberately**: no scrim/overlay color existed anywhere in the token set (checked both the semantic variable collection and via `search_design_system` — nothing). Rather than force it through the primitive→semantic alias machinery for a single, largely mode-independent use, treated it the same way `effects.css` already documents its own shadow tokens: an intentional, literal, hand-maintained value (`rgba(10, 10, 12, 0.5)`) rather than a variable-bound token. Applied identically in Figma (a literal fill) and code (`Modal.module.css`'s `.overlay` class) so the two stay in sync by convention, the same way shadow values already do.

**Code**: `Modal.tsx` wraps `@radix-ui/react-dialog` directly (`Dialog.Root`/`Title`/`Description`/`Close`), reusing `IconButton` for the close button and the real `Button` component for whatever the consumer places in `footer`. Real props: `size`, `title` (required — Radix needs an accessible title), `description` (optional — when omitted, `aria-describedby={undefined}` is passed explicitly to suppress Radix's own "missing description" console warning, its documented pattern for this exact case, confirmed via the browser console showing zero Radix warnings either way), `dismissible` (show/hide the close button), `closeOnOutsideClick`/`closeOnEscape` (independently disable either dismissal path — for flows where losing in-progress input would be costly; verified live that disabling both truly blocks scrim-click and Escape while the footer's own action still closes it), and `footer` (the slot). Body scrolls independently via `overflow-y: auto` + `max-height: calc(100vh - padding*2)` while header/footer stay pinned — verified live with a 20-paragraph story by scrolling to paragraph 20 with both header and footer still visible throughout.

**Motion**: added `AnimatedDialogContent` (`src/internal/`), the Dialog equivalent of the already-established `AnimatedPopoverContent` — same `forceMount` + `AnimatePresence` reasoning, same avoidance of `asChild` + `motion.div` together (confirmed broken empirically for Popover; not re-tested for Dialog, avoided out of caution). The one real difference: the overlay's animated opacity has to live on a plain `motion.div` *child* of `Dialog.Overlay` (which itself only carries `position:fixed;inset:0` inline, no visual styling), rather than on `Dialog.Overlay` itself — Radix still needs the real Overlay element for its own outside-click detection, and putting the scrim's actual background color on the same element that's being opacity-animated is what makes the fade visible at all. Same fix immediately applied to `Combobox`'s two Popover.Content usages once confirmed working (per direct request) — both now animate through `AnimatedPopoverContent` instead of a bare `Popover.Content`.

**Verified live**: `Default` (scrim + shadow + real Cancel/Confirm buttons matching Figma almost exactly), close via X, `NonDismissible` (no X shown; scrim-click and Escape both correctly no-op; footer action still closes it), `LongContent` (independent body scroll, header/footer pinned), `WithoutDescription` (zero console warnings). One real test bug caught and fixed: `StructureCheck`'s footer button was labeled literal text "Close", colliding with the close IconButton's `aria-label="Close"` — both matched `getByRole('button', {name: 'Close'})` by accessible name; renamed to "Done". Two rounds of environmental (not code) test flakiness hit and resolved: a cold Vite dependency-optimizer bundle of the newly-used `@radix-ui/react-dialog` mid-run cascaded failures into unrelated files (`Avatar`, `Table`) on the first run; a stale parse-cache `SyntaxError` in untouched files (`Alert`, `Tooltip`) on a later run, contradicted by a clean `tsc --noEmit` — both resolved by simply re-running. Final clean state: 43/43 test files, 222/222 tests passing.

**Post-build cohesion audit (2026-09-08), prompted by "does this fit cohesively, all checks done?"** — surfaced two real issues neither the build nor the initial live verification had caught:

1. **Dark mode**: initial screenshot looked broken (near-invisible text, the trigger button bleeding through the surface) — turned out to be a mid-animation frame caught by screenshot timing, not a real bug. Re-checked after the transition settled: renders correctly, `background/surface/raised`'s real dark-mode value (`rgb(31 31 31)`, confirmed solid/opaque, not the transparency initially suspected) and `Button`'s own dark-mode inversion both work as expected. Worth remembering: always let Motion's transition settle (or check twice) before judging a screenshot as broken.

2. **Real bug: nested dropdowns clipped behind the modal.** Added a `WithNestedCombobox` story (a real, supported composition — e.g. an "assign owner" combobox inside a modal) specifically to test cross-component z-index stacking, since `Combobox`/`FilterDropdown`/`Tooltip` (z-index 50) and `Modal` (z-index 101) are separate portals appended to `document.body` and don't stack by DOM nesting alone. Confirmed empirically: the `Combobox` panel rendered clipped behind the modal's bottom edge. Fixed by raising `Combobox`, `FilterDropdown`, and `Tooltip` to z-index 200 — the reasoning: a dropdown or tooltip must always render above whatever triggered it, whether that's the page or an open modal, so transient overlays need to sit above structural ones like Modal, not below. Re-verified: the dropdown now fully clears the modal. This is the kind of cross-component interaction that only surfaces once two independently-built components are actually combined — worth deliberately testing that combination for any future overlay-shaped component, not just each one in isolation.

Also addressed in the same pass: `FilterDropdown`'s checkbox row felt oversized relative to its 14px compact text — turned out `CheckboxIndicator` (20×20, a solid bordered box) and the row's own leading icon (`size-icon-md`, also 20px) were actually already the same nominal size; the real mismatch was both of them against the row's deliberately-compact 14px text (chosen to match the trigger's own density, confirmed intentional — the single-select `MenuItem` rows keep that 14px override too, by design, not touched here). Fixed by scaling just the checkbox down (`transform: scale(0.8)`, landing exactly on the real `size-icon-sm` value, 16px) via a className passed from `FilterDropdown` alone — `CheckboxIndicator.tsx`/`.module.css` themselves were not touched, so its default 20×20 size stays correct everywhere else it's used (the standalone `Checkbox`). `transform: scale` was the only viable approach here since CSS Modules scope class names per-file — there's no way to reach into `CheckboxIndicator`'s own internal icon class from `FilterDropdown`'s stylesheet to resize it directly.

### `Combobox` — trigger-level Leading Icon + Trailing Action slots (2026-09-08)

Prompted by a real onboarding screen from a *different* Figma file (`GEO-Lite`) showing an "Enter your brand" input: a leading circular logo/avatar placeholder in the trigger itself, live type-ahead suggestions with real brand logos, and an inline "Continue" button built into the input bar (replacing the caret entirely, active as soon as there's typed input — not just a confirmed selection). Confirmed with the user this isn't a one-off — it's expected to recur across other setup/wizard steps — so it was built as a real, generalized addition to `Combobox` itself, not a local one-off composition. The `GEO-Lite` file was never touched or treated as a token source (explicitly flagged by the user as inaccurate on tokens) — only its structure/behavior was referenced, fully rebuilt with this system's own real tokens, matching how Tooltip and the original Dropdown reference were handled earlier.

**Figma**: added four new component properties to the real `Combobox` set (`Leading Icon` instance-swap + `Show Leading Icon` boolean, `Trailing Action` instance-swap + `Show Trailing Action` boolean, plus a `Show Trailing Icon` boolean that didn't exist before — the caret had no toggle at all, so hiding it for the new inline-action case wasn't previously possible). Wired identically across all three state variants (`Collapsed`, `Expanded`, `Expanded With Search`), each of which has its own separate `Trigger` frame, not a shared sub-component. Each `Trigger` was also converted from a plain absolutely-positioned frame to real horizontal auto-layout (matching the exact original padding/gap values) specifically so hiding/showing the new leading icon reflows the label text automatically, the same way it already does in code — a static frame would have left a dead gap or overlap depending on which slots were toggled.

**Bug hit while testing the new slots**: resizing a test instance to preview a wider trigger silently had no effect on the actual rendered width — the instance's own top-level wrapper had `primaryAxisSizingMode: 'AUTO'` (hug), so it kept re-hugging back to the *inner* `Trigger` frame's original fixed width no matter what was set. Fixed by setting the wrapper to `FIXED` sizing first, resizing it, then setting the inner `Trigger`'s `layoutSizingHorizontal` to `FILL` — worth remembering for any future test-instance resize that appears to silently no-op: check whether an ancestor is hugging, not just the node being resized.

**Code**: `triggerLeadingIcon` (shown until a value is confirmed — once selected, that option's own `leadingIcon` takes over automatically, same swap the non-editable trigger already did) and `trailingAction` (fully replaces the caret) added to `ComboboxProps`, applied identically to both the editable and non-editable trigger branches. A real, separate gap surfaced while wiring the demo story: the reference's Continue button activates on *any* typed text, before a suggestion is ever selected — but Combobox had no way to expose its internal typed value to a consumer at all (`onChange` only fires on a confirmed selection). Added `onInputChange?: (value: string) => void` (editable mode only, fires on every keystroke) specifically so a `trailingAction` can track raw input, separately from `onChange`'s confirmed-selection semantics.

**Verified live**: `BrandEntry` story — dashed placeholder avatar and inactive Continue on load; typing "Tre" shows the "Tread" suggestion with its own real logo and flips Continue to active/primary immediately (before any selection); selecting the suggestion swaps the trigger's placeholder for the real logo and keeps Continue active; clicking outside closes the panel correctly (an initially-alarming "still open" screenshot turned out to be a mid-transition frame, not a real bug — confirmed by re-screenshotting after the close settled). No regressions: the existing Collapsed/Expanded default states and a pre-existing standalone `Combobox` instance elsewhere in the file render pixel-identical to before, since every new slot defaults to hidden. Clean `tsc`; two unrelated files (`Cell`, `IconButton`) hit the same stale-parse-cache flakiness already documented for the Modal work — confirmed transient again via a clean re-run. Final state: 43/43 test files, 224/224 tests passing.

### `Combobox` — `openOnTypeOnly`, and a caught-and-reverted master mistake (2026-09-08)

Follow-up to the `BrandEntry` slots work above, prompted by the user tweaking the Figma trigger directly ("wasn't feeling right") and asking to increase the leading avatar to fill the trigger's height.

**A real mistake, caught and reverted**: read the request as "apply this to the shared `Combobox` master" and restructured the actual `Collapsed` state's `Trigger` (bumped to 56px tall, zero-padding outer row, new inner wrapper) — before finishing, the user caught it: "no! don't carry that on the master state pls." Reverted immediately, back to the exact original 42px flat structure, verified via screenshot against the other unaffected states. The user's own follow-up explained why the height had looked like 56px in the first place: their test instance's row was *hugging* to fit a taller `Trailing Action` button, not a deliberate fixed size — a one-off consequence of that instance's content, not something to bake into the shared component.

**The "fill to height" ask itself turned out to be architecturally blocked** without touching Combobox's own CSS: the trigger wraps whatever's passed into `triggerLeadingIcon` in its own `.leadingIcon` class, hard-fixed at `size-icon-md` (20px) with `flex: none`. The only workaround that needs zero shared-file changes is `transform: scale()` on the consumer's own content (same technique as `FilterDropdown`'s checkbox sizing, just scaling up) — but that visually spills past the wrapper's own reserved space rather than the row cleanly growing around it. Flagged as a hack rather than implemented; parked per the user's "ok anyway" rather than forced through.

**Real, generalized fix that did ship**: `openOnTypeOnly` (default `false`). Today, focusing an editable trigger always opens the panel — the right call for a browsing use case like the font picker (see it all before typing). But `BrandEntry` has nothing useful to browse before typing starts, so focus alone should stay closed. Made this an opt-in prop rather than changing the default, specifically so the font-picker's own existing "see everything on focus" behavior stays exactly as-is — confirmed via live re-test after the change (`EditableTypeAhead` still opens its full list on click, `BrandEntry` now stays closed until the first keystroke). `onValueChange` already unconditionally opened the panel on every keystroke, so "open once typing begins" needed no separate change beyond gating the `onFocus` branch.

**Verified live**: `BrandEntry` — click/focus alone leaves the panel closed (dashed placeholder + inactive Continue visible, no suggestions); first keystroke opens it with the matching suggestion. `EditableTypeAhead` — unchanged, opens on focus/click as before. Clean `tsc`, 43/43 test files, 224/224 tests passing.

### `FilterDropdown` — leading slot widened for `AvatarGroup`, plus a real `AvatarGroup` overlap-scaling bug found along the way (2026-09-08)

The user added a `Logos` boolean directly to the real Figma `FilterDropdown` component set (default `false`, wired to a frame's `visible`), showing a multi-brand-logo preview in the collapsed trigger for multi-select — the natural next step after `MultiSelect`'s single-icon `leadingIcon` from earlier. Asked whether this "just fits into the multi-select one you already have" — yes, in principle: `leadingIcon` is already a plain `ReactNode`, so an `AvatarGroup` slots into the exact same prop as a single icon, no new API needed.

**One real blocker, found and fixed**: `FilterDropdown`'s `.leadingIcon` wrapper was hard-fixed at `size-icon-sm` (16px) with `flex: none` — same category of issue hit with Combobox's own leading-icon wrapper earlier, except this one was safe to fix directly (far fewer existing consumers, much lower blast radius). Changed the wrapper to auto-width (content-driven), keeping height fixed for row alignment; gave the plain-icon case an explicit svg size instead of the old `width:100%` rule so single icons render identically to before regardless of the wrapper's new auto width.

**The Figma side turned out to already be correctly wired** — initial concern (text overlapping the logo frame) was based on a stale pre-layout metadata read; once the frame's `visible` was actually toggled true, Figma's real auto-layout recalculated correctly with a clean gap, no overlap. Genuine cleanup that *was* needed: the 4 placeholder logo items were a mix of empty "Daily Mail" frames and one with a random placeholder image rect — restyled all 4 identically to match `Avatar`'s own real blank-placeholder treatment (`border/default` stroke, `background/surface/inverse` fill) for actual Figma/code parity, since no real brand logo assets exist to show instead.

**A real, separate bug found while reconciling sizes**: Figma's logos were sized at 20px (the user's own deliberate choice, kept as source of truth rather than forcing code's arbitrary 16px), but comparing overlap ratios surfaced that `AvatarGroup`'s `-10px` margin is a *fixed* value in its CSS — correct-looking only at its own original `size=30` (a ~33% overlap), but a full 50% overlap at `size=20` (measured live: 10px gap on 20px avatars). Fixed properly at the component level (not special-cased for FilterDropdown) since every future consumer benefits: overlap is now `-size / 3`, computed in `AvatarGroup.tsx` and passed as an inline `style` on each `Avatar` (`Avatar` needed a new `style` prop for this, previously unsupported). Removed the now-dead `.item`/`.overflow` margin rule from `AvatarGroup.module.css` — and caught, while removing it, that doing so silently made `styles.item` resolve to `undefined` (CSS Modules only exports classes that appear somewhere in the stylesheet), leaving a dead `className={styles.item}` reference with no visual effect; removed that too rather than leave an inert prop.

**Verified live**: `FilterDropdown`'s `MultiSelectWithAvatarGroup` story — 4 overlapping placeholder avatars render at their full natural width with no clipping, correct ~33% overlap (13px spacing measured on 20px avatars, matching the new formula). Re-checked `AvatarGroup`'s own pre-existing stories (`AllVisible` at the default `size=30`, `Limited` with the `+N` overflow circle) for regressions — both still render and overlap correctly, confirming the `-size/3` formula reproduces the original `-10px` look exactly at `size=30`. Clean `tsc`, 43/43 test files, 225/225 tests passing (one flaky Playwright-internal `SyntaxError` on a single run, contradicted by a clean immediate re-run — environmental, not code, matching the same pattern seen during Modal's build).

### `Modal` — `preview` slot for images/scrollable previews (2026-09-08)

Real layout addition, confirmed upfront via three quick questions rather than guessed at, since it touches an already-shipped, well-tested component: (1) placement — between description and body, not replacing the body; (2) edge-to-edge, no padding, unlike everything else in the modal; (3) its own capped-height, independently-scrollable region, not sharing the body's scroll. All three confirmed as recommended before touching Figma or code.

**Figma**: added to all three `Modal` size variants (Small/Medium/Large). The real trick — bleeding a middle child edge-to-edge inside an otherwise-uniformly-padded auto-layout frame — doesn't have a native "ignore my parent's padding" toggle in Figma the way CSS's negative margin does. Solved with the standard two-piece technique: a real, invisible **spacer** frame in the normal auto-layout flow (reserves the vertical gap so `Body`/`Footer` shift down correctly) plus the actual **Preview** frame set to `layoutPositioning: 'ABSOLUTE'`, manually positioned at `x: -24` (past the surface's own 24px padding) with `width` equal to the full surface width and `clipsContent: true` (so it respects the surface's rounded corners rather than poking past them). Verified visually on all three sizes before touching code.

**Code**: new `preview?: ReactNode` prop, rendered between `description` and `body`. `.preview` bleeds via `margin: 0 calc(-1 * var(--spacing-padding-600))` (the exact negative of `.surface`'s own padding — the real CSS equivalent of the Figma spacer+absolute trick, far simpler since CSS negative margins do this natively) and gets its own `max-height: 240px` + `overflow-y: auto`, with `flex: none` so it doesn't compete with `.body`'s `flex: 1` for remaining space. Added `overflow: hidden` to `.surface` itself as a defensive measure — the bleeding preview's straight edges only risk crossing a rounded corner in an unusually short modal, but it's a one-line guard against that either way.

**Verified live**: `WithImagePreview` — a real image (its own natural height of 520px, far taller than the 240px cap) renders bled full-width, correctly clipped/scrollable, with the body's real text and the footer's real buttons both rendering beneath it exactly as expected. `WithScrollablePreview` — confirmed the independent-scroll requirement directly (not just visually): set the preview's own `scrollTop` to 200 via script and verified the footer's on-screen position didn't move at all, proving the two scroll regions are genuinely decoupled rather than sharing one.

One real test-environment gotcha hit and resolved along the way: the Storybook canvas iframe's own viewport was only 380px tall by default (addon panel + toolbar chrome eating the rest), making `.surface`'s `max-height: calc(100vh - ...)` resolve to a tiny value and squeeze `.body` down to 0 height — looked exactly like a real bug until re-checked after switching to Storybook's own full-screen canvas view (a real ~680px viewport), where body/footer rendered correctly. Worth remembering for any future Modal-sizing check: verify at a realistic viewport height, not whatever the embedded Storybook canvas happens to default to.

Clean `tsc`, 43/43 test files, 227/227 tests passing.

### Real bug: `border/interactive-disabled` was more prominent than the enabled border (2026-09-09)

Flagged by the user as "input doesn't look disabled enough... it is prominent than primary" — confirmed with real numbers rather than trusting the impression alone, since "looks off" claims in this project get verified against actual computed values (established precedent from the Tooltip work).

**Root cause**: `border/interactive-disabled` (used by both `Input` and `Checkbox`) was aliased to a "darken" scale primitive (`color/darken/400` — near-black at 40% alpha) in light mode, and a "content" scale primitive (`color/content/400` — white at 40% alpha) in dark mode. In both modes this resolved to a *more visible* border than the enabled state: light mode's disabled blended to `rgb(154,157,160)` against white vs. the enabled border's flat `rgb(214,218,227)` — objectively darker despite being the disabled state; dark mode's disabled sat at 40% white opacity vs. the enabled border's 10% — objectively brighter. Backwards in both themes, not just one.

**Fix**: re-aliased `border/interactive-disabled` in Figma to `color/neutral/25` (light — one step lighter than the enabled state's own `color/neutral/50`) and `color/content/100` (dark — one step lighter than the enabled state's own `color/content/200`), matching the same "one step more muted than enabled, in the same scale" logic in both themes. Updated `scripts/figma-tokens.json`'s resolved values to match and regenerated `tokens.css` via `build-tokens.mjs` — new values: light `rgb(238,243,251)` (vs. enabled `rgb(214,218,227)`), dark `rgba(255,255,255,0.05)` (vs. enabled `0.10`), correctly lighter than enabled in both directions now.

**Verified live**: computed `border-color` on both `Input`'s and `Checkbox`'s real disabled stories in Storybook — both resolve to the new `rgb(238,243,251)`, confirming the shared-token fix reached both consumers correctly. Clean `tsc`, 43/43 test files, 227/227 tests passing.

### `TabItem`/`TabToggle` — Accent → Default typography (2026-09-09)

User's instinct: `TabBar`'s labels felt too close to a button's own weight. Before touching anything, audited all three "tab-shaped" components together (`TabItem`/`TabBar`, `TabToggle`, `ProgressTab`) — all three consistently used Accent-family/Medium-weight labels (`TabToggle`'s base `.tab` class actually declared `font-family: var(--font-family-default)` directly, but its own `.small`/`.medium`/`.large` rules redeclared Accent afterward and won the cascade — so it rendered as Accent in practice despite the misleading base rule).

Recommended splitting by role rather than changing all three or just one: `TabBar`/`TabToggle` are both "switch between views" navigation — closer to plain nav labels than buttons, and their selected/unselected distinction is already carried entirely by color (`content-secondary` → `content-default`), never weight, so dropping to Default loses zero legibility. `ProgressTab` is a different job (step/progress indicator, more button-adjacent), so it stays on Accent. Confirmed with the user before touching anything.

**Figma**: `TabItem`'s three states (5527:2710/2712/2729) had real, directly-editable text nodes — swapped `fontName` to `{Haffer, Regular}` and `letterSpacing` from Accent's 2% to Default's 0% on each. `TabToggle` was more involved: its outer component set (local) wraps an internal `.TabItem` component that turned out to be **remote** (an external library import) — attempting to edit its master threw `Cannot write to internal and read-only node`, the same class of error hit earlier with `Dialog`/`Button/Icon`. Fixed via instance-level overrides instead: reached into each of the 3 local outer-state variants' own nested `.TabItem` instance and set the font directly on that instance's text (a write Figma permits even when the deeper master is remote, since the override lives on the local wrapping instance, not the remote definition).

**Code**: `TabItem.module.css`'s `.chip` and `TabToggle.module.css`'s `.small`/`.medium`/`.large` all swapped from `text-accent-tight-{300,400,500}` to the equivalent `text-default-tight-{300,400,500}` step (same size/line-height at each step, confirmed via `typography.css` — only family/weight/letter-spacing actually change). Also removed `TabToggle`'s now-fully-redundant `font-family: var(--font-family-default)` on the base `.tab` class, since the size rules declare the identical value explicitly and consistently now — no reason to leave the misleading duplicate declaration in place.

**Verified live**: both `TabBar` and `TabToggle` render at the lighter Default weight; `TabToggle`'s `Selected` story confirms the state distinction still reads clearly through background/border/color alone, matching what the CSS already implied before touching anything. Clean `tsc`, 43/43 test files, 227/227 tests passing. `ProgressTab` intentionally left untouched.

Separately flagged, not yet acted on: the file has 71 legacy Color Styles (`color/blue/25`, `content/default`, `background/action/primary`, etc.) alongside the real Variables system — none bound to variables (`boundVariables: {}` on every one), several directly shadowing semantic variable names. Never used by anything built this session. Likely safe to deprecate/remove, but that needs a real usage audit (any node still applying one as a literal style, not a variable) before touching them — not done yet.

### `FilterDropdown` — a new sibling to `Combobox`, for dense toolbar filters (2026-09-07)

User showed a competitor's dashboard toolbar (several filter pills — date range, model, topic — each hugging its own label tightly) and asked whether `Combobox` would work there as-is. It wouldn't: `Combobox`'s trigger carries a 200px min-width and Input-style padding (`spacing/padding/300`/`200`) specifically added earlier to fix a different cramped-trigger bug — dropped into a dense toolbar as-is it would look bulkier than every reference example. Then a second competitor screenshot showed a multi-select "All Models" filter with checkbox rows — a genuinely different interaction model (multiple values, panel stays open across selections, aggregate trigger label) from `Combobox`'s single-select/closes-on-select behavior.

**Recommendation given and confirmed**: don't extend `Combobox` with a `compact` size or a `multiple` mode — build a separate component, matching the real precedent (shadcn's own `Combobox` vs. `DataTableFacetedFilter` split, built from the same primitives but serving different jobs: a form-field value vs. a transient view filter). Also resolved a user pushback mid-discussion: the same option data (a font list, a color list) can legitimately back *both* a `Combobox` (setting a value) and a `FilterDropdown` (filtering a view) in different screens — the split is about UI role, not data type, so both share the same row-rendering conventions rather than diverging.

**Design built fresh in Figma** (`-> FilterDropdown` page, `gTMRwkqEA0X9yyLGx3TmaQ`) before any code, per this file's standing process. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that the compact trigger should use `border/radius/md` (matching `Combobox`, the rest of the system) rather than the competitor's fully-rounded pill shape — `border/radius/full` exists as a primitive but isn't used anywhere else in this system, and introducing it here would read as a new, unexplained shape language. Real components reused directly: `Menu Item` (header row, single-select rows), the real `Checkbox` atom (state-synced per row), `Menu Divider`. Built a new `Filter Checkbox Row` component (checkbox + instance-swappable `Leading Icon` + `Show Leading Icon` boolean + `Label` text property, `State` variant Default/Hover/Selected/Disabled) since no existing component combined a checkbox with an icon slot — verified the icon slot's real swappability by swapping it from the placeholder colored-circle to a completely different Phosphor icon on a fresh instance, not just eyeballing it.

**Bugs caught and fixed during the Figma build itself** (an audit pass, not just construction): (1) a `combineAsVariants` call reparented the literal master node that was ALSO being used inline as a demo row, silently deleting it from the panel — recreated as a proper instance instead of a raw master reference, a sharper case of the "master reparents on convert" gotcha than seen before. (2) A systematic tokenless-white-fill audit (prompted directly by the user: "why is there a tokenless white color sneaking in") found real bugs: `createAutoLayout`/`createFrame` calls I made defaulted to Figma's opaque white fill and were never explicitly cleared or bound — some needed a real token (`background/surface/raised` on the trigger, lost somewhere in a clone chain) and some needed the fill removed entirely (pure layout wrapper frames, and a stray fill on `Checkbox` instances introduced by my own clone/`swapComponent` calls, confirmed as self-introduced by checking the real `Checkbox` master's own `fills: []`). Also caught the checkbox rows' `cornerRadius: 0` with no binding at all — bound to `border/radius/sm` matching `Menu Item`'s own row convention, otherwise the Hover state's background wash would render as a hard-edged rectangle against the panel's rounded shape.

**Code build**: reuses real existing components directly rather than rebuilding — `Menu` (the panel shell), `MenuItem` (single-select rows, multi-select header row), `Divider`, `CheckboxIndicator` (the checkbox glyph). No `cmdk` — unlike `Combobox`, neither trigger mode has a type-ahead requirement, matching the Figma design exactly (plain list, no search field). `multiple` prop switches between single-select (`Popover.Trigger`, closes on select, `value`/`onChange`) and multi-select (checkbox rows, `values`/`onValuesChange`, panel stays open, aggregate trigger label via `getMultiSelectLabel` — "All X" when none/all selected, the option's own label when exactly one, "N selected" otherwise). The header row's own click toggles select-all/clear-all.

**Live usability fixes found only by testing, not by reading the code**: user flagged the trigger/row text should drop from 16px (`Default/Comfy/Desktop/400`, matching `Combobox`) to 14px (`Default/Comfy/Desktop/300`) — a real, already-established token step in this system (comment in `typography.css`: "Pagination Dropdown Medium label" — another compact-dropdown context already uses it), not one invented for this component. Applying it surfaced two follow-on bugs: (1) `MenuItem`'s own `.icon` class hardcodes 20px (`size-icon-md`) regardless of its own size prop — even in `MenuItem`'s existing small/medium/large variants, icons never scale with text size, confirming there's no system-wide font-size↔icon-size relationship to preserve, just a per-slot judgment call — fixed by sizing the `Check`/leading icons passed into `MenuItem` down to 16px via inline `style` (which wins over `MenuItem`'s own CSS rule; a `size` prop on the Phosphor icon would NOT have won, since CSS beats an SVG's own presentational size attributes). (2) The header/single-select rows (built from `MenuItem`, whose own size scale skips 14px entirely — only 12/16/18) still rendered at 16px next to the checkbox rows now at 14px — user chose a scoped override (`!important`, matching the exact precedent already established in `Input.module.css` for the same kind of same-specificity conflict) over leaving the mismatch as an intentional hierarchy cue.

Verified live for both modes: `SingleSelect` (compact trigger, plain list, checkmark on selected, select-and-close), `MultiSelect`/`MultiSelectPartial` (checkbox toggle without closing the panel, select-all/clear-all header toggle, aggregate label correctly reads "All Models" at 0-or-all selected, the single label at exactly one, "N selected" otherwise). Clean typecheck; suite green throughout (210/210 by the end of this component's work).

### `AnimatedPopoverContent` — shared open/close transitions for every Popover-based dropdown, using `motion` (2026-09-07)

User asked for smooth open/close transitions ("the interaction is too light, just instant") across every dropdown built this session, via the already-installed `motion` package (`motion/react`, the framer-motion successor — confirmed installed, not assumed). Built one shared internal helper (`src/internal/AnimatedPopoverContent.tsx`, not part of the public library surface) rather than duplicating the same `AnimatePresence`/`forceMount` boilerplate in both `Combobox` and `FilterDropdown` — centralizes the transition timing so every dropdown animates identically, and means `prefers-reduced-motion` (via `useReducedMotion()`) is only handled in one place.

**A real, non-obvious bug caught before shipping**: the first version wrapped `motion.div` via `Popover.Content asChild`. This left the panel permanently frozen at its `initial` style (`opacity: 0`, `scale: 0.96`) — confirmed two ways: `getComputedStyle` never changed across time-spaced reads, and `element.getAnimations()` returned zero, meaning Framer Motion never even attached a running animation to the DOM node. Root cause: Radix's `Slot` (what `asChild` uses) re-forwards refs in a way that breaks Framer Motion's own ref to the real DOM element — a documented incompatibility between Radix `asChild` and `motion.div` as its child. Fixed by rendering `motion.div` as a normal child of `Popover.Content` instead (one extra DOM level, no `asChild`) — confirmed via a series of isolated smoke-test stories (bare `AnimatePresence` + conditional mount, no `AnimatePresence` at all, no custom `transition` prop, an always-mounted element with a toggling `animate` target) that pinned the failure specifically to `asChild`, not `AnimatePresence`, not the transition config, and not the conditional-mount pattern itself.

**A real limitation of this session's own browser-automation tooling, surfaced during verification**: mid-transition, `getComputedStyle` and `element.getAnimations()` reads taken through the automated Browser pane repeatedly contradicted actual screenshots taken moments apart (a "frozen at initial" reading, while a screenshot taken around the same time showed the panel fully settled and visible) — consistent with animation-frame throttling specific to how that automation drives a backgrounded/non-real-focus browser tab, not a real defect. Escalated to the user directly rather than declaring victory on unreliable automated readings; user confirmed it looks smooth in their own real browser, and that's what shipped.

Wired into `FilterDropdown`'s single panel and both of `Combobox`'s `Popover.Content` usages (editable and non-editable), preserving each one's own existing props (`onOpenAutoFocus`, the `onInteractOutside` anchor-containment guard from the earlier click-to-open fix). Re-verified all previously-fixed `Combobox` behaviors still hold post-integration: real-click-opens-and-stays-open on the editable trigger, type-ahead filtering, select-and-close (the panel now visibly fades out over the transition rather than vanishing instantly — confirmed correct, not a regression, by checking again a moment later). Clean typecheck; 210/210 tests passing.

### `FilterToggle` — a pill-shell wrapper around the real `Toggle`, for toolbar rows mixing switches and filters (2026-09-07)

User spotted a third competitor toolbar element — a labeled toggle switch styled as a pill ("Include third-party media") sitting next to a filter dropdown — and asked whether `FilterDropdown` was flexible enough to cover it by just removing the chevron and swapping the leading icon for `Toggle`. It isn't: that reference doesn't open a panel at all, it flips a boolean immediately on click, while `FilterDropdown` is built around `Popover.Trigger` opening a panel — reusing it would leave a trigger that tries to open an empty popover on click instead of just toggling.

Concluded this didn't need a new stateful component — it's `Toggle` (already real, already accessible via its own `<label>`+real `<input>`) plus a text label, inside the same bordered-pill shell `FilterDropdown`'s trigger already uses (`border/radius/md`, `border-interactive`, compact `spacing/padding/100`/`200`, the same 14px `Default/Comfy/Desktop/300` type step). The pill's own CSS duplicates `FilterDropdown`'s trigger styling rather than sharing a class across the two component's CSS Modules — a deliberate call, since this is pure visual styling (not shared behavior like `AnimatedPopoverContent`), and the two files can't reach each other's local class names. Only the switch itself is a real click target, matching `Toggle`'s own accessible `<label>` — the pill's text is descriptive, not an additional hit area, avoiding an invalid nested-`<label>` structure. Caught one bug via a genuine console warning (not silent): a `checked` prop without `onChange` on the static `Checked` story — fixed by adding `readOnly`, matching the exact precedent already established in `Toggle.stories.tsx` for the same static-demo case. Verified live: renders and toggles correctly, matches the reference pill. Clean typecheck; 214/214 tests passing.

### `TabBar` — Default-weight labels, and a sliding underline via Motion's shared-layout animation (2026-09-09)

Two separate asks in one thread. First: whether `TabItem`'s label should move from Accent/medium to Default weight. Checked all three sibling "tab-shaped" components first rather than assume — `TabItem`, `TabToggle`, and `ProgressTab` all shared the same Accent/medium treatment, so this was a real cross-component consistency question, not an isolated tweak. Recommendation given and accepted: move `TabItem`/`TabBar` and `TabToggle` to Default weight (both are "switch between views" navigation, and the selected/unselected distinction is already carried entirely by color — `content-secondary` → `content-default` — not weight, so nothing functional depends on the heavier weight); leave `ProgressTab` on Accent, since it plays a genuinely different role (a step/progress indicator, more button-adjacent than a browsing tab). `TabItem.module.css` already reflected the Default-weight change by the time this was checked.

Also asked whether the *selected* tab specifically could keep the medium weight while others go Default. Flagged a real, concrete downside rather than just implementing it: `TabBar`'s tabs are auto-width (hug their own label, no fixed width), and medium-weight text measurably widens a label versus regular — so a per-selection weight change would make each tab's own box change width the moment it's selected, visibly shifting every tab after it in the row on every switch. Since color + the underline already communicate selection unambiguously, this wasn't worth the reflow risk; user agreed to skip it.

**Second ask: animate the tab switch itself** — today each `TabItem` independently toggles its own static `border-bottom-color` with no shared element, so switching tabs is an instant, un-animated jump. Replaced this with the same "sliding highlight, not rigid flip" principle already established for `LightDarkToggle`: `TabItem` now renders a `motion.div` (`layoutId="tab-underline"`) only when `selected`, so Motion detects the shared id moving to a new tab's DOM position on the next render and animates the transition automatically, rather than each tab drawing its own static border. `TabBar` wraps its children in a `LayoutGroup` (scoped via `useId()`) specifically so **multiple independent `TabBar`s on the same page don't cross-animate into each other's underline** — without it, every `TabBar` on a page would share one global `layoutId` and try to slide a single indicator between unrelated tab groups. `useReducedMotion` gates the transition duration to 0, matching `AnimatedPopoverContent`/`AnimatedDialogContent`'s existing convention.

**Verified live, with one real tooling limitation surfaced along the way**: confirmed exactly one `.underline` element exists in the DOM (not duplicated per-tab — the shared-element model is working structurally) and that its final position lands exactly on the newly-selected tab's own real bounding box after a click. Could not directly observe the *in-flight* sliding motion itself in this pass — the Browser pane was hidden during the test, which throttles `requestAnimationFrame`-driven progress (the same throttling behavior noted earlier this session for scroll interactions), so timed position samples during the transition came back frozen at the destination rather than showing intermediate frames. Structural correctness (one element, correct end position) is confirmed; the animation technique itself is the same one already proven working elsewhere in this codebase (`Combobox`/`FilterDropdown`/`Modal`'s open transitions, `LightDarkToggle`'s sliding highlight) — flagged to the user as worth a direct look to confirm the motion itself feels right, rather than overclaiming a visual check that the tooling couldn't actually perform. Clean `tsc`, 43/43 test files, 227/227 tests passing.

### `Alert` — icon and dismiss button top-align instead of center (2026-09-09)

Small, well-defined fix: `.alert`'s single `align-items: center` centered the *entire* row (icon, text block, close button) as one unit — fine for single-line content, but on a wrapped multi-line message it put the icon and dismiss button in the vertical middle of the whole block rather than level with the first line. Changed to `align-items: flex-start`, matching the equivalent fix in Figma (`counterAxisAlignItems` on the row frame, `CENTER` → `MIN`, applied to all 4 status variants — `Info`/`Warning`/`Error`/`Success` share one inner row structure so one fix covered all of them). Verified live on the `Dismissible` story (two-line body, a title, and a close button): icon and close button both now align with the first line ("Storage almost full") instead of floating at the vertical center of the two-line block. Clean `tsc`, 227/227 tests passing.

### `CheckboxOption` extracted as a standalone component (2026-09-10)

Prompted by a real question: if a future consumer wants "checkbox + logo + label" *without* the rest of `FilterDropdown`'s panel machinery, should that go on the base `Checkbox`, or stay ad hoc? Recommendation: neither — `Checkbox` is a real accessible form control (`<label>`+`<input>`), most of its uses never need a logo, and this pattern is really a *selectable list row* (closer kin to `MenuItem`) rather than a decorated form checkbox. Leaving it ad hoc risked the hard-won calibration (checkbox-vs-icon sizing, hover/disabled states, two-tier gap spacing) quietly drifting across screens. Extracted instead, matching how `MenuItem` already exists as `CheckboxOption`'s icon-row sibling for the non-checkbox case.

**Figma**: extended the real `Option/Checkbox` component (Checkboxes page) with `Leading Icon` (instance-swap, default the `Color Swatch` atom) + `Show Leading Icon` (boolean, default false) across all 6 state variants. Hit — and this time correctly diagnosed rather than just retried — the same instance-detach gotcha as before: setting a freshly-created child's `.visible` and its `componentPropertyReferences` in the *same* script silently dropped the wiring (`componentPropertyReferences` came back as `{}` on later inspection, despite no error). Fixed by re-doing the `componentPropertyReferences` assignment in an isolated follow-up call *after* confirming the child's `visible`/id were stable — worked immediately. Verified on fresh instances, not just the master: default correctly omits the icon from `.children` entirely (no gap left behind), toggling it on shows it correctly positioned between checkbox and label, confirmed on both `Default` and `Selected` states.

**Code**: new `src/components/CheckboxOption/` — `checked`, `indeterminate`, `disabled`, `label`, `leadingIcon`, `onCheckedChange`, reusing `CheckboxIndicator` internally. Real typography check corrected a wrong assumption from memory along the way: the label text is `Accent/Tight/Desktop/400` (Haffer Medium, 16px) — re-verified fresh via `get_variable_defs` rather than trusting an earlier recollection of "Default/Regular," which turned out to be wrong.

**The interesting part — preserving `FilterDropdown`'s existing compact calibration without polluting the shared component's API**: `FilterDropdown`'s rows were deliberately shrunk earlier (checkbox scaled to ~16px, text at 14px) to match its own dense trigger — a real, confirmed design decision, not something migrating to a shared component should silently regress. Rather than adding FilterDropdown-specific props to `CheckboxOption`, gave it a small set of CSS custom properties instead (`--checkbox-option-scale`, `--checkbox-option-font-size`, `--checkbox-option-line-height`, all with real-token fallbacks) — an escape hatch every other consumer can ignore entirely, while `FilterDropdown` passes them via its own `style` prop to reproduce its exact prior look. `FilterDropdown.tsx`'s inline row markup and its now-dead CSS (`.checkboxRow`, `.rowContent`, `.rowIcon`, `.rowLabel`, `.checkboxIndicatorCompact`) were removed in favor of the shared component.

**Verified live**: `CheckboxOption`'s own stories (`Default`, `WithLeadingIcon`, `InAList`) render at full Figma-matching size (20px checkbox, 16px text). `FilterDropdown`'s `MultiSelect` story re-checked after the migration — rows look pixel-identical to before (same compact sizing), confirming the CSS custom-property override reproduced the prior calibration exactly rather than silently reverting to the new component's own defaults. Clean `tsc`, 44/44 test files, 234/234 tests passing.

### `border/default` — beige cast fixed at the token level (2026-09-10)

Real, specific complaint: the borders on `Menu`/`Combobox`/`Table`/`TemplateCard`/`Divider`/etc. (17 components in all) read as beige, not grey. Traced it to the actual cause rather than eyeballing a fix: `border/default` (Light mode) aliased to `color/background/350` — rgb(226, 222, 208), genuinely warm/yellow-cast (R>G>B) — a background-scale primitive, not a neutral one. `border/interactive` (Input/Combobox's own trigger border, which never looked off) aliases to `color/neutral/50` — rgb(214, 218, 227), a properly cool blue-grey. Dark mode was already fine: both tokens already shared the same primitive (`color/content/200`) there — the mismatch was Light-mode only.

Fixed by redirecting `border/default`'s Light value to the same `color/neutral/50` primitive `border/interactive` already uses — one token-level change (Figma variable, then `figma-tokens.json` + regenerated `tokens.css`), no component code touched, since all 17 consumers reference the semantic token rather than a hardcoded value. Verified live across multiple unrelated components (`TemplateCard`, `Combobox`'s dropdown panel) — border now reads as a clean neutral grey in both, confirming the fix actually propagated through the shared token rather than needing a per-component patch. Clean `tsc`, 234/234 tests passing.

### Figma-only: caret/chevron strokes were unbound literal colors, invisible in dark mode (2026-09-10)

User-reported, Figma-only (confirmed: code/Storybook already rendered correctly) — the trailing caret on `FilterDropdown` (all 3 states) and `Combobox` (all 3 states, 6 vectors total) had its stroke set to a **literal, unbound color** (`rgb(52, 51, 48)`, `boundVariables: {}`), not a token — unlike the label text right next to it, which correctly uses `content/secondary`. A hardcoded dark charcoal reads fine against a light background by coincidence, but doesn't adapt at all in dark mode, where it sits nearly invisible against an equally dark background. Root cause: each caret icon has two vector children — a full-bounding-box vector (no paint, a hit-area) and the actual visible chevron path — the literal color lived on the second one specifically, in every instance checked.

Fixed by binding all 6 to `content/secondary`, matching the label text exactly. Confirmed the real dark-mode resolved value before calling it done (`color/content/700`, white at 80% opacity — same as the text) rather than assuming the token swap alone was sufficient; light mode is visually unchanged (same dark chevron against light backgrounds) since `content/secondary`'s own light-mode value is close to the original literal. No code changes — this was purely a Figma source-file binding gap, not something the shipped tokens/components inherited.

### `Button`/`IconButton` Secondary — no fill, lighter border (2026-09-10)

Two real complaints, both against a competitor reference (not asking to copy it, just flagging ours felt off by comparison): the Secondary type's grey background fill (`background-action-secondary-default`, a 5% wash) didn't need to be there at all — a true stroke/outline button, no fill — and its border (`border-strong`, near-black) felt too stark next to a softer reference.

Fixed in `button-types.module.css` (shared by both `Button` and `IconButton`, since they `composes` the same per-type tokens): `--button-bg` set to `transparent` (was the grey wash), with the same subtle `background-interactive-hover`/`-pressed` washes `Ghost` already uses for its own hover/pressed states — so it's flat at rest but still gives real feedback on interaction, not just a static outline. Border moved from `border-strong` to `border-interactive-strong` — a real, already-existing lighter token (10% opacity near-black, reads as a soft grey), not a new one invented for this; directly answers "do we have a lighter provision" with a yes. Removed the now-unnecessary `--button-fg-pressed` override too, since with a transparent/light background at every state, the text should just stay `content-default` throughout — it was only ever needed to contrast against the old darker pressed fill.

Fixed in Figma first, across all 15 Secondary `Button/Action` variants (3 sizes × 5 states — Default/Hover/Pressed/Inactive/Focus): removed fill entirely except Hover (bound to `background/interactive/hover`) and Pressed (bound to `background/interactive/pressed`), rebound every state's stroke to `border/interactive-strong`. Verified visually in Figma (Default: clean stroke-only, matching the reference exactly; Hover: correct subtle wash) before touching code. Verified live in Storybook after: `Button`'s `Secondary` story computed `background-color: rgba(0,0,0,0)` and `border-color: rgba(2,9,18,0.1)` — confirming both fixes landed exactly as intended, not just visually similar. Clean `tsc`, 234/234 tests passing.

### `SuggestionChip` — a new chip built from a rough Figma reference, not a polished one (2026-09-10)

User pasted a rough exploratory reference (from a Figma "possible use cases" board, node `5819-8419`) with two rough chip concepts and explicitly invited questions rather than a literal-preservation build. Asked three clarifying questions before touching anything: (1) what does clicking the chip actually do — turned out to be "try a sample brand," advancing the flow, with the avatar/brand swappable via a separate icon; (2) is a formal "selected" state needed — not yet, explicitly deferred, don't build proactively; (3) does the second pasted concept ("Choice chips," four `Button` instances styled Primary/Tertiary with a selection ring) need its own dedicated component, or is it fine staying as restyled `Button`s — user confirmed a dedicated component is wanted, since the treatment may diverge later and the selected-state ring is a real requirement, not just a style tweak on `Button`.

**Figma reference was a paste, not a design**: several of its properties (`strokeWeight: 1.2155...`, `paddingLeft: 9.724...`) were non-round numbers consistent with a leftover copy/paste scale transform, not intentional values. Rather than reverse-engineering the scale factor to preserve them, rebuilt fresh on the real token scale — `spacing/gap/200` (8px) between avatar+label group and the swap icon, `spacing/gap/100` (4px) between avatar and label, `spacing/padding/200`/`100` (8px/4px) for the outer padding, `border/radius/md` (8px), stroke bound to `border/interactive-strong` (the same lighter border token just adopted for `Button` Secondary), fill bound to `background/surface/raised`. Built from real existing atoms, not new assets: the real `Avatar` component (20px instance) and the real Phosphor `ArrowsCounterClockwise` icon (`Format=Stroke, Weight=Regular`, not the separately-wrapped "Gist Design System" library's non-standard `Repeat` icon that also came up in search). Converted the assembled frame into a real Figma component with a `Label` text property.

**Code**: new `src/components/SuggestionChip/` — `label`, `leading` (swappable `ReactNode`, matching `FilterDropdown`/`Combobox`'s own leading-slot convention), `onSelect` (whole-chip click), `onSwap` (swap-icon click, `stopPropagation`'d exactly like `Combobox`'s own trailing-icon-click pattern, so swapping never also fires select), `disabled`. Label text uses `text-default-tight-300` (14px/140%/regular) — matches the built Figma text exactly, not the heavier `text-accent-tight-*` family `Tag`/`CheckboxOption` use, since this label is plain informational text next to an avatar, not an accent-weight badge. Swap icon colored `content-secondary`, the same token the caret/chevron dark-mode fix just standardized on.

**Verified live**: `Default`, `Disabled`, `InAList` (three independently-cycling chips — confirmed clicking one chip's swap icon only swaps that chip's brand and never fires its own or a sibling's `onSelect`), and a `StructureCheck` interaction test (chip click fires `onSelect` only, swap click fires `onSwap` only) all render and pass. Checked both themes directly in Storybook, not just Light: dark mode correctly resolves the stroke to a light/subtle border and the surface to the dark raised fill, matching the same token-driven adaptation already confirmed for the Secondary button fix. Clean `tsc`; full suite passes in isolation (4/4) — a same-batch flake unrelated to this component intermittently fails 4 unrelated pre-existing story files (`PaginationPage`, `TabToggle`, `Tag`, and this one) with an identical "Unexpected token" parse error when run all together, confirmed pre-existing and not caused by this change.

**Follow-up same day**: both the avatar and the swap icon needed independent optional visibility, not just always-shown. Avatar was already optional (`leading?: ReactNode`, omit to hide — no code change needed), but the swap icon was unconditionally rendered — added `showSwap` (default true) to hide it entirely for a suggestion slot with no alternate available. Mirrored in Figma first: two new component-level `BOOLEAN` properties (`Show Avatar`, `Show Swap Icon`, both default true) bound to the Avatar and Swap Icon instances' own `visible`, matching `CheckboxOption`'s existing `Show Leading Icon` convention exactly — added the properties and bound `componentPropertyReferences` in two separate script calls (not one), since same-script property-add + visibility-bind on a freshly-touched node has previously dropped the wiring silently. Verified both toggles independently on real test instances (screenshotted, then removed) before touching code. Added `NoLeading`/`NoSwap` stories; verified both live in Storybook. Clean `tsc`, 45/45 test files, 240/240 tests passing.

### `ChoiceChip` — a dedicated single-select chip, not restyled `Button`s (2026-09-10)

The second half of the same pasted reference (`5819:8400`, "Choice chips") — four `Button/Action` instances styled Primary/Tertiary with a selected-state ring, not a real component. Earlier clarifying question flagged this might not need a new component at all, since it was just restyled `Button`s; user confirmed a dedicated one is wanted anyway, since the treatment may diverge later and the ring is a real, persistent requirement — not just a `Button` variant.

**Figma**: inspected the pasted instances fresh rather than trust the earlier read — the "selected" chip (`Brand`) was actually a *detached* `Button` frame, not a live instance, with its fill/stroke/shadow hand-tuned: fill bound to `button/background/primary` (resolves to `background/action/primary`), a 2px stroke bound to `color/background/200` (a literal background token, not a border one), and a `DROP_SHADOW` (spread 2, blur 0) bound to that same fill color — a "gap ring + colored ring" halo effect. Traced `border/focus` and confirmed it resolves to the exact same value as `background/action/primary` in both Light and Dark (literally the same primitive) — so rebuilt the ring using the codebase's own established double-ring technique (`RowMenuTrigger`'s `:focus-visible` box-shadow: 2px page-background gap + 4px accent) with `border/focus` for the outer ring, the semantically correct token, instead of reusing a background token as a stroke/shadow color the way the paste improvised.

Built a real `ChoiceChip` component set on the `-> Chip` page — one `State` variant property (`Default`/`Hover`/`Pressed`/`Selected`/`Disabled`), matching `Checkbox`'s own established flat-state-list convention rather than a full interaction × selection cross product. Shape matches `Button` Medium exactly (40px height, `spacing/padding/400` horizontal padding, `border/radius/md`) — traced the *canonical* `Button/Action` set (`4541:675`, the one already audited/fixed for the Secondary button work) for the real token IDs, since the pasted reference turned out to reference a second, non-canonical `Button` component elsewhere in the file with different (but same-value) variable IDs — worth knowing the file has this duplication, even though it didn't affect the outcome here. Selected fill/text use `background/action/primary-default` / `content/inverse` (same pairing `Button` Primary already uses); unselected states reuse the exact `quaternary` fill tokens `Button` Tertiary uses for Default/Hover/Pressed; Disabled uses `background/action/secondary-default` + `content/disabled`, mirroring `Button`'s own disabled convention for non-primary types. Added a shared `Label` text property across all 5 variants (added the property and bound each variant's text node in separate script calls, same known gotcha as `SuggestionChip`'s boolean properties). Verified all 5 states rendered correctly against a real light background (not the default transparent-canvas screenshot, which made the two 5%-opacity washes read as either invisible-on-white or near-solid-black depending on how the transparency composited — a rendering-tool artifact, not a real bug, confirmed by re-checking the actual bound fill/opacity values directly before re-screenshotting in a proper wrapper).

**Unrelated one-off fix caught along the way**: user separately flagged a `Test — Brand entry trigger` exploration instance on the Combobox page (`5668:353`) — its leading placeholder circle (a swapped-in `Color Swatch`, 20px) looked too small against the 56px-tall trigger row. Diagnosed why a direct resize silently no-op'd: the icon is a nested instance-swap child living inside a live instance of the real `Combobox` master, and Figma disallows structural mutation of an instance's internals beyond exposed overrides — confirmed by cloning the icon onto the page directly (resized fine standalone) versus in place (reverted every time), and by a second attempt to swap the resized clone back in, which threw an explicit `Cannot move node. New parent is an instance or is inside of an instance`. Since this was only a scratch test frame (not the shared master, not a shipped screen), detached just that one instance (`detachInstance()`) into a plain frame, then resized the now-freestanding icon to 32×32 — exactly filling the trigger's available content height (56px row − 24px padding = 32px, coincidentally already matching the row's own `Trailing Action` button height). The real `Combobox` component itself was never touched.

**Code**: new `src/components/ChoiceChip/` — `label`, `selected`, `onSelect`, `disabled`; `role="radio"`/`aria-checked`, leaving the actual single-select group behavior (only one selected at a time, `role="radiogroup"` wrapper) to the consumer, same division of labor as `Radio`/`RadioIndicator`. One real CSS-cascade bug caught before shipping: `.selected` was defined after `.chip:disabled` with equal specificity, so a disabled+selected chip would've silently shown the selected look instead of disabled — reordered so `:disabled` always wins (Figma has no combined variant for this combination, so "disabled wins" is the sensible default, not an arbitrary pick).

**Verified live**: `Default`/`Selected`/`Disabled`/`DisabledSelected` render correctly; `InAGroup` (4 chips, single-select, matches the reference's own Brand/Publisher/Creator/Agency options) — clicking any chip correctly moves the ring/fill to it and away from the previous selection; `StructureCheck` interaction test (initial `aria-checked` state, click updates it, previous selection's `aria-checked` flips back to false) passes. Checked dark mode directly: fill/text/ring all invert correctly (selected chip becomes a light fill with dark text and the same double-ring technique, unselected chips get the dark-mode quaternary wash) — token-driven, no dark-mode-specific code needed. Clean `tsc`, 46/46 test files, 246/246 tests passing.

### `Combobox` — BrandEntry story's leading circle was a real oval bug, plus a proper size escape hatch (2026-09-10)

Follow-up from the `Test — Brand entry trigger` Figma exploration earlier this session (the one where the placeholder circle got enlarged on a detached test instance): checking the equivalent real `BrandEntry` story in Storybook surfaced two separate, verifiable problems, not just a comparison against the one-off Figma test.

**A real rendering bug, confirmed via computed styles, not just eyeballing:** `placeholderAvatar()`'s span declared an explicit `width:20, height:20` plus a `1px dashed` border, with default `content-box` sizing — the border added 2px on top of the declared size (22×22 natural), and the parent `.leadingIcon` flex container (`width: var(--size-icon-md)`, `flex: none`, default row direction) shrank the child back to 20 on the main axis (width) via flexbox's default `flex-shrink: 1`, but never touched the cross axis (height) — leaving a genuine 20×22px oval. Fixed with `boxSizing: 'border-box'` so the border draws inside the declared box instead of adding to it.

**Then a real design question, not a bug:** should this story's icon actually be bigger, matching the Figma exploration's own "fill the row's content height" resize? Traced the Figma *master* component's own `Trigger` `itemSpacing` before assuming the test instance's spacing was canonical — it wasn't: the master uses 4px (`spacing/gap/100`, matching the code default exactly), while the one-off test instance had a local 8px override, made *after* that instance's icon had already been enlarged. So the takeaway wasn't "code has the wrong gap" — it was "a bigger icon needs a bigger gap next to it," confirmed only after the icon itself grew and the existing 4px suddenly read as visibly tight. Settled on 24×24 (not 32×32, tried first) with an 8px gap, both applied only to this one story via two new CSS custom-property escape hatches — `--combobox-leading-icon-size` and `--combobox-trigger-gap`, both defaulting to the real tokens (`size-icon-md`/`spacing-gap-100`) for every other Combobox consumer — the same convention `CheckboxOption` already established for exactly this kind of "one consumer needs to be bigger without changing the shared default" case. Confirmed the escape hatch stays properly scoped: `AnimatedPopoverContent` renders through a `Popover.Portal` to `document.body`, so the wrapping story `<div>`'s CSS variables don't leak into the dropdown list's own `leadingIcon` spans (verified live — the story's trigger icon measured 24×24 while the list's suggestion icons stayed at the normal ~20px).

Also fixed `brandLogo()` (the confirmed-brand-logo shown in the same slot after selection) the same way — it had the identical fixed-`width:20`-on-a-flex-child issue, just without a border, so it wasn't producing a visible oval, but it would've stayed a small fixed dot inside the now-bigger 24px slot instead of filling it. Both helpers now render at `width:'100%', height:'100%'` so they always fill whatever size their container resolves to. Synced the Figma test instance back down from 32×32 to the final 24×24 to keep it from drifting out of agreement with the shipped code. Clean `tsc`, 46/46 test files, 246/246 tests passing.

### `DateChip` — a third chip use case, and a thorough token audit that caught two real bugs before building (2026-09-10)

User pointed to another pasted Figma reference (`5852:14371`, "Days" — a Tue/Wed/**Thu (selected)**/Fri/Mon week strip) and asked what to do with it, having just built `SuggestionChip` and `ChoiceChip` the same day. It shares `ChoiceChip`'s exact single-select semantics (one active at a time, dark fill + ring when selected) but a completely different shape — a fixed-size cell with a day abbreviation stacked over a date number, not a single-line label. Recommended treating it as its own dedicated component rather than stretching `ChoiceChip`'s `label` to accept arbitrary two-line content, matching the same reasoning that already kept `SuggestionChip` and `ChoiceChip` themselves separate; user agreed.

**Asked for a thorough token audit before building anything** ("even radius tokens, paddings, etc.") — checked every bound variable on both the selected and unselected reference cells, not just colors, and found real, concrete problems:
- The selected cell's 2px stroke was a genuinely **unbound literal** (`rgb(244,242,234)`, no `boundVariables`) — traced its exact value to a real local variable, `color/background/200`, but chose *not* to bind to it: that primitive is from the same warm/cream-tinted family already flagged and fixed once this session (`border/default`'s beige-cast fix) rather than the cooler, already-established `background/surface/page` that `ChoiceChip`'s own selected ring correctly uses. Bound to `background/surface/page` + `border/focus` instead — the same double-ring pair `ChoiceChip` uses, for real cross-component consistency rather than reproducing a token family this session had already moved away from once.
- The selected cell's day-abbreviation text ("Thu") was bound to a **raw, mode-invariant primitive** (`color/content/800`, always white 90%, only one mode defined) instead of the semantic `content/inverse` the sibling date-number text ("6") correctly used. Since the selected fill itself inverts per theme (`background/action/primary-default`: near-black in Light, near-white in Dark), a mode-invariant white label would've gone invisible against the flipped background in Dark mode — the same class of bug as the earlier caret/chevron dark-mode fix this session, caught this time *before* shipping rather than after a user report.
- Padding (10px vertical) and the outer row gap (8px) were both **unbound**, and 10px isn't a real step on the spacing scale at all (4/8/12/16/20/24…) — rounded to `spacing/padding/300` (12px). The day label's 13px font size is similarly off-scale (steps run 10/12/14/16/18/24…) — rounded to `text/default/tight/200` (12px). The date number's 18px/Medium matched `text/accent/tight/500` exactly, except its own 2% letter-spacing didn't match that token's real (0) value — used the real token as-is rather than preserving the deviant spacing.
- Radius (`border/radius/md`, 8px) and the inner day-to-date gap (2px, matching `spacing/gap/050`) were the only values already correctly bound — confirmed, not assumed.

Built a real `DateChip` component set (`State` = Default/Hover/Pressed/Selected/Disabled, matching `ChoiceChip`'s own flat-state convention) on the `-> Chip` page, entirely on the corrected bindings above — no unbound literals, no off-scale values carried over from the pasted reference. Added shared `Day`/`Date` text properties across all 5 variants (same two-step add-then-bind pattern as every other chip built this session). Verified all 5 states against a real background before combining into variants, then verified the `Day`/`Date` properties on a live test instance (screenshotted, then removed).

**Code**: new `src/components/DateChip/` — `day`, `date`, `selected`, `onSelect`, `disabled`; `role="radio"`/`aria-checked`, same consumer-owns-the-group division of labor as `ChoiceChip`, same disabled-wins-the-cascade ordering (this time provably correct by specificity alone — `.chip:disabled .day` has one more selector than `.selected .day`, not just source-order dependent like `ChoiceChip`'s equivalent rule).

**Verified live**: `Default`/`Selected`/`Disabled`/`DisabledSelected` render correctly; `InAWeek` (5 chips matching the reference's own Tue 4/Wed 5/Thu 6/Fri 7/Mon 10) — clicking any chip moves the ring/fill correctly; `StructureCheck` interaction test passes (one real fix along the way: the accessible name for a two-`<span>` button renders as `"Wed 5"` with a space, not `"Wed5"` — confirmed via the actual test failure output rather than assumed). Checked dark mode directly and specifically confirmed the fix that mattered most: the selected chip's day label ("Thu") now correctly flips to dark text on the inverted light fill, exactly the contrast failure the unbound-primitive bug would have caused had it been carried over uncorrected. Clean `tsc`, 47/47 test files, 252/252 tests passing.

### `background/interactive/selected` — the same beige family, this time live in a shipped screen (2026-09-11)

User flagged a screenshot of a "selected" row (`gist-geo-demo`'s `src/geo-v2/TopicScreen.tsx`, the "Marathon shoes" topic picker) with a beige/cream selected background that "doesn't seem to go with our other elements" — no Figma link this time, just a direct visual complaint. Traced it to `--background-interactive-selected`, which that screen consumes directly as `SELECTED_BG`.

Checked the real Figma variable's binding chain rather than guessing from the rendered color alone: Light mode aliased to `color/background/200` — the exact same warm/cream-tinted primitive (`rgb(244,242,234)`) already identified and deliberately avoided yesterday while building `DateChip`'s selected-state ring, for the same reason (it's the same family as the `border/default` beige-cast bug fixed earlier this session).
