Dark mode, by contrast, aliased to `color/content/300` (white, 30% opacity) — fine, and structurally telling: `background/interactive/hover` and `background/interactive/pressed` both alias to a neutral ink wash (`color/darken/100` at 5%, `color/darken/200` at 10%) in Light mode with a matching opacity-paired white wash in Dark mode. `selected`'s Light-mode value broke that pattern; a `color/darken/300` primitive already exists (ink at 30% opacity) — the exact Light-mode counterpart to Dark's existing 30%.

**Fixed at the token source, then reverted on user call**: rebound `background/interactive/selected`'s Light mode from `color/background/200` to `color/darken/300` in Figma, restoring the same hover→pressed→selected opacity ladder (5%/10%/30%) already established in both modes; Dark mode untouched (it was already correct). Synced `tokens.css`, rebuilt `dist-lib`, verified live in `gist-geo-demo`'s `#v2-topicscreen` route in both themes — confirmed working (Light showed a cool ink wash instead of beige). Immediately after seeing it live, user said to keep the beige after all — reverted both Figma (`background/interactive/selected` Light rebound back to `color/background/200`) and `tokens.css` (back to `rgb(244 242 234)`), rebuilt `dist-lib` again, reverted live. Net effect: **no change** — `background/interactive/selected` is beige by deliberate choice, not an unfixed bug. Don't re-attempt this "fix" in a future session without the user raising it again.

### New token: `chart/status/warning` — filling a real gap the token guide itself predicts (2026-09-11)

A parallel agent session was recoloring a comparison bar chart's "losing" bars from `content/status-error` to a warning yellow (`color/warning/400`, `#FBBF24`) at the user's request, and correctly flagged the problem before shipping it: that's a primitive, and the token guide says bind semantic ~95% of the time. The reason they'd reached for the primitive was real, not laziness — `content/status-warning` resolves to `#B06D00` in Light (calibrated for text contrast on a white background), which is the right call for a label and the wrong one for a large chart fill; and the chart token family has no warning-status role at all — it stops at `chart/categorical/4` (`#F6C687`, soft peach). User asked directly: for an info-viz fill, can this primitive get a proper semantic home?

Checked the real `chart/*` family in Figma before adding anything (all live in the `semantic` collection, Light/Dark modes, each aliasing a `global`-collection primitive) and noticed a pattern worth following rather than guessing: `chart/categorical/3`, `/4`, `/5`, and `chart/single/bar` all alias the **same primitive in both Light and Dark** — i.e. several chart colors are deliberately mode-invariant because they're already bright/saturated enough to read on both a near-white and a near-black chart background. `#FBBF24` fits that same description (it's already `content/status-warning`'s own Dark-mode value, chosen there for exactly that brightness). So the new token follows the categorical-3/4/5 pattern rather than the categorical-1/2/6 pattern (which do vary by mode).

Added `chart/status/warning` to the `semantic` collection, both Light and Dark aliasing `color/warning/400` (`VariableID:33:557`, `#FBBF24`) — matching `background/status/warning`'s fully-segmented naming (not `content/status-warning`'s hyphenated form). `ALL_SCOPES`, matching every other `chart/*` sibling (an explicit exception to the usual "always scope narrowly" rule — here, matching the family's own established convention wins). Synced `tokens.css` (`--chart-status-warning: rgb(251 191 36)`, identical across all three light/dark blocks). Clean `tsc`, `dist-lib` rebuilt.

Not yet done: the parallel agent's own chart bars haven't been rebound from the raw primitive to this new token — that's a rebind on their file/nodes, not mine to make blind. The token is ready for that swap (`var(--chart-status-warning)` in code, `chart/status/warning` in Figma) whenever they or the user does it.

### `EmptyState` — a pasted reference bound to a foreign library, caught at the token level (2026-09-14)

User pointed to a proposed empty-state pattern already sitting on its own dedicated `-> Empty state` page (`5900:97`) and asked directly: "is this a good standard design for an empty state? run usual checks, make fixes, and add it to our system." The concept was sound and standard — icon, title, description, plus an optional CTA — but the full bound-variable audit (not just eyeballing the rendered colors) turned up something the earlier caret/chevron and `chart/status/warning` audits hadn't seen yet: **the border stroke, the padding, and the description-text color were all bound to a *remote* variable collection named `semantic`/`global` — a different, external community-kit library that happens to use the exact same collection names ours does.** `border/action/inactive`, `content/inactive`, and `size/6` all resolved to real-looking values, but none of them are our tokens; our own equivalents are `border-interactive-strong`, `content-disabled`, and `spacing-padding-400` respectively. This is a new variant of the "wrong library" trap this session keeps finding (previously: Gist-wrapped icon duplicates, `sds-color-*` leftovers) — this time it fooled a plain `boundVariables` check too, since only resolving the variable's own *collection* (and its `remote` flag) revealed it wasn't ours.

Also found: the hidden optional CTA button (`Button/Action`, real `Type=Secondary, Size=Small`) sat beside the icon/title/description column rather than below it — a leftover of the slot never having been laid out for real use, not a deliberate design. Rebuilt the whole thing as vertical stack instead, CTA below the description, which is the standard placement.

Rebuilt fresh on the `-> Empty state` page rather than patching the reference in place: real `border-interactive-strong` dashed stroke, real `spacing-padding-400` padding, real `spacing-gap-200` gap, real `content-default`/`content-disabled` text colors (`text-accent-tight-500` / `text-default-tight-300` — both matched the reference's own font-size/weight/line-height exactly, no rounding needed this time). Converted to a real component with `Title`/`Description` text properties and a `Show Action` boolean gating the nested Button instance's visibility. Verified with a real-content test instance ("No campaigns yet" / "Create your first campaign to see it here." / visible "Get started" button), screenshotted, then removed.

**One correction made, then reverted on user call**: initially swapped the icon from the reference's `Format=Stroke, Weight=Fill` to `Weight=Regular`, assuming it was a stray inconsistency against this session's established Phosphor convention (every other icon built this session uses Regular). User caught this immediately — "icon should use the filled type in phosphor for this not stroke" — the Fill weight was deliberate, not a bug. Swapped back to `Weight=Fill` in Figma and in the `FileDashed` usages in the stories. Lesson for next time: an icon *weight* choice isn't automatically a bug just because it diverges from this session's own precedent elsewhere — check before "fixing."

**Code**: new `src/components/EmptyState/` — `title` (required), `description`, `icon` (`ReactNode`, swappable — same convention as `MenuItem`/`FilterDropdown`'s leading-content slots, even though Figma's own property isn't a formal instance-swap slot), `action` (`ReactNode`, typically a `<Button>`; omitting it renders no action at all, mirroring `Show Action`'s boolean). Verified live in Storybook in both themes (dashed border, muted description, and the Fill-weight icon all read correctly in Dark). Clean `tsc`, 48/48 test files, 257/257 tests passing.

### Stale sync bug: `background/action/secondary-default` (Dark) was fixed in Figma, never synced to code (2026-09-15)

User reported a live-page bug: a disabled Primary button was invisible in Dark mode. Traced it by comparing the real Figma binding chain against `tokens.css` rather than guessing from the symptom alone — `background/action/secondary-default`'s Dark-mode value in Figma currently resolves to `background/surface/raised` → `color/background/450` (`rgb(31,31,31)`), but `tokens.css` still had a stale flat literal, `rgb(20,20,20)` — which happens to be exactly `background-surface-page`'s own Dark value. So the disabled button's background was pixel-identical to the page behind it: not a new bug, a previously-fixed-in-Figma one that was simply never synced to code (this file has no automated Figma→code sync; every token here gets hand-verified and copied, same as every component).

User separately asked, before I'd traced any of this, "secondary-default token updated. is that gonna be a prob?" — worth confirming rather than assuming: checked every consumer (`grep` across `src/`) before syncing. `background-action-secondary-default` also drives the disabled-state background for `DateChip`, `ChoiceChip`, and `TabItem`, all via the same "muted/disabled surface" role — meaning all three had the identical latent blend-into-page-in-Dark bug, just not yet reported. Syncing was a pure fix across all four consumers, no downside found.

Updated `tokens.css` (`rgb(20 20 20)` → `rgb(31 31 31)`, both Dark blocks). Verified live: `Components/Button — Disabled` in Dark mode now shows a Primary button clearly reading as a distinct raised surface instead of disappearing into the page. Clean `tsc`, `dist-lib` rebuilt, 48/48 test files, 257/257 tests passing.
